### Training-Free Multimodal Guidance for Video to Audio Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24550v1)
- **Authors**: Eleonora Grassucci, Giuliano Galadini, Giordano Cicchetti, Aurelio Uncini, Fabio Antonacci, Danilo Comminiello
- **Abstract**: Video-to-audio (V2A) generation aims to synthesize realistic and semantically aligned audio from silent videos, with potential applications in video editing, Foley sound design, and assistive multimedia. Although the excellent results, existing approaches either require costly joint training on large-scale paired datasets or rely on pairwise similarities that may fail to capture global multimodal coherence. In this work, we propose a novel training-free multimodal guidance mechanism for V2A diffusion that leverages the volume spanned by the modality embeddings to enforce unified alignment across video, audio, and text. The proposed multimodal diffusion guidance (MDG) provides a lightweight, plug-and-play control signal that can be applied on top of any pretrained audio diffusion model without retraining. Experiments on VGGSound and AudioCaps demonstrate that our MDG consistently improves perceptual quality and multimodal alignment compared to baselines, proving the effectiveness of a joint multimodal guidance for V2A.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel, training-free multimodal guidance mechanism (MDG) for video-to-audio (V2A) generation.  The key idea is to leverage the volume spanned by audio-video-text embeddings in a shared latent space as a control signal for a pretrained audio diffusion model. The hypothesis is that minimizing this volume during the denoising process encourages semantic consistency across modalities.  The method operates without requiring joint training or large paired datasets. Experiments on VGGSound and AudioCaps datasets demonstrate that MDG improves perceptual quality and multimodal alignment compared to existing approaches, particularly a pairwise similarity-based method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using the volume of multimodal embeddings as a guidance signal for diffusion models is novel in the context of V2A generation. The method's training-free nature is also a significant advantage, addressing the high computational cost and data requirements of joint training approaches. While latent alignment and multimodal learning are not new, the specific combination of volume minimization with diffusion models for V2A constitutes a contribution. The reliance on the Gram matrix and its determinant as a measure of multimodal consistency provides a geometrically intuitive approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key limitation in V2A generation: the need for costly joint training and large paired datasets. By providing a training-free alternative that still improves performance, the work has potential practical implications. It makes V2A generation more accessible and adaptable to new domains. The superior performance compared to pairwise-based alignment methods suggests that capturing the joint multimodal geometry provides a more robust and coherent guidance signal.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free Approach:**  A significant strength is the elimination of the need for large, labeled datasets and expensive joint training.
    *   **Geometric Intuition:** The use of the volume of embeddings as a measure of semantic consistency is conceptually appealing and well-justified.
    *   **Improved Performance:**  The experimental results clearly demonstrate improvements in both perceptual quality and multimodal alignment compared to established baselines, particularly Seeing&Hearing, which shares a similar training-free philosophy.
    *   **Modularity and Lightweightness:** The approach is modular and lightweight, making it easy to integrate with existing pretrained audio diffusion models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation of Different Embedding Spaces:** The paper relies on pre-trained GRAM embeddings. A more thorough investigation could have explored the sensitivity of the results to the choice of embedding space and different encoders for each modality. Does it work well with other multimodal embeddings, or is there some synergy between the loss function used to train the embeddings and the guidance process?
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** The gains, while consistent, might be viewed as incremental rather than revolutionary. The AudioCaps results, while demonstrating generalizability, still show relatively high FAD scores. Further improvements in perceptual quality are needed.
    *   **Lack of Subjective Evaluation:** The paper depends heavily on objective metrics.  Incorporating subjective listening tests would strengthen the evaluation.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research on multimodal generation by highlighting the importance of joint multimodal geometry and offering a practical training-free approach. It might inspire the development of new volume-based or other geometrically inspired guidance mechanisms. The method’s modularity could also encourage its adoption in other multimodal generation tasks.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and effective training-free multimodal guidance mechanism, the improvements, though statistically significant, are somewhat incremental, particularly on a more challenging out-of-domain data set such as AudioCaps. Further, while the method is modular, the success appears to rely on the specific embeddings used. Despite these weaknesses, the work is well-executed, addresses a relevant problem, and offers a promising approach to improving multimodal generation. The training-free characteristic alone is a major advantage.

### AdaThink-Med: Medical Adaptive Thinking with Uncertainty-Guided Length Calibration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24560v1)
- **Authors**: Shaohao Rui, Kaitao Chen, Weijie Ma, Xiaosong Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in inference time scaling with extended long chain-of thought have significantly improved the reasoning capabilities of both general and medical large language models (LLMs). However, these models tend to engage in lengthy reasoning processes regardless of the difficulty of the input question, leading to increased inference costs in real-world applications. Therefore, enabling adaptive thinking where models think less for simpler questions and think more for complex ones is critical for the effective use of medical LLMs in practice. Despite its importance, there is a lack of end-to-end approaches designed to enhance the adaptive thinking capabilities of medical LLMs while providing a comprehensive examination of the trade-off between performance and computational cost. To bridge this gap, we propose AdaThink-Med, the first end-to-end framework designed to enhance adaptive thinking ability in medical reasoning models with uncertainty-guided length calibration. AdaThink-Med first generates multiple candidate outputs for each question, evaluates the correctness and uncertainty of each candidate, and then estimates problem difficulty via an uncertainty-guided length calibration module. For outputs with low difficulty and correct answers, the framework penalizes longer reasoning paths; whereas for those with high difficulty and incorrect answers, it encourages extending the chain of thought to explore alternative solutions. On six public medical QA benchmarks, AdaThink-Med achieves up to 6.4x length reduction on average while retaining performance with only minimal degradation. Intriguingly, we observe that AdaThink-Med spontaneously develops two distinct reasoning modes, which we characterize as "non-thinking" and "thinking", demonstrating the model's ability to suppress redundant reasoning processes dynamically.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AdaThink-Med: Medical Adaptive Thinking with Uncertainty-Guided Length Calibration":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AdaThink-Med, a novel end-to-end framework designed to enhance adaptive thinking in medical reasoning models.  The core idea is to enable medical Large Language Models (LLMs) to adjust their reasoning length based on the perceived difficulty of a question. AdaThink-Med works by: 1) Generating multiple candidate outputs for each question. 2) Evaluating the correctness and uncertainty of each candidate. 3) Estimating problem difficulty using an uncertainty-guided length calibration module, penalizing longer reasoning for simple, correctly answered questions and encouraging longer reasoning for difficult, incorrectly answered ones.  Experiments on six medical question answering benchmarks demonstrate that AdaThink-Med achieves significant reductions in output length (up to 6.4x) while maintaining or minimally degrading performance. The framework also appears to spontaneously develop "thinking" and "non-thinking" modes of reasoning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its end-to-end approach to adaptive thinking in medical LLMs that explicitly considers *uncertainty* in estimating problem difficulty. This is a significant departure from previous methods that rely solely on correctness or human annotations for difficulty assessment, or that blindly penalize reasoning length. While length-based reward shaping has been explored previously, integrating uncertainty estimation *within* the training loop to guide length calibration is a valuable contribution. The observation of emergent "thinking" and "non-thinking" modes is also intriguing and potentially significant.

*   **Significance:** The increasing complexity and computational cost of LLMs pose a practical challenge for real-world deployment, especially in resource-constrained environments. Adaptive thinking, as demonstrated by AdaThink-Med, is crucial for making medical LLMs more efficient and practical. The paper offers a promising solution for balancing performance and computational cost, making it highly relevant to the medical AI community. The dataset selection strategy, although not entirely new, also reinforces the practicality of efficient resource allocation with adaptive models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The end-to-end framework and the inclusion of uncertainty estimation are significant strengths.

    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments across six diverse medical QA benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of AdaThink-Med across various difficulty levels.

    *   **Practical Impact:** The observed reduction in output length directly translates to reduced inference costs, enhancing the practicality of medical LLMs.

    *   **Emergent Behavior:** The discovery of spontaneous "thinking" and "non-thinking" modes adds an interesting dimension to understanding how the model learns and adapts.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Uncertainty Estimation:**  The reliance on entropy as a measure of uncertainty, while common, may not fully capture the complexities of model confidence. More robust uncertainty quantification methods could potentially improve the framework's performance and robustness.

    *   **Dataset Sensitivity:** While the paper addresses this, it's worth noting that medical datasets are notoriously prone to biases and potential shortcuts. While testing across 6 datasets mitigates this it's still a valid concern.

    *   **Limited Analysis of Reasoning Modes:** While the paper mentions the emergence of distinct reasoning modes, a more in-depth analysis of the characteristics and triggers for each mode would be valuable.  What types of questions elicit each mode? What are the key differences in the reasoning processes?

    *   **Ablation study parameters are only partially ablated:** The ablation study (Table 3) only varies τ and α. One should also vary parameters regarding staged adaptive training, K (the number of top-K tokens), and EMA smoothing. Additionally, there are many parameters of GRPO which are not varied, which could affect performance.

*   **Potential Influence:**  AdaThink-Med has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and adaptable medical AI systems. The framework's core principles could be extended to other domains and incorporated into different LLM architectures. The discovery of emergent reasoning modes could spur further research into understanding and controlling LLM behavior.

**Justification for Score:**

AdaThink-Med represents a valuable and novel contribution to the field of medical LLMs. It effectively addresses a critical challenge – the increasing computational cost of large models – through an innovative end-to-end adaptive thinking framework. While there is room for improvement, the empirical results and the demonstration of emergent reasoning modes are promising. It improves on current methods by introducing entropy measures that can be tuned with hyper parameters for adaptive training which improves state of the art benchmarks.

### NeMo: Needle in a Montage for Video-Language Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24563v1)
- **Authors**: Zi-Yuan Hu, Shuo Liang, Duo Zheng, Yanyang Li, Yeyao Tao, Shijia Huang, Wei Feng, Jia Qin, Jianguang Yu, Jing Huang, Meng Fang, Yin Li, Liwei Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in video large language models (VideoLLMs) call for new evaluation protocols and benchmarks for complex temporal reasoning in video-language understanding. Inspired by the needle in a haystack test widely used by LLMs, we introduce a novel task of Needle in a Montage (NeMo), designed to assess VideoLLMs' critical reasoning capabilities, including long-context recall and temporal grounding. To generate video question answering data for our task, we develop a scalable automated data generation pipeline that facilitates high-quality data synthesis. Built upon the proposed pipeline, we present NeMoBench, a video-language benchmark centered on our task. Specifically, our full set of NeMoBench features 31,378 automatically generated question-answer (QA) pairs from 13,486 videos with various durations ranging from seconds to hours. Experiments demonstrate that our pipeline can reliably and automatically generate high-quality evaluation data, enabling NeMoBench to be continuously updated with the latest videos. We evaluate 20 state-of-the-art models on our benchmark, providing extensive results and key insights into their capabilities and limitations. Our project page is available at: https://lavi-lab.github.io/NeMoBench.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "NeMo: Needle in a Montage for Video-Language Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NeMo, a new benchmark for evaluating the long-context reasoning capabilities of Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs). NeMo uses a novel "needle in a montage" task where models must identify the temporal locations of specific video clips (needles) embedded within longer, synthesized videos (montages) constructed from loosely related short video segments.  The authors create NeMoBench, a large, automatically generated dataset of question-answer pairs based on authorized TV programs. They evaluate 20 state-of-the-art VideoLLMs on this benchmark, revealing the limitations of current models, particularly in long-context recall and temporal grounding. The paper also details the automated data generation pipeline and analyzes its efficiency and data quality.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The "needle in a montage" task is a clever adaptation of the "needle in a haystack" concept from NLP to the video domain. A key improvement is that the "needles" are semantically relevant to the overall video context, making the task more challenging and reflective of real-world scenarios than previous approaches that used unrelated static images or clips. The emphasis on temporal grounding is also a valuable contribution. The automated data generation pipeline offers a practical solution to the scalability problem of video benchmark creation, making use of video foundation models, though some elements (like Visual Prompts) have become quite standard. The specific application to long videos from Phoenix TV, an authorized source, is a unique and significant strength of the work.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in VideoLLM evaluation. Existing benchmarks often lack the complexity and temporal granularity needed to assess true video understanding capabilities. NeMoBench provides a more challenging and realistic testbed, driving the development of VideoLLMs that can perform complex reasoning over extended video sequences. The analysis of current models exposes clear performance gaps, especially between open-source and closed-source models, and highlights areas for future research (long-context handling, temporal reasoning, efficient video representation). The use of TV programs provides a stream of new video content and addresses data contamination issues.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The NeMo task is well-designed and challenging, with clear relevance to real-world applications.
    *   The automated data generation pipeline is a significant contribution, enabling scalable benchmark creation.
    *   The dataset is large, diverse, and derived from a reliable source (Phoenix TV).
    *   The extensive evaluation of 20 models provides valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses.
    *   Thorough analysis of the benchmark construction and model performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the automated data generation is a strength, the reliance on prompting multimodal models to generate questions and verify answers introduces potential biases or inaccuracies. Although manual cleaning is done on a subset, the reliance on the pipeline means a systematic source of error may be present.
    *   The prompt engineering for specific models (as discussed in the paper) highlights the sensitivity of VideoLLMs to input format, suggesting that model performance is not always a direct reflection of their underlying understanding.
    *   The dataset is still limited to certain video types (TV programs). Expanding to other domains (e.g., egocentric video, scientific data) could further enhance the benchmark's generalizability.
    *   The study could benefit from ablation studies that analyze the impact of various design choices on the difficulty of the task (e.g., the number and placement of needles, the length and complexity of the montages).

*   **Potential Influence:** NeMo has the potential to become a widely adopted benchmark for VideoLLM evaluation, driving progress in long-context reasoning and temporal understanding. The automated data generation pipeline could inspire new approaches to benchmark creation in other modalities. The analysis of model capabilities will inform future research directions and guide the development of more powerful and robust VideoLLMs.

*   **Score Justification:**
    The paper presents a novel task, a scalable benchmark, and valuable insights into the capabilities of VideoLLMs. The main strength is the semantically relevant needles, but there still remains a source of systemic error due to the automated pipeline's reliance on prompting multimodal models and there may be a need to extend the dataset to various other domains. Therefore,

### U-DiT Policy: U-shaped Diffusion Transformers for Robotic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24579v1)
- **Authors**: Linzhi Wu, Aoran Mei, Xiyue Wang, Guo-Niu Zhu, Zhongxue Gan
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based methods have been acknowledged as a powerful paradigm for end-to-end visuomotor control in robotics. Most existing approaches adopt a Diffusion Policy in U-Net architecture (DP-U), which, while effective, suffers from limited global context modeling and over-smoothing artifacts. To address these issues, we propose U-DiT Policy, a novel U-shaped Diffusion Transformer framework. U-DiT preserves the multi-scale feature fusion advantages of U-Net while integrating the global context modeling capability of Transformers, thereby enhancing representational power and policy expressiveness. We evaluate U-DiT extensively across both simulation and real-world robotic manipulation tasks. In simulation, U-DiT achieves an average performance gain of 10\% over baseline methods and surpasses Transformer-based diffusion policies (DP-T) that use AdaLN blocks by 6\% under comparable parameter budgets. On real-world robotic tasks, U-DiT demonstrates superior generalization and robustness, achieving an average improvement of 22.5\% over DP-U. In addition, robustness and generalization experiments under distractor and lighting variations further highlight the advantages of U-DiT. These results highlight the effectiveness and practical potential of U-DiT Policy as a new foundation for diffusion-based robotic manipulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "U-DiT Policy: U-shaped Diffusion Transformers for Robotic Manipulation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces U-DiT Policy, a novel U-shaped Diffusion Transformer framework for robotic manipulation. It aims to address the limitations of existing diffusion-based policies, specifically DP-U's limited global context modeling and DP-T's lack of multi-scale feature fusion. U-DiT embeds Transformers into the U-Net architecture, combining the strengths of both approaches. The framework is evaluated extensively in simulation and real-world robotic manipulation tasks, demonstrating improved performance, generalization, and robustness compared to baseline methods like DP-U and DiT-based policies. The key contributions include the novel U-DiT backbone with an asymmetric decoder, and an enhanced conditional modeling strategy using AdaLN.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining the U-Net architecture with Transformers within a diffusion policy framework, while inspired by image generation, is a worthwhile exploration in the context of robotic manipulation. The specific design choices, such as the asymmetric decoder to improve action representation and AdaLN for conditional modeling, contribute to the novelty. While U-shaped architectures for diffusion models exist, its application and adaptation for *robotic manipulation*, particularly in the way they address the limitations of prior diffusion-based robotics policies, represents a novel aspect.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant problem in robot learning: improving the performance and robustness of diffusion-based policies. Diffusion policies have shown promise, but their limitations, especially in long-horizon tasks and real-world scenarios, hinder their widespread adoption. The performance gains demonstrated by U-DiT in both simulation and real-world tasks suggest the potential for U-DiT to be a useful building block in the field. The empirical results are promising; however, it is a bit limited to manipulation tasks. The thorough evaluation, including ablation studies and experiments on generalization under visual variations, strengthens the significance of the results.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Novel Architecture:** The U-DiT framework provides a sensible and effective solution.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The evaluations demonstrate the advantages of U-DiT across various settings.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies isolate the contributions of different components.
    *   **Real-World Validation:** The experiments on real robots add significant value to the paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Tasks:** The evaluation is primarily focused on tabletop manipulation tasks. Generalization to more complex and dynamic environments is not fully explored.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the performance gains are consistent, they may not be considered groundbreaking.
    *   **Inference Latency:** The paper acknowledges the inference latency introduced by the diffusion process, which could limit real-time applications. This is not thoroughly investigated in the experiments.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** There is a lack of theoretical analysis to explain why the proposed architecture works better.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on diffusion-based policies for robotics. The U-DiT framework could serve as a new foundation for manipulation tasks. The work suggests that U-Net architectures, coupled with transformers, could be helpful in robotics and merits further exploration.

**Justification for Score:**

U-DiT provides a solid engineering contribution that addresses specific limitations within the growing field of diffusion-based robot learning. The adaptation of U-shaped architectures from image generation is a sensible and reasonably novel approach for improving both the global context awareness and feature fusion. Given that it is an architecture adaptation of current methods, the value of the paper comes from the strong experimental results and the real-world validation (albeit in a limited setting).

### SAIP: A Plug-and-Play Scale-adaptive Module in Diffusion-based Inverse Problems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24580v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyu Wang, Xiangming Meng
- **Abstract**: Solving inverse problems with diffusion models has shown promise in tasks such as image restoration. A common approach is to formulate the problem in a Bayesian framework and sample from the posterior by combining the prior score with the likelihood score. Since the likelihood term is often intractable, estimators like DPS, DMPS, and $\pi$GDM are widely adopted. However, these methods rely on a fixed, manually tuned scale to balance prior and likelihood contributions. Such a static design is suboptimal, as the ideal balance varies across timesteps and tasks, limiting performance and generalization. To address this issue, we propose SAIP, a plug-and-play module that adaptively refines the scale at each timestep without retraining or altering the diffusion backbone. SAIP integrates seamlessly into existing samplers and consistently improves reconstruction quality across diverse image restoration tasks, including challenging scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SAIP: A Plug-and-Play Scale-Adaptive Module in Diffusion-Based Inverse Problems."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a critical limitation in diffusion-based inverse problem solvers: the need for manual tuning of the scale factor (λ) that balances the prior (diffusion model) and likelihood terms. The authors propose SAIP (Scale-Adaptive Inversion Plug-in), a novel plug-and-play module that dynamically estimates and refines this scale during inference. SAIP integrates seamlessly with existing diffusion-based solvers without requiring retraining or modification of the core diffusion model. Through experiments on various image restoration tasks (denoising, deblurring, inpainting), the paper demonstrates that SAIP consistently improves reconstruction quality, even in challenging scenarios with severe degradation or high noise levels, exceeding existing baselines like DPS, DMPS, and πGDM. The core innovation lies in self-adaptively adjusting the scale factor for the likelihood, improving reconstruction quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of adaptively scaling the likelihood score in diffusion-based inverse problems is relatively novel. Existing methods rely on fixed, manually tuned scale factors. The SAIP module provides a mechanism to dynamically adjust this scale at each timestep, leading to more robust performance. The idea of the adaptive scale originates from research such as CFG-Zero* that adapt scaling factors, but extending this approach to inverse problems represents a non-trivial adaptation.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical benefits of automating the scaling process. Manual tuning is tedious, computationally expensive, and often suboptimal. SAIP offers a more efficient and robust approach, potentially broadening the applicability of diffusion models to a wider range of inverse problems, including scenarios where prior knowledge of the optimal scale is limited. The demonstrated improvements across various tasks (denoising, deblurring, inpainting) also suggest broad applicability of the module.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Plug-and-play design:** SAIP's key strength is its ease of integration. It can be added to existing diffusion-based solvers without requiring retraining or modifying the original diffusion model.
    *   **Consistent improvements:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate performance gains across diverse tasks and degradation levels, showing SAIP's robustness.
    *   **Theoretical foundation:** The paper provides a theoretical justification for SAIP, deriving a closed-form solution for the optimal scale factor based on minimizing the discrepancy between estimated and ground-truth posterior scores.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results consistently outperform baselines, the improvements in some scenarios, especially the latent domain and nonlinear transfer settings, can be relatively modest in the realm of such generative approaches. The visual differences aren't overwhelmingly significant, so the importance is muted.
    *   **Limited scope of the theoretical argument:** While the derivation of the optimal *s* is presented, its relation to a truly optimal bayesian estimator may be overstated, given that *s* is still dependent upon the underlying likelihood estimators (DPS, DMPS, GDM) rather than being directly derived from intractable posterior sampling. In other words, SAIP enhances the base-estimator but it is still limited by it.
    *   **Reliance on existing likelihood estimators:** SAIP improves the scaling of likelihood scores but still relies on the accuracy of these estimators (DPS, DMPS, πGDM). If the underlying estimators are poor, SAIP's performance will be limited.

*   **Potential Influence:** SAIP has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging further research into adaptive scaling techniques for diffusion-based models.
    *   Simplifying the application of diffusion models to inverse problems by reducing the need for manual tuning.
    *   Improving the robustness and generalization performance of diffusion-based inverse solvers.

*   **Justification of the Score:**

The paper offers a practical, easy-to-implement solution to a significant limitation in diffusion-based inverse problems. The demonstrated improvements across various tasks, the plug-and-play design, and the theoretical justification contribute to the paper's significance. However, the incremental nature of the improvements in certain scenarios, reliance on existing estimators, and theoretical framework limitations prevent the work from being truly transformative. The scope is also limited to image restoration and might not translate immediately to different modalities (eg. medical imaging) without specific tailoring.

### PoseDiff: A Unified Diffusion Model Bridging Robot Pose Estimation and Video-to-Action Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24591v1)
- **Authors**: Haozhuo Zhang, Michele Caprio, Jing Shao, Qiang Zhang, Jian Tang, Shanghang Zhang, Wei Pan
- **Abstract**: We present PoseDiff, a conditional diffusion model that unifies robot state estimation and control within a single framework. At its core, PoseDiff maps raw visual observations into structured robot states-such as 3D keypoints or joint angles-from a single RGB image, eliminating the need for multi-stage pipelines or auxiliary modalities. Building upon this foundation, PoseDiff extends naturally to video-to-action inverse dynamics: by conditioning on sparse video keyframes generated by world models, it produces smooth and continuous long-horizon action sequences through an overlap-averaging strategy. This unified design enables scalable and efficient integration of perception and control. On the DREAM dataset, PoseDiff achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and real-time performance for pose estimation. On Libero-Object manipulation tasks, it substantially improves success rates over existing inverse dynamics modules, even under strict offline settings. Together, these results show that PoseDiff provides a scalable, accurate, and efficient bridge between perception, planning, and control in embodied AI. The video visualization results can be found on the project page: https://haozhuo-zhang.github.io/PoseDiff-project-page/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the PoseDiff paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PoseDiff, a unified conditional diffusion model designed to bridge robot pose estimation and video-to-action control.  It operates by mapping raw visual observations (single RGB images) into structured robot states (3D keypoints or joint angles), eliminating the need for multi-stage pipelines or auxiliary modalities in pose estimation. Furthermore, PoseDiff extends to video-to-action inverse dynamics by conditioning on sparse video keyframes generated by world models, producing smooth action sequences via an overlap-averaging strategy. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art accuracy and real-time performance for pose estimation on the DREAM dataset and improved success rates on Libero-Object manipulation tasks compared to existing inverse dynamics modules.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a novel approach by unifying robot pose estimation and inverse dynamics within a single diffusion model framework. While diffusion models have been used in robotics before, the end-to-end integration of vision-based pose estimation and action generation is a significant advance.  The use of sparse keyframes to condition action generation is also interesting. However, each of the components are not necessarily completely novel.  Diffusion models are known, and conditional diffusion models are also established. The individual architecture choices (FiLM-modulated Conditional U-Net, DDPM denoising) are known components. The primary novelty is in the system-level integration and the specific application to robot pose and control.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is considerable. Pose estimation is a fundamental problem, and efficient, accurate solutions like PoseDiff can benefit numerous robotic applications. Furthermore, the ability to generate smooth and continuous action sequences from sparse video keyframes has significant implications for improving robot control and reducing reliance on complex and slow planning algorithms.  If PoseDiff can be scaled and generalized to more complex tasks, it could streamline the development of embodied AI systems. The improvement over prior methods, especially in the inverse dynamics task, highlights the promise of the integrated approach. However, the success relies on the quality of the world model and its generated keyframes; this dependence is a potential limitation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Unified Framework:**  The single diffusion model for both pose estimation and control is elegant and efficient.
    *   **State-of-the-Art Results:** Demonstrated superior performance in pose estimation and improved success in manipulation tasks.
    *   **Real-time Performance:** Achieving real-time performance for pose estimation is a valuable contribution.
    *   **End-to-End Learning:** Avoidance of multi-stage pipelines simplifies the development process.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **World Model Dependence:**  The inverse dynamics module relies heavily on the accuracy and plausibility of the world model's generated videos. Performance is capped by world model performance.
    *   **Limited Task Complexity:** The Libero-Object manipulation tasks, while challenging, are still relatively simple. The generalizability to more complex scenarios remains to be seen.
    *   **Offline Training:** The inverse dynamics model is trained offline, which may limit its adaptability to unforeseen situations or changing environments.
    *   **Incrementality:** While the integration is novel, the individual components (diffusion model, conditional U-Net) are not groundbreaking.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Inspiring more research on unified perception and control frameworks.
    *   Demonstrating the power of diffusion models for robot pose estimation and action generation.
    *   Encouraging the development of better world models for embodied AI.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a compelling and well-executed approach to a fundamental problem in robotics. The unified framework and strong empirical results make it a valuable contribution. While the novelty of individual components is limited, the system-level integration and demonstrated improvements justify a positive score. However, the dependence on world model quality and the relatively limited task complexity should temper enthusiasm.

### BPMN Assistant: An LLM-Based Approach to Business Process Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24592v1)
- **Authors**: Josip Tomo Licardo, Nikola Tankovic, Darko Etinger
- **Abstract**: This paper presents BPMN Assistant, a tool that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for natural language-based creation and editing of BPMN diagrams. A specialized JSON-based representation is introduced as a structured alternative to the direct handling of XML to enhance the accuracy of process modifications. Process generation quality is evaluated using Graph Edit Distance (GED) and Relative Graph Edit Distance (RGED), while editing performance is evaluated with a binary success metric. Results show that JSON and XML achieve similar similarity scores in generation, but JSON offers greater reliability, faster processing, and significantly higher editing success rates. We discuss key trade-offs, limitations, and future improvements. The implementation is available at https://github.com/jtlicardo/bpmn-assistant.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the BPMN Assistant paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BPMN Assistant, a tool leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) for natural language-based creation and editing of Business Process Model and Notation (BPMN) diagrams. It proposes a JSON-based representation as a structured alternative to direct XML manipulation to improve the accuracy and reliability of process modifications. The paper evaluates process generation quality using Graph Edit Distance (GED) and Relative Graph Edit Distance (RGED), and editing performance using a binary success metric. The results indicate that while JSON and XML achieve similar similarity scores in generation, JSON offers superior reliability, processing speed, and editing success rates.  The paper discusses limitations, potential improvements, and provides access to the implementation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its focus on the *editing* of BPMN diagrams via LLMs, an aspect less explored than diagram *generation* in existing literature. The comparison between JSON and XML as intermediate representations for LLM-based BPMN manipulation is also a valuable contribution. However, the core idea of using LLMs for BPMN modeling is not entirely novel, with tools like BPMN-Chatbot and ProMoAI already existing. The JSON representation provides an alternative implementation to using AST trees or direct XML editing, but is also not entirely breakthrough on it's own. The novelty is in the application and evaluation.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing a critical gap in BPMN tooling – the difficulty in modifying existing models, especially for non-technical users. By demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs for editing BPMN diagrams through natural language, the paper contributes to making BPMN more accessible and maintainable. The performance gains observed using JSON representation also has practical implications for developing efficient BPMN editing tools. The performance difference during the editing process is significant as it reduces the wait time for a user significantly. The findings that JSON requires more input tokens but yields shorter output tokens helps developers design the prompts that are used.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges associated with traditional BPMN modeling and the need for more accessible tools.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The evaluation metrics (GED, RGED, and editing success rate) are appropriate for assessing the structural accuracy and reliability of the generated and edited diagrams.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The comparison between JSON and XML representations provides valuable insights into their respective strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The availability of the implementation enhances the reproducibility and potential impact of the research.
    *   **Addressing practical limitations of LLMs for BPMN representation:** By proposing a structured representation of BPMN elements for LLMs to work with, it overcomes many of the limitations posed by working with the native XML structure. This reduces the chance of inconsistencies in the generation process.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of BPMN Elements:** The paper acknowledges the limited support for BPMN elements, restricting the complexity of models that can be handled.  This impacts the generalizability of the findings. The lack of collaboration diagram types is another restriction.
    *   **Lack of Semantic Evaluation:** The focus on structural accuracy neglects semantic aspects of the models. A diagram may be structurally correct but semantically nonsensical.
    *   **Dependence on LLM Quality:** The system's reliability is heavily dependent on the quality of the underlying LLM, a factor not fully addressed in the evaluation. The variation in different model's effectiveness also calls in to question if the proposed tool or prompt engineering approach is generalizable.
    *   **Missing Human Evaluation:** The lack of human evaluation and only focusing on GED, RGED and binary success rates does not capture the user experience and how useful the diagrams are for non-experts.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a practical and well-executed approach to LLM-based BPMN editing, it also inherits limitations from the existing BPMN automation research field. Namely, the models don't take into account the semantics of the generated BPMN process flows. The core idea builds upon existing approaches while focusing on a specific niche of process editing. The performance differences in edit rates is significant, but does not outweigh the limitations listed above. The paper makes a solid contribution within a well-established area.

### FreeRet: MLLMs as Training-Free Retrievers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24621v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhan Zhu, Xiangyu Zeng, Chenting Wang, Xinhao Li, Yicheng Xu, Ziang Yan, Yi Wang, Limin Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are emerging as versatile foundations for mixed-modality retrieval. Yet, they often require heavy post-hoc training to convert them into contrastive encoders for retrieval. This work asks: Can off-the-shelf MLLMs serve as powerful retrievers without additional training? We present FreeRet, a plug-and-play framework that turns any MLLM into a two-stage retriever. FreeRet first derives semantically grounded embeddings directly from the model for fast candidate search, and then exploits its reasoning ability for precise reranking. The framework contributes three advances: bypassing lexical alignment layers to obtain semantically faithful embeddings, conditioning representation generation with explicit priors, and mitigating framing effect in reranking via neutral choice framing. On the MMEB and MMEB-V2 benchmarks spanning 46 datasets, FreeRet substantially outperforms models trained on millions of pairs. Beyond benchmarks, FreeRet is model-agnostic and scales seamlessly across MLLM families and sizes, preserves their generative abilities, supports arbitrary modality combinations, and unifies retrieval, reranking, and generation into end-to-end RAG within a single model. Our findings demonstrate that pretrained MLLMs, when carefully harnessed, can serve as strong retrieval engines without training, closing a critical gap in their role as generalists.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FreeRet, a novel framework that enables Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to function as effective retrievers *without* requiring additional training. FreeRet operates in two stages: it first generates semantically-grounded embeddings directly from the MLLM for efficient candidate search, and subsequently leverages the MLLM's reasoning capabilities for precise reranking. The framework enhances embedding quality by bypassing lexical alignment layers, conditions representation generation using explicit priors, and mitigates framing effects in reranking through neutral choice framing. Evaluations on the MMEB and MMEB-V2 benchmarks demonstrate that FreeRet significantly outperforms models trained on millions of paired examples.  The authors emphasize FreeRet's model-agnostic nature, scalability, preservation of generative abilities, and its ability to unify retrieval, reranking, and generation within a single model for RAG applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of leveraging pre-trained MLLMs directly as retrievers *without* fine-tuning is novel and addresses a significant limitation of existing MLLM retrieval approaches.  The individual components (bypassing lexical layers, controlled prompt generation, MCQ-based reranking) have some precedents, but their specific combination and application *to make a training-free MLLM retriever viable* constitutes a substantial innovation. FreeRet stands out for its complete elimination of the need for fine-tuning or extra training data and also its comprehensive handling of the different stages of retrieval and reranking in an MLLM.

*   **Significance:** FreeRet holds significant potential for several reasons:
    *   **Reduced Training Cost:** Eliminating the need for expensive data curation and fine-tuning accelerates the deployment of MLLM-based retrieval systems across diverse modalities.
    *   **Improved Generalization:**  The reliance on pre-trained MLLM knowledge rather than task-specific training could lead to better generalization performance compared to fine-tuned models, especially in out-of-domain scenarios. The results showing FreeRet being competitive with, and even outperforming models trained on millions of pairs, supports this claim.
    *   **Simplified RAG Pipelines:** The ability to integrate retrieval, reranking, and generation within a single MLLM streamlines RAG pipelines, enhancing efficiency and reducing architectural complexity.
    *   **Increased Accessibility:** The model is easily deployable for different models as it works training-free.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The reported results on MMEB and MMEB-V2 benchmarks provide compelling evidence of FreeRet's effectiveness.
    *   **Comprehensive Ablation Study:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.
    *   **Practical Advantages:** The paper highlights the practical benefits of FreeRet, such as instant deployment, omni-modality support, and preservation of multimodal intelligence.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the framework and its advantages.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper highlights the reduced training cost, the computational cost of running inference on MLLMs can still be substantial. The paper acknowledges it may be higher latency than simpler CLIP based approaches. This trade-off between accuracy and efficiency needs to be considered in practical applications.
    *   **Reliance on Prompt Engineering:** The performance of FreeRet relies on carefully designed prompts. While the authors provide guidelines for prompt design, it can still be a challenge to find optimal prompts for different tasks and MLLMs.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides a brief theoretical analysis of lexicalization alignment, further theoretical understanding of the framework's behavior could be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** FreeRet has the potential to significantly influence the field of multimodal retrieval by promoting the use of pre-trained MLLMs as general-purpose retrieval engines. Its training-free nature and versatility could lead to wider adoption of MLLM-based retrieval systems in diverse applications.

*   **Areas for improvement/future work:**  Further exploration into optimization techniques to decrease inference latency, development of automated prompt optimization methods, and rigorous analysis of FreeRet's performance in few-shot or zero-shot transfer learning settings would be valuable.

**Conclusion:**

FreeRet presents a valuable contribution by demonstrating the feasibility and effectiveness of training-free MLLM retrieval. The framework offers significant advantages in terms of reduced training cost, improved generalization, and simplified RAG pipelines. While there are some limitations regarding computational cost and prompt engineering, the benefits outweigh the drawbacks.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** FreeRet achieves a high score due to its significant novelty, strong empirical results, and potentially high impact on the field. While some limitations exist, the framework represents a significant advancement in MLLM-based retrieval and has the potential to enable wider adoption of this technology. The rigorous rationale and comprehensive experimental verification underscores the value of this research.

- **Score**: 8/10

### PRIVMARK: Private Large Language Models Watermarking with MPC
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24624v1)
- **Authors**: Thomas Fargues, Ye Dong, Tianwei Zhang, Jin-Song Dong
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of Large Language Models (LLMs) has highlighted the pressing need for reliable mechanisms to verify content ownership and ensure traceability. Watermarking offers a promising path forward, but it remains limited by privacy concerns in sensitive scenarios, as traditional approaches often require direct access to a model's parameters or its training data. In this work, we propose a secure multi-party computation (MPC)-based private LLMs watermarking framework, PRIVMARK, to address the concerns. Concretely, we investigate PostMark (EMNLP'2024), one of the state-of-the-art LLMs Watermarking methods, and formulate its basic operations. Then, we construct efficient protocols for these operations using the MPC primitives in a black-box manner. In this way, PRIVMARK enables multiple parties to collaboratively watermark an LLM's output without exposing the model's weights to any single computing party. We implement PRIVMARK using SecretFlow-SPU (USENIX ATC'2023) and evaluate its performance using the ABY3 (CCS'2018) backend. The experimental results show that PRIVMARK achieves semantically identical results compared to the plaintext baseline without MPC and is resistant against paraphrasing and removing attacks with reasonable efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PRIVMARK: Private Large Language Models Watermarking with MPC" introduces a novel framework, PRIVMARK, for watermarking Large Language Models (LLMs) while preserving privacy. It tackles the challenge of verifying content ownership and ensuring traceability in sensitive settings where traditional watermarking methods, which require direct access to model parameters or training data, are not viable. PRIVMARK leverages Secure Multi-Party Computation (MPC) to enable multiple parties to collaboratively watermark an LLM's output without exposing the model's weights. The authors analyze and adapt PostMark, a state-of-the-art LLM watermarking method, constructing efficient MPC protocols for its operations. The framework is implemented using SecretFlow-SPU with the ABY3 backend, demonstrating comparable results to plaintext baselines without MPC, while maintaining resistance to paraphrasing and removal attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the *integration* of MPC with LLM watermarking. While both MPC and LLM watermarking are established fields, their combination to address the privacy concerns surrounding LLM ownership verification is a relatively new direction. The specific adaptation of the PostMark algorithm for secure computation contributes a non-trivial engineering effort. However, the *application* of MPC to watermarking is more innovative than the invention of novel MPC *primitives*. Many of the underlying MPC techniques are drawn from existing literature.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge: the privacy risks associated with watermarking LLMs, which can hinder adoption in collaborative or sensitive environments. A working private watermarking solution expands the applicability of watermarking, enabling accountability and IP protection without compromising data confidentiality. The demonstrated feasibility of the approach, through implementation and experimental results, is a valuable contribution. The paper’s contribution to the broader fields of privacy and security, specifically in relation to LLMs, is significant, because it serves as a tangible proof of concept showing how these concerns can be addressed in the near future.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Systematic analysis of the PostMark algorithm and its decomposition into MPC-compatible operations.
    *   Concrete implementation and experimental evaluation.
    *   Demonstration of the practicality of the approach.
    *   Identifies a valuable use case (watermarking co-owned LLMs).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on existing MPC primitives; the cryptographic contribution is incremental rather than groundbreaking.
    *   Performance limitations due to the computational overhead of MPC, especially noticeable in the WAN setting, although the authors acknowledge these and suggest future work to alleviate these issues.
    *   Security model is honest-but-curious, which might not be sufficient for all real-world scenarios.
    *   Limited robustness evaluation; more thorough analysis against stronger attacks would strengthen the findings.
    *   Dependency on the vocabulary V and the fact the the watermarking party must be trustworthy (see Remark 1 from the paper)

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research directions in several ways:
    *   Inspire further work on optimizing MPC protocols for LLM watermarking and other LLM-related privacy-preserving tasks.
    *   Encourage exploration of more sophisticated security models for MPC-based LLM applications.
    *   Promote the development of practical tools and frameworks for private LLM deployments.
    *   Motivate investigations into alternative watermarking methods that are inherently more privacy-friendly.

**Justification:**
The paper tackles a real-world problem with a practical solution using established cryptographic techniques. While the core MPC building blocks are not novel, their skillful application to LLM watermarking, along with a concrete implementation and experimental validation, makes a worthwhile contribution. The demonstrated feasibility is a key strength. The limitations, particularly in performance and threat model, prevent a higher score. The paper serves as a solid starting point and proof of concept, but significant optimization and extensions are needed to make it a truly transformative technology.

### Bridging Developer Instructions and Code Completion Through Instruction-Aware Fill-in-the-Middle Paradigm
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24637v1)
- **Authors**: Zhensu Sun, Chengran Yang, Chao Peng, Pengfei Gao, Xiaoning Du, Li Li, David Lo
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have significantly advanced code completion, yet they often fail when the developer's intent is underspecified in the code context. To address this, developers usually add natural language instructions (e.g., comments) into the code context to clarify their intent. However, existing code LLMs applied for code completion systems merely undergo a fill-in-the-middle (FIM) pre-training, which struggles to leverage this information effectively due to the lack of instruction-like training data. Existing instruction-tuning techniques, which improve instruction-following in general code generation, paradoxically degrade FIM performance, forcing a trade-off between instruction-following and infilling capabilities. To address this gap, we introduce Instruction-aware Fill-in-the-Middle (IFIM), an instruction-tuning method specifically designed to enhance FIM code completion models. IFIM extends the conventional FIM training objective by incorporating an explicit instruction section into the input, enabling the model to learn from (prefix, instruction, suffix) triplets. This approach allows the model to effectively leverage developer-provided directives while preserving its core completion abilities when no instructions are present. To facilitate this, we constructed a large-scale dataset by using GPT-4o to generate concise, intent-focused instructions for code infilling examples. We evaluated IFIM by applying it to two popular base models, Deepseek-Coder and Qwen2.5-Coder, on the benchmarks derived from HumanEval-infilling and RepoMasterEval. The results demonstrate that IFIM significantly improves instruction-following capabilities, boosting the Pass@1 score from 84.6% to 93.6% on HumanEval-infilling. Moreover, this enhancement does not compromise the models' original performance on FIM code completion tasks with no instructions provided.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Bridging Developer Instructions and Code Completion Through Instruction-Aware Fill-in-the-Middle Paradigm."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of Large Language Models (LLMs) for code completion struggling to effectively leverage natural language instructions (e.g., comments) provided by developers when the code context is underspecified. Existing fill-in-the-middle (FIM) pre-training alone isn't sufficient, and instruction tuning can degrade FIM performance. The authors propose Instruction-aware Fill-in-the-Middle (IFIM), a specialized instruction-tuning method for FIM code completion. IFIM incorporates an explicit instruction section into the input during training, allowing the model to learn from (prefix, instruction, suffix) triplets.  To facilitate this, they generate a large-scale dataset of code infilling examples with concise, intent-focused instructions using GPT-40.  They evaluate IFIM on two base models (Deepseek-Coder and Qwen2.5-Coder) and demonstrate improved instruction-following capabilities without compromising original FIM performance. The results show significant improvements in Pass@1 score on HumanEval-infilling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in recognizing and directly addressing the conflict between instruction-following and FIM code completion abilities in existing LLMs. While instruction tuning and FIM are individually established techniques, the authors identify a crucial gap in their combined application to code completion and propose a targeted solution.  The IFIM approach, extending the FIM objective rather than replacing it, is a significant contribution. The creation of a large-scale instruction dataset for code completion is also a valuable resource for the community. The specific insertion of the instruction before the middle segment (`I-before-M`) demonstrates insights towards instruction alignment to enhance LLMs.
* **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to improve the practical utility of LLM-powered code completion systems. By enabling models to better understand and respond to developer instructions, IFIM could lead to more helpful and accurate code suggestions in IDEs.  The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of IFIM on standard benchmarks, validating its potential. The finding that simply adding comments as instructions (CFIM) is detrimental underscores the importance of the structural separation of instructions and code segments in the model's input. Furthermore, the investigation on various instruction-data ratios in training is relevant to the community in tuning models.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and the limitations of existing approaches.
    * **Well-Designed Method:** IFIM is a simple but elegant solution that effectively addresses the identified gap.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct a thorough experimental evaluation with multiple base models, benchmarks, and ablation studies. The experiments show the dual benefit: improving instruction-following without degrading original FIM performance.
    * **Practical Implications:** The paper provides practical guidance for using IFIM in real-world code completion systems, including details on input formatting and IDE integration. The release of the dataset and code is a significant contribution to reproducibility and future research.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Synthetic Instructions:** The instructions used for training and evaluation are generated by GPT-40. While the authors perform manual revision for evaluation benchmarks, the reliance on synthetic data raises concerns about the realism and diversity of the instructions.  Real-world developer instructions may be more complex, ambiguous, or noisy.
    * **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation focuses on Python code and models with a limited parameter size. Generalizability to other programming languages and larger models remains to be demonstrated.
    * **Potential for Improvement:** Though the method claims to address the instruction alignment for code completions in LLMs, it focuses specifically on the fill-in-the-middle approach. Although effective, this narrows the scope to infilling code fragments and overlooks the overall challenges encountered in more expansive contexts, such as generating substantial code blocks or entire functions that require contextual alignment to complex requirements. The paper could benefit from exploring IFIM’s adaptability to different code generation challenges that extend beyond the infilling task.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in code completion by highlighting the importance of instruction-following and providing a practical method for improving it. The IFIM approach could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners to build more helpful and responsive code completion tools. The released dataset could also serve as a valuable resource for the community.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed solution to a significant problem in code completion. The IFIM approach effectively bridges the gap between instruction-following and FIM capabilities, and the experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness. The paper is well-written, clearly articulates its contributions, and provides practical guidance for implementation. However, the reliance on synthetic instructions and the limited scope of the evaluation are weaknesses that prevent it from achieving a higher score. Considering its contribution to the community in providing effective instructions for improving the FIM code completion LLMs, I think it is a valuable method for researchers to explore for their future works.

### Learning Object-Centric Representations Based on Slots in Real World Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24652v1)
- **Authors**: Adil Kaan Akan
- **Abstract**: A central goal in AI is to represent scenes as compositions of discrete objects, enabling fine-grained, controllable image and video generation. Yet leading diffusion models treat images holistically and rely on text conditioning, creating a mismatch for object-level editing. This thesis introduces a framework that adapts powerful pretrained diffusion models for object-centric synthesis while retaining their generative capacity. We identify a core challenge: balancing global scene coherence with disentangled object control. Our method integrates lightweight, slot-based conditioning into pretrained models, preserving their visual priors while providing object-specific manipulation. For images, SlotAdapt augments diffusion models with a register token for background/style and slot-conditioned modules for objects, reducing text-conditioning bias and achieving state-of-the-art results in object discovery, segmentation, compositional editing, and controllable image generation. We further extend the framework to video. Using Invariant Slot Attention (ISA) to separate object identity from pose and a Transformer-based temporal aggregator, our approach maintains consistent object representations and dynamics across frames. This yields new benchmarks in unsupervised video object segmentation and reconstruction, and supports advanced editing tasks such as object removal, replacement, and insertion without explicit supervision. Overall, this work establishes a general and scalable approach to object-centric generative modeling for images and videos. By bridging human object-based perception and machine learning, it expands the design space for interactive, structured, and user-driven generative tools in creative, scientific, and practical domains.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided thesis document. Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the research it presents:

**Summary**

The dissertation addresses the challenge of enabling generative models to understand and manipulate visual scenes in an object-centric manner. State-of-the-art diffusion models, while powerful, typically process images holistically and are conditioned on text, leading to semantic misalignment when object-level control is required. The thesis proposes a framework that adapts pre-trained generative models for object-centric image and video synthesis. The core contributions are:

1.  **SlotAdapt:** A method that augments diffusion models with lightweight, slot-based modules. A register token captures background and style, while slot-conditioned components encode object-specific information. This design mitigates text-conditioning bias and provides precise, object-centric control in images.

2.  Extending the framework to video using Invariant Slot Attention (ISA) to disentangle object identity from pose, combined with a Transformer-based temporal aggregator. This ensures consistent object representation and dynamics across time.

The framework achieves state-of-the-art results in unsupervised object segmentation, compositional editing (both image and video), and controllable image generation. The research demonstrates a novel approach to adapting pre-trained diffusion models for structured visual reasoning and manipulation.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the way the research tackles the conditioning problem in pre-trained diffusion models. Instead of training from scratch or naively forcing object slots into a text-centric framework, the thesis introduces a dual-pathway approach with adapter layers and register tokens. This approach and its specific implementations, such as the use of ISA for video, demonstrate a clever combination of existing techniques with a novel architecture and training strategy. The adaptation technique for pre-trained models, in particular, is an important contribution. Further, the extension to video and the enablement of compositional video editing are novel additions.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work comes from bridging the gap between two currently disparate fields: powerful but holistic generative models (diffusion models) and structured scene understanding (object-centric learning). Enabling object-centric control and manipulation in high-quality generative models has significant implications for content creation, scientific simulation, and AI that can reason about the physical world. The framework enables more intuitive control and reduces the reliance on text prompts, a practical bottleneck in current generative AI workflows. Also, high-fidelity compositional editing from unsupervised object-centric representations is a notable advancement with many downstream applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed framework with distinct components addressing specific challenges.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.
    *   Thorough ablation studies and analysis.
    *   Addresses a critical issue in current diffusion models and generative AI research.
    *   Extension to video and enablement of compositional editing demonstrates versatility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The framework still relies on a pre-trained diffusion model and is therefore limited to some extent by the training data and biases of that base model.
    *   Performance on highly cluttered or ambiguous scenes can still be improved.
    *   The object addition task is more challenging than object removal and replacement, indicating an area for future research.
    *   The work focuses on relatively short video clips of medium resolution. Scaling to long-form, high-resolution video represents a challenge.

*   **Potential Influence:** The thesis provides a foundation for future research in object-centric generative modeling. The adaptive framework can be extended to other generative architectures and tasks. Future research can build on this work to enable more sophisticated forms of reasoning and manipulation in generated content. The work can influence research in AI systems that require a structured understanding of the visual world, such as robotics and autonomous driving.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:** The thesis makes a significant contribution to the field by addressing the challenge of enabling object-centric control in diffusion models. The proposed framework is well-designed and achieves state-of-the-art results in multiple tasks. The extension to video and the ability to perform compositional editing are noteworthy. While there are limitations, such as reliance on pre-trained models and challenges with highly cluttered scenes, the strengths of the thesis far outweigh the weaknesses. It presents a substantial advance in object-centric generative modeling with potential impact for future research. The score of 8 reflects the novelty, significance, and thoroughness of the research, while acknowledging areas for further improvement.

### Identity Bridge: Enabling Implicit Reasoning via Shared Latent Memory
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24653v1)
- **Authors**: Pengxiao Lin, Zheng-An Chen, Zhi-Qin John Xu
- **Abstract**: Despite remarkable advances, large language models often fail at compositional reasoning tasks, a phenomenon exemplified by the ``curse of two-hop reasoning''. This paper introduces the Identity Bridge, a simple yet powerful mechanism that resolves this compositionality gap by supervising the model on a zero-hop identity task. We demonstrate empirically that this addition enables models to successfully perform out-of-distribution two-hop reasoning, a task they otherwise completely fail. To explain this phenomenon, we provide a theoretical analysis using a simplified Emb-MLP model, proving that identity supervision reshapes the model's latent geometry. We show this alignment is induced by an implicit nuclear-norm regularization during optimization, which favors low-rank solutions that share structure across tasks. For complex tasks, we use small initialization or weight decay to enhance the regularization effect, which enhances the latent space alignment effect and slows down the generalization decay. Finally, we extend our investigation to large-scale models, observing that they still achieve two-hop reasoning through the latent memory, which provides crucial inspiration for enhancing their implicit reasoning abilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Identity Bridge: Enabling Implicit Reasoning via Shared Latent Memory":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of compositional reasoning in large language models (LLMs), specifically the "curse of two-hop reasoning," where LLMs struggle to combine two single-hop facts to reach a conclusion. The authors introduce a simple yet effective mechanism called the "Identity Bridge."  This involves augmenting the training data with a zero-hop identity task, where bridge entities are mapped to themselves. The authors demonstrate empirically that this addition significantly improves the LLMs' ability to perform out-of-distribution (OOD) two-hop reasoning. They provide a theoretical analysis using a simplified Emb-MLP model to explain how identity supervision reshapes the model's latent geometry through implicit nuclear-norm regularization, promoting cross-task memory sharing and aligning representations. Furthermore, they address failure modes in high-complexity scenarios by employing techniques like small initialization or weight decay to enhance regularization and improve OOD generalization. They also observe that even pretrained LLMs benefit from the latent memory created with the addition of the Identity Bridge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of using an identity bridge to improve compositional reasoning is interesting and potentially novel. While the concept of auxiliary tasks is not new, applying it in this specific way to address the two-hop reasoning problem and providing theoretical justification adds value. The explanation of how identity supervision reshapes the latent space is particularly novel.

*   **Significance:**  The two-hop reasoning problem is a significant bottleneck for LLMs, limiting their ability to perform more complex reasoning tasks. The Identity Bridge, if proven robust, could be a valuable tool for improving LLMs' compositional abilities. The paper's theoretical analysis provides insights into the underlying mechanisms that enable this improvement. The focus on out-of-distribution generalization is also highly relevant, as it addresses the challenge of models overfitting to training data. The authors address real LLM failures and suggest a potential solution inspired by real LLM observations in later layers.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of two-hop reasoning and its importance.
    *   **Simple and Effective Solution:** The Identity Bridge is a simple addition to the training process, yet it yields significant improvements.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The authors provide a theoretical analysis to explain the mechanism underlying the Identity Bridge.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes empirical results on both simplified and large-scale models, supporting the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Addressing Failure Modes:** The authors acknowledge and address the limitations of the approach in high-complexity scenarios.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Simplified Model:** The theoretical analysis is based on a simplified Emb-MLP model. It is useful for understanding the core mechanism, it's a simplification of full Transformers with all their attention mechanisms. Also, the paper explicitly says that it's still researching why real models work with the Identity Bridge.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses primarily on synthetic datasets, which may not fully capture the complexities of real-world reasoning tasks.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Pretrained LLMs:** Fine-tuning results on pre-trained LLMs are interesting, but not quite conclusive on their own.
    *   **Potential Scalability Issues:** While the approach seems effective, the paper does not provide a thorough discussion of potential scalability issues when applied to very large models and datasets, which could involve a large number of entities.
    *   **Practical Implementation Detail Gaps:** The practical impact section would benefit from a description of how much memory is consumed and how much runtime it takes in various LLM settings, but is only theoretical.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Identity Bridge could be adopted by researchers and practitioners to improve the compositional reasoning abilities of LLMs. The theoretical insights could also inspire new approaches to representation learning and regularization.  The paper could also influence research on implicit reasoning and generalization in LLMs.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a promising approach to a significant problem, there are potential downsides to this approach. The theoretical model doesn't apply directly to large language models, and it remains unknown whether the same effects hold when applying the Identity Bridge to LLMs. This means that the paper doesn't apply directly in the field, instead influencing future research in LLMs.

**Score: 7**
