- **Score**: 7/10

### InfLLM-V2: Dense-Sparse Switchable Attention for Seamless Short-to-Long Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24663v1)
- **Authors**: Weilin Zhao, Zihan Zhou, Zhou Su, Chaojun Xiao, Yuxuan Li, Yanghao Li, Yudi Zhang, Weilun Zhao, Zhen Li, Yuxiang Huang, Ao Sun, Xu Han, Zhiyuan Liu
- **Abstract**: Long-sequence processing is a critical capability for modern large language models. However, the self-attention mechanism in the standard Transformer architecture faces severe computational and memory bottlenecks when processing long sequences. While trainable sparse attention methods offer a promising solution, existing approaches such as NSA introduce excessive extra parameters and disrupt the conventional \textit{pretrain-on-short, finetune-on-long} workflow, resulting in slow convergence and difficulty in acceleration. To overcome these limitations, we introduce dense-sparse switchable attention framework, termed as InfLLM-V2. InfLLM-V2 is a trainable sparse attention that seamlessly adapts models from short to long sequences. Specifically, InfLLM-V2 reuses dense attention parameters through parameter-free architecture modification, maintaining consistency between short and long sequence processing. Additionally, InfLLM-V2 ensures computational efficiency across all sequence lengths, by using dense attention for short inputs and smoothly transitioning to sparse attention for long sequences. To achieve practical acceleration, we further introduce an efficient implementation of InfLLM-V2 that significantly reduces the computational overhead. Our experiments on long-context understanding and chain-of-thought reasoning demonstrate that InfLLM-V2 is 4$\times$ faster than dense attention while retaining 98.1% and 99.7% of the performance, respectively. Based on the InfLLM-V2 framework, we have trained and open-sourced MiniCPM4.1 (https://huggingface.co/openbmb/MiniCPM4.1-8B), a hybrid reasoning model, providing a reproducible implementation for the research community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "INFLLM-V2: DENSE-SPARSE SWITCHABLE ATTENTION FOR SEAMLESS SHORT-TO-LONG ADAPTATION":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces InfLLM-V2, a trainable sparse attention framework designed to improve the efficiency of large language models (LLMs) when processing long sequences. InfLLM-V2 addresses the limitations of existing sparse attention methods, particularly the Non-Native Sparse Attention (NSA), which introduces extra parameters and disrupts the standard pretrain-on-short, finetune-on-long workflow. InfLLM-V2 reuses dense attention parameters through a parameter-free architectural modification, ensuring consistency across different sequence lengths and enabling a seamless transition between dense attention (for short sequences) and sparse attention (for long sequences). Furthermore, it introduces an efficient implementation to reduce computational overhead. The authors demonstrate that InfLLM-V2 achieves significant speedups compared to dense attention while maintaining high performance on long-context understanding and reasoning tasks. They also release a model, MiniCPM4.1, based on InfLLM-V2 to promote reproducibility and further research.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The novelty of this paper lies in its approach to sparse attention that aims for seamless integration with existing pretraining and finetuning paradigms for LLMs. The key innovative aspects are:

*   **Parameter Reuse:** The core idea of reusing dense attention parameters is a significant step forward, avoiding the parameter explosion associated with methods like NSA. This contributes to efficient training and adaptation.
*   **Dense-Sparse Switchability:** The framework's ability to dynamically switch between dense and sparse attention depending on the input sequence length is a valuable optimization.
*   **Efficient Implementation:** Addressing the overhead associated with block selection via a hardware-aware approach is critical for practical acceleration.
*   **Simplicity:** The design is cleaner and more aligned with the standard transformer architecture than NSA, potentially easing adoption and integration.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work is substantial due to the increasing importance of long-sequence processing in LLMs.

*   **Addresses a Core Bottleneck:** Long sequence processing is a well-known limitation of transformers, and InfLLM-V2 tackles this head-on by increasing speed by approximately 4x with a slight degradation in performence.
*   **Practical Benefits:** The seamless adaptation, reduced parameter overhead, and the provided efficient implementation translate into tangible benefits for training and deploying long-context LLMs. The released model, MiniCPM4.1, serves as a practical demonstration of the framework's effectiveness.
*   **Addresses NSA issues:** This paper identifies and addresses issues with NSA, particularly its disruption of established training workflows, its performance drop on short sequences and training instability making this paper very relevant.

**Weaknesses and Areas for Improvement:**

*   **Dependency on Block Sparsity:** The framework still relies on a block-sparse approach, which might not be optimal for all types of data or tasks. It would be beneficial to investigate adaptive or content-aware block selection strategies.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the approximation error introduced by the sparse attention mechanism. This would provide a deeper understanding of the trade-offs between sparsity and performance.
*   **Ablation Study Detail:** More details are required for the Ablation study, it is not clear why one method performs better than the other.

**Potential Influence:**

InfLLM-V2 has the potential to influence the field by:

*   **Guiding Future Research:** The insights gained from this work can guide future research towards developing more efficient and adaptable sparse attention mechanisms.
*   **Improving Existing LLMs:** The framework can be incorporated into existing LLMs to enhance their ability to process long sequences without significantly increasing computational costs.
*   **Enabling New Applications:** By enabling efficient long-sequence processing, InfLLM-V2 can unlock new applications for LLMs in areas such as scientific research, document summarization, and code generation.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a significant advance in sparse attention by addressing limitations of existing methods and offering a practical, efficient, and adaptable solution for long-sequence processing in LLMs. The novelty, significance, and potential influence on the field are undeniable. However, there are opportunities for more in-depth analysis and exploration of alternative sparsity patterns.

### Understanding the Dilemma of Unlearning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24675v1)
- **Authors**: Qingjie Zhang, Haoting Qian, Zhicong Huang, Cheng Hong, Minlie Huang, Ke Xu, Chao Zhang, Han Qiu
- **Abstract**: Unlearning seeks to remove specific knowledge from large language models (LLMs), but its effectiveness remains contested. On one side, "forgotten" knowledge can often be recovered through interventions such as light fine-tuning; on the other side, unlearning may induce catastrophic forgetting that degrades general capabilities. Despite active exploration of unlearning methods, interpretability analyses of the mechanism are scarce due to the difficulty of tracing knowledge in LLMs' complex architectures. We address this gap by proposing unPact, an interpretable framework for unlearning via prompt attribution and contribution tracking. Typically, it quantifies each prompt token's influence on outputs, enabling pre- and post-unlearning comparisons to reveal what changes. Across six mainstream unlearning methods, three LLMs, and three benchmarks, we find that: (1) Unlearning appears to be effective by disrupting focus on keywords in prompt; (2) Much of the knowledge is not truly erased and can be recovered by simply emphasizing these keywords in prompts, without modifying the model's weights; (3) Catastrophic forgetting arises from indiscriminate penalization of all tokens. Taken together, our results suggest an unlearning dilemma: existing methods tend either to be insufficient - knowledge remains recoverable by keyword emphasis, or overly destructive - general performance collapses due to catastrophic forgetting, still leaving a gap to reliable unlearning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, adhering to your specific instructions:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the effectiveness and underlying mechanisms of unlearning in large language models (LLMs).  It introduces UNPACT, an interpretable framework based on prompt attribution and contribution tracking, to analyze how unlearning alters an LLM's reliance on prompt tokens.  The authors conduct experiments across several unlearning methods, LLMs, and benchmarks, finding that unlearning disrupts the focus on relevant keywords, but the knowledge is often not truly erased and can be recovered through simple keyword emphasis. They also highlight that catastrophic forgetting arises from indiscriminate penalization of all tokens during unlearning. The paper concludes that existing unlearning methods face a dilemma: they are either insufficient (knowledge remains recoverable) or overly destructive (causing catastrophic forgetting), identifying a gap toward reliable and balanced unlearning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the UNPACT framework, which offers a prompt-centric interpretable lens for analyzing unlearning. While prompt attribution isn't entirely new in the broader LLM interpretability space, its application to unlearning, specifically to track changes in token importance before and after the process, appears to be a solid contribution. The observation of the "unlearning dilemma" is also a valuable insight, though the concepts of insufficient unlearning and catastrophic forgetting are not entirely unexplored in existing literature.
*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it addresses a crucial aspect of responsible AI development: ensuring that LLMs can effectively and safely forget specific knowledge. The UNPACT framework can assist in diagnosing the strengths and weaknesses of existing unlearning algorithms. The identified dilemma offers actionable insights for future research, suggesting the need for more refined unlearning methods that target specific knowledge while minimizing disruption to general capabilities.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Interpretability:** UNPACT provides a valuable tool for understanding the inner workings of unlearning methods.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The framework is applied to a range of unlearning techniques, LLMs, and datasets, lending credibility to the findings.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The "unlearning dilemma" is articulated clearly and provides a compelling framing for the challenges in the field.
    *   **Prompt-centric Approach:** The prompt-centric approach is beneficial as it works for both open and closed-source LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Recovery Strategies:** The paper focuses primarily on keyword emphasis as a recovery strategy. Exploring a broader range of recovery methods, such as rephrasing the prompt or using different prompting techniques, could provide a more comprehensive understanding of knowledge persistence after unlearning.
    *   **Qualitative Aspects of Catastrophic Forgetting:** The analysis of catastrophic forgetting focuses on nonsense outputs but could benefit from more in-depth qualitative analysis of the *types* of general knowledge lost.
    *   **Limited Scope on Evaluation Metrics:** The paper could use more standard metrics for evaluating the model's performance before and after unlearning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research by:

    *   Motivating the development of more targeted unlearning techniques.
    *   Encouraging the use of interpretability methods to evaluate unlearning effectiveness.
    *   Highlighting the need for methods that balance knowledge removal with general performance.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper makes a meaningful contribution to the field of LLM unlearning through its interpretable framework and insightful analysis of the challenges involved. While some aspects could be explored in more depth, it provides a valuable step forward in understanding and addressing the limitations of existing unlearning techniques.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a valuable and reasonably novel framework for interpreting unlearning in LLMs. The identification of the "unlearning dilemma" is a useful contribution to the field. However, the limitations in the scope of explored recovery strategies and in-depth analysis of the kinds of knowledge lost to catastrophic forgetting detract from the paper's overall impact. The reliance on LLM-as-a-judge is also open to interpretation, adding uncertainty to the qualitative evaluation.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SANA-Video: Efficient Video Generation with Block Linear Diffusion Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24695v1)
- **Authors**: Junsong Chen, Yuyang Zhao, Jincheng Yu, Ruihang Chu, Junyu Chen, Shuai Yang, Xianbang Wang, Yicheng Pan, Daquan Zhou, Huan Ling, Haozhe Liu, Hongwei Yi, Hao Zhang, Muyang Li, Yukang Chen, Han Cai, Sanja Fidler, Ping Luo, Song Han, Enze Xie
- **Abstract**: We introduce SANA-Video, a small diffusion model that can efficiently generate videos up to 720x1280 resolution and minute-length duration. SANA-Video synthesizes high-resolution, high-quality and long videos with strong text-video alignment at a remarkably fast speed, deployable on RTX 5090 GPU. Two core designs ensure our efficient, effective and long video generation: (1) Linear DiT: We leverage linear attention as the core operation, which is more efficient than vanilla attention given the large number of tokens processed in video generation. (2) Constant-Memory KV cache for Block Linear Attention: we design block-wise autoregressive approach for long video generation by employing a constant-memory state, derived from the cumulative properties of linear attention. This KV cache provides the Linear DiT with global context at a fixed memory cost, eliminating the need for a traditional KV cache and enabling efficient, minute-long video generation. In addition, we explore effective data filters and model training strategies, narrowing the training cost to 12 days on 64 H100 GPUs, which is only 1% of the cost of MovieGen. Given its low cost, SANA-Video achieves competitive performance compared to modern state-of-the-art small diffusion models (e.g., Wan 2.1-1.3B and SkyReel-V2-1.3B) while being 16x faster in measured latency. Moreover, SANA-Video can be deployed on RTX 5090 GPUs with NVFP4 precision, accelerating the inference speed of generating a 5-second 720p video from 71s to 29s (2.4x speedup). In summary, SANA-Video enables low-cost, high-quality video generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SANA-Video paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SANA-Video, a small and efficient diffusion model for generating high-resolution (up to 720x1280) videos of minute-long duration.  The core contributions are two-fold: 1) *Linear DiT*, which replaces standard attention with more computationally efficient linear attention, reducing the complexity and improving speed; and 2) *Constant-Memory KV Cache for Block Linear Attention*, which employs a block-wise autoregressive approach for generating long videos using a fixed-size memory cache derived from the properties of linear attention.  This approach eliminates the need for a traditional KV cache, enabling the generation of longer videos efficiently. The authors also incorporate effective data filtering and model training strategies to reduce training costs substantially. Experiments show SANA-Video achieving competitive performance with existing models, but at significantly faster generation speeds and lower hardware requirements. The model is also quantizable and deployable on devices like RTX 5090.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of linear attention with a constant-memory KV cache represents a useful step forward in efficient video generation. While linear attention has been explored before, its specific adaptation to the video domain with block-wise generation and a memory-efficient KV cache is reasonably novel. The use of RoPE after ReLU is also a relevant design choice. However, the incremental improvements might not be considered extremely groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:**  The primary significance of the work stems from its efficiency gains. SANA-Video's reduced computational cost makes high-resolution video generation more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited resources. The ability to deploy on devices like the RTX 5090 is also a practical advantage. The reduced training cost also presents a benefit to the broader research community. The improvements in the training method can have an impact as well.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Efficiency:* The paper convincingly demonstrates significant improvements in inference speed and reduced memory footprint compared to existing methods, which is valuable.
    *   *Data filtering pipeline and training strategies* The paper showcases a well-designed pipeline.
    *   *Experimental Results:* The quantitative results (VBench scores, latency measurements) support the claims of improved performance.
    *   *Practicality:* The ability to run SANA-Video on consumer-grade GPUs with quantization is a practical and relevant advantage.
    *   *Good Ablations:* The ablation studies are reasonably comprehensive and help to explain the impact of different design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Incremental Novelty:* The work builds upon existing techniques (linear attention, diffusion models). While the integration is clever, the novelty may be viewed as incremental compared to a totally fresh approach.
    *   *Visual Quality vs. SOTA:* While competitive, the paper doesn't definitively establish a substantial improvement in *visual quality* over state-of-the-art *large* diffusion models. The focus is more on efficiency. There might be certain artifacts or limitations to visual quality inherent in the linear attention approach.
    *   *Limited details of DCAE-V*: Details of the DCAE-V are very sparse.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work could influence future research by encouraging further exploration of efficient attention mechanisms and training strategies for video generation. The constant-memory KV cache could be a useful technique for long-sequence generation in other domains as well. It also makes video generation more accessible, which could have impact.

**Justification of Score:**

Based on the above analysis, SANA-Video represents a valuable engineering contribution to the field of video generation. It successfully addresses the challenge of efficient high-resolution video generation through a combination of existing and novel techniques. While the novelty is not revolutionary, the practical benefits in terms of speed, cost, and hardware requirements are significant.  It doesn't achieve SOTA results, but is competitive while being much more efficient.

### T-POP: Test-Time Personalization with Online Preference Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24696v1)
- **Authors**: Zikun Qu, Min Zhang, Mingze Kong, Xiang Li, Zhiwei Shang, Zhiyong Wang, Yikun Ban, Shuang Qiu, Yao Shu, Zhongxiang Dai
- **Abstract**: Personalizing large language models (LLMs) to individual user preferences is a critical step beyond generating generically helpful responses. However, current personalization methods are ill-suited for new users, as they typically require either slow, resource-intensive fine-tuning or a substantial amount of pre-existing user data, creating a significant cold-start problem. To address this challenge, we introduce a new paradigm for real-time personalization by learning from online pairwise preference feedback collected during text generation. We propose T-POP (Test-Time Personalization with Online Preference Feedback}), a novel algorithm that synergistically combines test-time alignment with dueling bandits. Without updating the LLM parameters, T-POP steers the decoding process of a frozen LLM by learning a reward function online that captures user preferences. By leveraging dueling bandits, T-POP intelligently queries the user to efficiently balance between exploring their preferences and exploiting the learned knowledge to generate personalized text. Extensive experiments demonstrate that T-POP achieves rapid and data-efficient personalization, significantly outperforming existing baselines and showing consistent improvement with more user interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "T-POP: TEST-TIME PERSONALIZATION WITH ONLINE PREFERENCE FEEDBACK":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces T-POP (Test-Time Personalization with Online Preference Feedback), a novel algorithm designed to personalize large language model (LLM) outputs to individual user preferences, addressing the cold-start problem where there is limited or no prior user data. T-POP combines test-time alignment with a dueling bandit framework. It guides the decoding process of a frozen LLM using an online-learned reward function that captures user preferences through pairwise feedback. By leveraging dueling bandits, T-POP balances exploration (learning user preferences) and exploitation (generating personalized text) efficiently. Experiments demonstrate that T-POP achieves rapid, data-efficient personalization, outperforming existing methods with relatively few user interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining test-time alignment with dueling bandits for online personalization is novel. While test-time alignment and dueling bandits are not new individually, their synergistic combination in the context of LLM personalization with online feedback presents a unique contribution. The T-POP algorithm provides a practical instantiation of this idea. Prior methods often rely on either computationally expensive fine-tuning or extensive pre-existing user data. T-POP offers a training-free, data-efficient alternative. However, similar works utilizing related bandit approaches for online text customization may exist, lessening the apparent novelty. Careful comparison against them could strengthen the evaluation. The method also depends on an existing LLM backbone, so it doesn't offer innovation in the LLM architecture itself.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in the practical deployment of LLMs – tailoring responses to individual users without requiring extensive resources or historical data. Overcoming the cold-start problem is essential for scaling personalized AI applications. The experimental results show significant performance gains over baselines across different LLMs, datasets, and personalization attributes. The fact that T-POP consistently improves with more user interactions indicates its potential for long-term personalization. The claim of not requiring any parameter updates also enhances the scalability. However, there are limitations in defining and evaluating the personalization attributes, it would also be very helpful to add more details regarding the preference of the generated responses for different users.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a relevant and significant problem (cold-start personalization).
    *   Proposes a novel and principled algorithm (T-POP).
    *   Presents thorough experimental results with multiple LLMs, datasets, and personalization attributes.
    *   Demonstrates superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   Highlights the data efficiency and scalability of the approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation relies heavily on a pre-trained reward model (ArmoRM) and GPT-4o as a judge, which could introduce biases and limitations.  It's unclear how robust the approach is to different reward models or evaluators.
    *   The experimental setup, particularly the simulation of user preferences, could be more realistic. The AlpacaEval standard format might not fully capture the complexities of human preferences.
    *   The paper could delve deeper into the limitations and potential failure cases of T-POP. How does it handle contradictory feedback or noisy preferences?
    *   Computational costs beyond training-free personalization are not discussed. Memory, latency and overall hardware requirements could be clarified for practitioners.

* **Potential Influence**: The method allows LLMs to deliver more customized content for new users. The proposed framework could inform future research on online learning, test-time adaptation, and LLM personalization.

The significant advancement in addressing the cold-start problem and the consistent results across diverse settings warrant a solid score. However, weaknesses in evaluation and a less complete analysis of method limitations keeps it from being a top tier paper.

### FedPOB: Sample-Efficient Federated Prompt Optimization via Bandits
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24701v1)
- **Authors**: Pingchen Lu, Zhi Hong, Zhiwei Shang, Zhiyong Wang, Yikun Ban, Yao Shu, Min Zhang, Shuang Qiu, Zhongxiang Dai
- **Abstract**: The performance of large language models (LLMs) is highly sensitive to the input prompt, making prompt optimization a critical task. However, real-world application is hindered by three major challenges: (1) the black-box nature of powerful proprietary LLMs, (2) the need for high sample efficiency due to query costs, and (3) the desire for privacy-preserving collaboration among multiple users. To address these challenges simultaneously, we introduce a novel framework for sample-efficient federated prompt optimization based on multi-armed bandits (MABs). The MAB framework is uniquely suited for this problem as it is (1) inherently a black-box optimization method, (2) practically sample-efficient, and (3) enables collaborative learning with theoretically guaranteed benefit from more participating agents. We first propose the Federated Prompt Optimization via Bandits (FedPOB) algorithm, a federated variant of the Linear UCB algorithm, where agents collaborate by sharing model parameters instead of raw data. We then extend our approach to the practical setting of comparative user feedback by introducing FedPOB with Preference Feedback (FedPOB-Pref), an efficient algorithm based on federated dueling bandits. Extensive experiments demonstrate that both FedPOB and FedPOB-Pref significantly outperform existing baselines and that their performance consistently improves as more agents participate in the collaboration, validating the effectiveness of our federated approach.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FEDPOB: Sample-Efficient Federated Prompt Optimization via Bandits":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of prompt optimization for Large Language Models (LLMs) in a federated setting, considering the challenges of black-box access to LLMs, the need for sample efficiency due to query costs, and the desire for privacy-preserving collaboration among multiple users. The authors propose a novel framework based on multi-armed bandits (MABs) called Federated Prompt Optimization via Bandits (FedPOB).  They introduce two algorithms: FedPOB, which handles direct score feedback, and FedPOB-Pref, which extends the approach to the practical setting of comparative user feedback using federated dueling bandits. The algorithms are evaluated experimentally, demonstrating improved performance over existing baselines and increased performance with more participating agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its combination of federated learning and multi-armed bandits for prompt optimization, particularly in the context of black-box LLMs and privacy concerns. While federated learning and MABs are established areas, their application to prompt optimization with these specific constraints is a meaningful contribution. The extension to preference feedback (FedPOB-Pref) is also valuable, aligning with practical scenarios.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a significant problem: how to effectively optimize prompts for LLMs when access is limited and data privacy is paramount. The MAB framework is well-suited for this setting. The reported experimental results are promising, indicating a potential for real-world impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a relevant and challenging problem:** The paper tackles a practical issue that hinders the widespread adoption of LLMs.
    *   **Sound theoretical foundation:** The use of MABs provides a principled approach to black-box optimization and collaboration.
    *   **Strong empirical validation:**  Experiments demonstrate significant performance improvements over existing baselines, showcasing the effectiveness of the proposed algorithms.
    *   **Addresses practical constraints:** The work specifically tackles the challenges of sample efficiency and user privacy.
    *   **The dynamic regularization technique addresses an important problem in federated learning:** Specifically, it reduces fluctuations caused by inter-agent heterogeneity.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope of LLMs used:** While GPT-3.5-turbo is a reasonable choice, it would strengthen the paper to show applicability across more LLMs.
    *   **Potential for better baseline comparison:** The comparison to FedOne is appropriate for the federated setting, but the baselines for non-federated prompt optimization (INSTINCT, PromptBreeder) are not necessarily state-of-the-art, which potentially understates how much FedPOB is truly contributing.
    *   **Reliance on pre-trained embedding models:**  The dependence on pre-trained text embedding models such as MPNet is a potential limitation.  The performance is likely affected by the quality of these embeddings, and the framework doesn't address how to optimize prompts when such pre-trained resources are not available or are domain-mismatched.
    *   **Practical deployment considerations:** While the paper addresses communication costs to an extent, a more thorough discussion of the practical aspects of deploying these algorithms in real-world federated environments, including agent heterogeneity (varying computational resources and network connectivity), would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in federated prompt optimization, particularly in scenarios involving black-box LLMs and privacy constraints. The combination of MABs and federated learning offers a promising approach for tackling these challenges.

*   **Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. The paper presents a valuable and innovative solution to a practical problem. The approach is well-founded in theory and supported by strong empirical evidence, clearly demonstrating improvements over existing baselines.  The weaknesses discussed—specifically the limited scope of LLM models and potential for better baselines—slightly detract from the overall impact.

### Enhancing Physical Plausibility in Video Generation by Reasoning the Implausibility
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24702v1)
- **Authors**: Yutong Hao, Chen Chen, Ajmal Saeed Mian, Chang Xu, Daochang Liu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models can generate realistic videos, but existing methods rely on implicitly learning physical reasoning from large-scale text-video datasets, which is costly, difficult to scale, and still prone to producing implausible motions that violate fundamental physical laws. We introduce a training-free framework that improves physical plausibility at inference time by explicitly reasoning about implausibility and guiding the generation away from it. Specifically, we employ a lightweight physics-aware reasoning pipeline to construct counterfactual prompts that deliberately encode physics-violating behaviors. Then, we propose a novel Synchronized Decoupled Guidance (SDG) strategy, which leverages these prompts through synchronized directional normalization to counteract lagged suppression and trajectory-decoupled denoising to mitigate cumulative trajectory bias, ensuring that implausible content is suppressed immediately and consistently throughout denoising. Experiments across different physical domains show that our approach substantially enhances physical fidelity while maintaining photorealism, despite requiring no additional training. Ablation studies confirm the complementary effectiveness of both the physics-aware reasoning component and SDG. In particular, the aforementioned two designs of SDG are also individually validated to contribute critically to the suppression of implausible content and the overall gains in physical plausibility. This establishes a new and plug-and-play physics-aware paradigm for video generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "Enhancing Physical Plausibility in Video Generation by Reasoning the Implausibility":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of physically implausible motions in text-to-video generation. It introduces a training-free framework that enhances physical plausibility by reasoning about implausibility and guiding the generation away from it. The approach involves:

1.  **Physics-Aware Reasoning (PAR):** Using a large language model (LLM) to analyze a text prompt, identify entities, interactions, scene conditions, and predict the physically valid trajectory of the event. Then, it creates a counterfactual prompt that violates the expected physical law.
2.  **Synchronized Decoupled Guidance (SDG):** A novel guidance strategy that leverages the original and counterfactual prompts. It consists of:

    *   *Synchronized Directional Normalization:*  Normalizes the difference between the noise predictions from the original and counterfactual prompts, ensuring that the counterfactual prompt has an immediate and consistent influence.
    *   *Trajectory-Decoupled Denoising:*  Evolves two separate latent trajectories based on the original and counterfactual prompts.  This helps avoid biases introduced by early-stage conditioning on the original prompt.

The authors demonstrate that their method improves physical fidelity on PhyGenBench and VideoPhy benchmarks without requiring any training or fine-tuning of the underlying diffusion model.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a good degree of novelty in its approach to physics-aware video generation. While negative prompting is a known technique, the paper identifies specific limitations (lagged suppression, cumulative bias) when applied to physics problems and introduces a new guidance strategy (SDG) to directly address those limitations. Using LLMs to generate counterfactual prompts is also a novel and effective way to steer generation away from physical impossibilities.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in text-to-video generation: ensuring physical plausibility. By explicitly reasoning about physics, the proposed method represents a step toward creating general-purpose world simulators. The fact that the method is training-free makes it applicable to a wide range of existing diffusion models.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Training-free approach:*  The framework can be applied to existing models without retraining, enhancing its practical value.
    *   *Explicit reasoning:* The approach explicitly reasons about physics using LLMs, leading to more targeted counterfactual prompts than generic negative prompts.
    *   *Effective guidance strategy:* The proposed SDG strategy overcomes limitations of negative prompting, ensuring prompt suppression and more complete physics adherence.
    *   *Quantitative and qualitative evaluation:* The paper provides thorough experiments on established benchmarks.
    *   *Clear articulation of problems and solutions:*  The identification of lagged suppression and cumulative bias is clearly explained. The designs of SDG (directional normalization and decoupled denoising) effectively mitigate these problems.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Reliance on LLMs:* The quality of the generated counterfactual prompts depends on the performance of the underlying LLM. The LLM usage and prompt instructions should be outlined in more detail.
    *   *Limited improvement on all benchmarks:* The improvement on VideoPhy is small. The evaluation on VideoPhy is also limited by the fact that the evaluator does not have access to user prompts at test time.
    *   *Computational cost of two branches:* Running two branches for trajectory-decoupled denoising will have a non-negligible cost when generating videos.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work could influence future research in video generation by promoting a more explicit approach to physical reasoning. The proposed SDG could be adopted as a general-purpose guidance strategy for addressing limitations of negative prompting in various contexts. The LLM-based counterfactual prompt generation method can be used for generating prompts in other domains too.
*   **Areas of Future work:**
    * Improve the LLM reasoning for counterfactual prompt generation
    * Explore how to make the approach more computationally efficient

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and effective training-free framework for improving physical plausibility in video generation. The explicit reasoning approach and novel guidance strategy address limitations of existing methods. The experiments demonstrate clear improvements on several benchmarks, even though improvement on all of the tests are limited. The approach also has practical benefits, that enhance the significance of the presented results. The reliance on the LLM is a weakness as is its limited improvements. However, given the clear contributions to the physics plausibility in video generation, the paper is assigned a score of:

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MAD: Manifold Attracted Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24710v1)
- **Authors**: Dennis Elbrächter, Giovanni S. Alberti, Matteo Santacesaria
- **Abstract**: Score-based diffusion models are a highly effective method for generating samples from a distribution of images. We consider scenarios where the training data comes from a noisy version of the target distribution, and present an efficiently implementable modification of the inference procedure to generate noiseless samples. Our approach is motivated by the manifold hypothesis, according to which meaningful data is concentrated around some low-dimensional manifold of a high-dimensional ambient space. The central idea is that noise manifests as low magnitude variation in off-manifold directions in contrast to the relevant variation of the desired distribution which is mostly confined to on-manifold directions. We introduce the notion of an extended score and show that, in a simplified setting, it can be used to reduce small variations to zero, while leaving large variations mostly unchanged. We describe how its approximation can be computed efficiently from an approximation to the standard score and demonstrate its efficacy on toy problems, synthetic data, and real data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MAD: Manifold Attracted Diffusion":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces Manifold Attracted Diffusion (MAD), a novel inference procedure for score-based diffusion models.  The core idea is to modify the inference step to leverage the manifold hypothesis, which posits that meaningful data lies near a low-dimensional manifold within the high-dimensional data space. MAD uses an "extended score" function that acts like the standard score where it exists, but also has a well-defined behavior for Dirac delta functions.  This allows it to filter out noise that is orthogonal to the manifold and effectively attract samples toward the underlying data structure. The paper details the extended score's definition and analysis, presents an efficient implementation of the inference algorithm, and validates the approach empirically on synthetic data, standard image datasets (FFHQ, AFHQv2, ImageNet), and real cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) data. The MAD approach does not require modification of the training procedure, and is compatible with pre-trained diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central concept of an "extended score" that smoothly handles Dirac delta distributions and can be approximated from standard scores is a novel contribution. Prior works have addressed noisy training in diffusion models, but these generally require modifying the training process itself. The inference-only modification is a significant point of differentiation, giving this paper some novel flexibility.

*   **Significance:** The ability to generate clean samples from diffusion models trained on noisy data has potential impact across various domains where data acquisition is inherently noisy (e.g., medical imaging, scientific data). The experiments on cryo-EM data are particularly compelling because this data type has real-world impact. The MAD inference procedure is also efficient and simple to implement since it only relies on estimating the derivative of a pre-trained model.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Conceptual Clarity:** The paper clearly explains the manifold hypothesis and its motivation for the proposed approach. The definition of extended score is well-described, with theoretical analysis and justification.
    *   **Practical Applicability:** MAD's compatibility with pre-trained models and ease of implementation makes it highly accessible. The experiments across diverse datasets demonstrate generalizability.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper uses a range of experiments, from illustrative low-dimensional examples to high-dimensional image generation and real-world scientific data (cryo-EM), to provide strong empirical evidence supporting the claims. The ablation studies on synthetic data help to showcase the role of each hyperparameter.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the paper shows impressive results, there are several hyperparameters in the MAD inference process. The authors mention that manual tuning may be required. A deeper analysis of the hyperparameter space and some suggestions for automatic tuning would significantly improve the method's usability. Without this analysis, MAD will likely be considered a difficult to use or computationally expensive method.

    *   **Limited Theoretical Guarantees:** While the paper presents an analysis of the extended score, there's a lack of strong theoretical guarantees about the convergence and denoising properties of the MAD inference algorithm itself, especially in high-dimensional settings. Addressing this would considerably improve the paper's overall strength.
    *   **Cryo-EM Experiment Specificity:** Although the cryo-EM experiment is compelling, the generated shapes are qualitatively compared to existing work and could be improved by a more in depth comparison to the State Of The Art (SOTA) for Cryo-EM denoising and image recovery. Additionally, the current parameters were determined by hand based on known information. While the generated shapes are well presented, the method may be very problem specific and not readily transferrable.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the hyperparameter tuning issue is addressed, MAD has the potential to become a widely used inference technique for diffusion models, particularly in noisy data scenarios. The framework can also be integrated with other inference optimization techniques and conditional diffusion models.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practically relevant approach to denoising diffusion models trained on noisy data. The extended score is a good theoretical and practical contribution, with the major strength being its capacity to be combined with pre-trained models and its practical applicability. The thorough empirical validation across different datasets is also a significant strength. However, the significant hyperparameter sensitivity and a lack of strong theoretical guarantees limits its influence. Further improving the experimental setup to better compare to existing cryo-EM recovery approaches or by applying hyperparameter tuning to more standard diffusion-based image generation (FFHQ, AFHQ, etc) would increase the method's impact. Therefore, while it's a good contribution, the limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### Discrete Variational Autoencoding via Policy Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24716v1)
- **Authors**: Michael Drolet, Firas Al-Hafez, Aditya Bhatt, Jan Peters, Oleg Arenz
- **Abstract**: Discrete latent bottlenecks in variational autoencoders (VAEs) offer high bit efficiency and can be modeled with autoregressive discrete distributions, enabling parameter-efficient multimodal search with transformers. However, discrete random variables do not allow for exact differentiable parameterization; therefore, discrete VAEs typically rely on approximations, such as Gumbel-Softmax reparameterization or straight-through gradient estimates, or employ high-variance gradient-free methods such as REINFORCE that have had limited success on high-dimensional tasks such as image reconstruction. Inspired by popular techniques in policy search, we propose a training framework for discrete VAEs that leverages the natural gradient of a non-parametric encoder to update the parametric encoder without requiring reparameterization. Our method, combined with automatic step size adaptation and a transformer-based encoder, scales to challenging datasets such as ImageNet and outperforms both approximate reparameterization methods and quantization-based discrete autoencoders in reconstructing high-dimensional data from compact latent spaces, achieving a 20% improvement on FID Score for ImageNet 256.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Discrete Variational Autoencoding via Policy Search":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Discrete Autoencoding via Policy Search (DAPS), a novel training framework for discrete variational autoencoders (VAEs). DAPS addresses the challenges of training discrete VAEs, which often rely on non-differentiable operations and approximate reparameterization techniques like Gumbel-Softmax or straight-through estimators, leading to issues with gradient variance, temperature sensitivity, and memory footprint. DAPS frames encoder learning as a KL-regularized policy search problem, using the natural gradient of a non-parametric encoder to update a parametric encoder without requiring reparameterization tricks. The method incorporates automatic step size adaptation and a transformer-based encoder. The paper demonstrates that DAPS achieves superior reconstruction quality on high-dimensional datasets like ImageNet, outperforming existing methods such as Gumbel-Softmax, GR-MCK, and vector quantization-based autoencoders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in reframing discrete VAE training as a policy search problem. The use of a non-parametric target distribution and weighted maximum likelihood updates to train the encoder avoids the usual approximations and differentiability problems inherent in discrete latent variable models.  The application of techniques such as trust-region methods based on the Kullback-Leibler divergence to discrete VAE training is a somewhat novel adaption of well known methods, although they are also relatively straightforward.
*   **Significance:**  The ability to train discrete VAEs effectively on high-dimensional data like ImageNet is a significant advancement. Discrete latent spaces offer advantages in bit-efficiency, multimodal modeling, and integration with combinatorial optimization tools. Improved training stability (addressed through automatic step size adaptation) also adds to the practicality of this approach. The results show a marked improvement on ImageNet 256 in terms of FID score compared to alternatives, suggesting practical relevance. The exploration of explicit entropy control via β opens up opportunities for more controlled latent space exploration.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement: The paper articulates the challenges of training discrete VAEs well.
    *   Sound methodology:  The policy search approach is well-motivated and mathematically sound.
    *   Strong empirical results:  DAPS outperforms existing methods on multiple datasets, including the challenging ImageNet dataset.
    *   Stable training: Automatic step-size adaptation leads to more stable training dynamics.
    *   Explicit control: The framework provides an avenue to explictly regularize entropy.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Computational complexity:  While DAPS avoids backpropagation through sampling, the policy search approach might introduce its own computational overhead, which the paper doesn't discuss extensively. The need to compute importance weights, even with self-normalization, adds complexity.
    *   Limited ablation studies: The ablation studies focus primarily on B and ESS target, but could benefit from deeper analysis of the impact of different components of the policy search algorithm (e.g. the advantage estimator, the ESS calculation).
    *   Reliance on Transformer encoder: The advantage on ImageNet seems partially due to using a transformer based encoder while other models may use less complex encoders, which introduces a confounding factor.
    *   Incrementality: The approach is primarily an adaptation of existing policy search approaches, although those approaches have been overlooked in the discrete VAE literature.

*   **Potential Influence:** DAPS could influence the development of more efficient and stable training algorithms for discrete latent variable models.  It could lead to new applications of discrete VAEs in areas like image generation, representation learning, and reinforcement learning, where structured or compact latent spaces are beneficial.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by introducing a novel and effective training framework for discrete VAEs. The results, especially on ImageNet, are compelling and demonstrate the superiority of DAPS over existing methods. While the approach relies on existing policy search techniques to some extent, the adaption of these techniques to dicrete VAEs is novel and effective. The paper is well-written, clearly explains the methodology, and provides thorough empirical evidence. The limitations regarding computational complexity and ablation studies detract slightly from the overall score. The reliance on a transformer-based encoder presents a potential confounding factor. Thus, despite the strengths, the limitations hold back the paper from achieving a truly outstanding score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Socratic-Zero : Bootstrapping Reasoning via Data-Free Agent Co-evolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24726v1)
- **Authors**: Shaobo Wang, Zhengbo Jiao, Zifan Zhang, Yilang Peng, Xu Ze, Boyu Yang, Wei Wang, Hu Wei, Linfeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent breakthroughs in large language models (LLMs) on reasoning tasks rely heavily on massive, high-quality datasets-typically human-annotated and thus difficult to scale. While data synthesis or distillation offers a promising alternative, existing methods struggle with inconsistent data quality and an inability to dynamically adapt to the evolving capabilities of the model, leading to suboptimal training signals. To address these limitations, we introduce Socratic-Zero, a fully autonomous framework that generates high-quality training data from minimal seed examples through the co-evolution of three agents: the Teacher, the Solver, and the Generator. The Solver continuously refines its reasoning by learning from preference feedback on both successful and failed trajectories; the Teacher adaptively crafts increasingly challenging questions based on the Solver's weaknesses; and the Generator distills the Teacher's question-design strategy to enable scalable, high-fidelity curriculum generation. This closed-loop system produces a self-improving curriculum-requiring no pre-existing tasks or labels. Remarkably, starting from only 100 seed questions, our Socratic-Solver-8B achieves an average gain of +20.2 percentage points over prior data synthesis methods across seven mathematical reasoning benchmarks (AMC23, AIME24-25, Olympiad, MATH-500, Minerva, and GSM8K), with consistent gains on both Qwen3 and GLM4 series models. Even more surprisingly, synthetic data from Socratic-Generator-32B enables student LLMs to achieve superior performance compared to other state-of-the-art (SOTA) commercial LLMs on these benchmarks, including Qwen3-235B-A22B, DeepSeek-V3.1-671B, GPT-5, Gemini-2.5-Pro, Grok-4, and Claude-4.1-Opus.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Socratic-Zero: Bootstrapping Reasoning via Data-Free Agent Co-Evolution" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Socratic-Zero, a novel framework for training reasoning abilities in large language models (LLMs) *without* relying on large, labeled datasets. This is achieved through the co-evolution of three agents:

*   **Solver:** An LLM trained to solve mathematical reasoning problems.
*   **Teacher:** A fixed LLM that evaluates the Solver's solutions, provides feedback, and designs progressively more challenging questions.
*   **Generator:** An LLM that learns from the Teacher's question-design strategy to create a scalable curriculum.

The Solver is trained using preference learning, and the Generator is trained to mimic the Teacher's question design. This closed-loop system iteratively improves the Solver's reasoning abilities. Starting from only 100 seed questions, the results show a gain of +20.2 percentage points over prior data synthesis methods across seven mathematical reasoning benchmarks. Interestingly, synthetic data from Socratic-Generator-32B enables smaller LLMs to outperform state-of-the-art commercial LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a fundamentally novel approach to training reasoning in LLMs. The co-evolutionary framework, combining preference learning, adaptive curriculum generation, and distillation, is a significant departure from traditional methods relying on large, static datasets or simple data synthesis. The use of a multi-agent system to drive self-improvement is a novel contribution. This is a departure from existing methods that create tasks to external agents. The framework's ability to function with minimal initial seed data is also highly noteworthy. The translation of the Socratic method into a concrete computational framework is quite clever.
*   **Significance:** The results presented are compelling. The performance gains achieved by Socratic-Zero, especially the ability of smaller models trained on synthetically generated data to outperform much larger, commercial models (GPT-5, Gemini, DeepSeek), suggests that this is more effective than pure data-scaling approaches. This could have a profound impact on the accessibility of high-quality reasoning capabilities, as it reduces the reliance on costly, large-scale data annotation efforts. It also suggests that the *quality* of the training data, as shaped by this co-evolutionary process, is more critical than simply the *quantity*.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The co-evolutionary framework is well-designed and justified.
    *   The experimental results are strong and clearly presented.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of different components.
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to understand.
    *   The code release is a significant step towards reproducibility and community adoption.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a Teacher model, even though it's fixed, still introduces some dependence on a pre-trained LLM. While the paper mitigates this by focusing on *distillation* of the Teacher's strategy, completely eliminating this dependency would make the framework even more powerful. It seems the framework is not entirely "data-free" since the teacher needs to be prompted.
    *   While the paper discusses potential avenues for scaling, the current results are limited to mathematical reasoning. Expanding the framework to other reasoning domains would further validate its general applicability.
    *   The theoretical foundations of the co-evolutionary process are not fully explored. While the paper notes the importance of understanding convergence properties, more theoretical analysis is needed to fully understand the long-term behavior of the system.
    *   The paper could benefit from a deeper discussion of the potential biases introduced by the Teacher model and how these biases might be propagated through the system.
    *   It seems that Teacher is using an LLM Judge to check if the student model solutions are right. It would be interesting to study how the LLM Judge accuracy would affect the overall results.

**Overall:**

This is a highly significant paper that presents a novel and effective approach to training reasoning abilities in LLMs. The results are impressive, and the framework has the potential to transform the field by reducing the reliance on large, labeled datasets. While there are some weaknesses, the strengths of the paper far outweigh them. The potential impact on the field is significant, particularly regarding the democratization of AI.

**Score: 8.5**

**Rationale for Score:** The paper presents a genuinely innovative approach with demonstrably strong results. It tackles a critical problem in LLM development (data dependency) with a clever solution. However, the remaining reliance on a Teacher model and the limited theoretical analysis prevent it from achieving a higher score. Further exploration of the theoretical convergence properties, the bias introduced by the Teacher, and applications to broader reasoning domains will solidify the framework's impact and could potentially justify a higher score in the future.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Diamonds in the rough: Transforming SPARCs of imagination into a game concept by leveraging medium sized LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24730v1)
- **Authors**: Julian Geheeb, Farhan Abid Ivan, Daniel Dyrda, Miriam Anschütz, Georg Groh
- **Abstract**: Recent research has demonstrated that large language models (LLMs) can support experts across various domains, including game design. In this study, we examine the utility of medium-sized LLMs, models that operate on consumer-grade hardware typically available in small studios or home environments. We began by identifying ten key aspects that contribute to a strong game concept and used ChatGPT to generate thirty sample game ideas. Three medium-sized LLMs, LLaMA 3.1, Qwen 2.5, and DeepSeek-R1, were then prompted to evaluate these ideas according to the previously identified aspects. A qualitative assessment by two researchers compared the models' outputs, revealing that DeepSeek-R1 produced the most consistently useful feedback, despite some variability in quality. To explore real-world applicability, we ran a pilot study with ten students enrolled in a storytelling course for game development. At the early stages of their own projects, students used our prompt and DeepSeek-R1 to refine their game concepts. The results indicate a positive reception: most participants rated the output as high quality and expressed interest in using such tools in their workflows. These findings suggest that current medium-sized LLMs can provide valuable feedback in early game design, though further refinement of prompting methods could improve consistency and overall effectiveness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the feasibility of using medium-sized Large Language Models (LLMs), runnable on consumer-grade hardware, to assist in the early conceptualization phase of game design. The authors identify ten key aspects of a strong game concept and use these as a framework for evaluating the performance of three medium-sized LLMs (LLaMA 3.1, Qwen 2.5, and DeepSeek-R1).  They generate a dataset of game ideas using ChatGPT, prompt the LLMs to evaluate these ideas based on the defined aspects, and compare the outputs through a qualitative human evaluation. The best-performing model, DeepSeek-R1, is then integrated into a prototype tool (SPARC) and tested in a pilot study with students involved in game development. The results suggest that medium-sized LLMs can provide valuable feedback at this stage, although prompt engineering needs further refinement to improve consistency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Relevance:** The paper addresses a pertinent challenge for indie developers and small studios: accessing AI-powered tools without relying on expensive cloud-based solutions. The focus on medium-sized LLMs and consumer hardware makes the research highly practical and accessible.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The research follows a clear and logical methodology, including the identification of key game concept aspects, the creation of a controlled dataset, model comparison, and a user study.
    *   **Pilot Study:**  The inclusion of a pilot study with real students working on game concepts strengthens the ecological validity of the findings. It demonstrates how these models could be applied in the real world.
    *   **Clear Identification of Limitations:** The authors are upfront about the limitations of the study, including the dataset bias, potential biases in the study design, and output quality variability.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in LLM Usage:** While applying LLMs to game design is growing, using them for automated evaluation is somewhat novel. The paper's novelty lies primarily in the focus on medium-sized models and the accessible hardware constraint, rather than a groundbreaking new AI technique.
    *   **Dependence on ChatGPT for Data Generation:** The reliance on ChatGPT to generate the game idea dataset introduces a potential bias. While the authors acknowledge this, it impacts the generalizability of the evaluation. Furthermore, the game ideas generated by ChatGPT are only as good as its training data, which could limit the creativity reflected in the generated concepts.
    *   **Modest Impact of User Study:** The results from the user study, while positive, show only a modest proportion of participants willing to immediately integrate the suggestions. This hints at the need for further refinement in the process.
    *   **Limited Scope of the User Study:** The user study only involved students and the duration of the study appears to be relatively short, which can affect the generalizability of the results.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper offers valuable insights into the potential of accessible LLMs for game design assistance.
    *   It provides a concrete framework for evaluating game concepts using AI.
    *   The findings can inform future research on prompt engineering and AI-assisted design tools, particularly in contexts where resources are limited.

*   **Overall:** The study provides some valuable insights into the ability of medium-sized LLMs in assisting in the early conceptualization phase of the game development cycle. However, there are limitations regarding the dataset design, scope of user testing and novelty.

Score: 6.5
**Rationale:**
The paper offers a practical application of LLMs in game design that considers accessibility constraints. The clear methodology and pilot study contribute to the credibility of the findings. However, the incremental nature of the AI contribution, dependence on ChatGPT, and mixed results from the user study limit the overall impact and justify a middling, slightly above-average score. The accessibility and realistic constraint settings of the research is compelling and the results can be used in future works.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### ProxyAttn: Guided Sparse Attention via Representative Heads
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24745v1)
- **Authors**: Yixuan Wang, Huang He, Siqi Bao, Hua Wu, Haifeng Wang, Qingfu Zhu, Wanxiang Che
- **Abstract**: The quadratic complexity of attention mechanisms limits the efficiency of Large Language Models (LLMs) on long-text tasks. Recently, methods that dynamically estimate block importance have enabled efficient block sparse attention, leading to significant acceleration in long-text pre-filling of LLMs. However, their coarse-grained estimation inevitably leads to performance degradation at high sparsity rates. In this work, we propose ProxyAttn, a training-free sparse attention algorithm that achieves more precise block estimation by compressing the dimension of attention heads. Based on our observation of the similarity among multiple attention heads, we use the scores of pooled representative heads to approximate the scores for all heads. To account for the varying sparsity among heads, we also propose a block-aware dynamic budget estimation method. By combining the scores from representative proxy heads with multi-head dynamic budgets, we achieve a more fine-grained block importance evaluation at low computational cost. Experiments on a variety of mainstream models and extensive benchmarks confirm the underlying similarity among attention heads. Leveraging a fine-grained estimation, the proposed method achieves substantial gains in performance and efficiency compared to existing methods. More precisely, ProxyAttn can achieve up to 10.3x attention acceleration and 2.4x prefilling acceleration without significant performance loss. Our code is available at https://github.com/wyxstriker/ProxyAttn.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ProxyAttn, a novel training-free sparse attention mechanism designed to improve the efficiency of Large Language Models (LLMs) on long-text tasks. The core idea is to approximate the attention scores of all attention heads by using the scores from a small set of representative (proxy) heads. This reduces computational complexity compared to calculating full attention. The method also includes a block-aware dynamic budget estimation to account for varying sparsity levels across different heads. The authors demonstrate through experiments on various models and benchmarks that ProxyAttn achieves significant acceleration in attention computation and prefilling without substantial performance loss compared to existing sparse attention methods. The paper claims improvements in both performance and efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel idea, ProxyAttn, that achieves a better fine-grained block estimation via head dimension compression. While the general concept of sparse attention and approximating full attention with subsets is not entirely new, the way ProxyAttn compresses across heads and dynamically allocates budgets presents a contribution. Existing methods focus more on sequence dimension compression, which is different from head dimension compression. The claim of better fine-grained block importance estimation is a key point of novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to improve the efficiency of LLMs on long-text tasks. Long-text is a significant bottleneck for transformer-based models due to quadratic attention complexity, so any method that can practically reduce this is of importance. The presented results showcase that ProxyAttn can accelerate the attention layer significantly with a small performance loss, making the method potentially valuable. The improvements in pre-filling are important, as that is a common and computationally intensive stage in LLM usage.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a clear explanation of the ProxyAttn algorithm and its motivation, supported by observations on the behavior of attention heads. The intuition behind using a few representative heads is reasonable and well-articulated.
    *   The integration of dynamic budget estimation is a valuable addition. Allowing different heads to have varying degrees of sparsity seems to address the variability in head behavior, as demonstrated in the experiments.
    *   The authors present comprehensive experiments across a variety of models (Llama, Qwen) and benchmarks (RULER, InfiniteBench, LongBench-v2). This provides strong empirical evidence for the method's effectiveness. The comparison to several strong baselines is good.
    *   The ablation studies and analyses (e.g., effect of proxy heads, impact of stride, dynamic budget effect) offer insights into how the different components of ProxyAttn contribute to overall performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper relies on empirical evaluation rather than theoretical proof. While empirical results are strong, a more theoretical analysis of the approximation error and the conditions under which ProxyAttn is most effective would strengthen the paper.
    *   The selection of proxy heads is based on simple pooling operations. While efficient, it might not always capture the most representative heads, especially for models without Grouped-Query attention. More advanced methods to determine proxy heads could potentially lead to greater improvements.
    *   The performance degradation is more pronounced as sparsity rates go up. While the proposed mechanism manages to maintain performance with higher sparsity rates compared to baseline implementations, at extreme sparsity the degradation could potentially render this mechanism less useful.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of efficient LLM inference. The idea of ProxyAttn is novel, and the experimental results are compelling. While the method lacks strong theoretical backing and could be further improved in terms of proxy head selection, the empirical gains in efficiency and the comprehensive evaluation make it a significant contribution to the field. It seems like it would be a fairly straightforward and useful method to implement for practitioners looking to increase inference speed.

### From Ambiguity to Verdict: A Semiotic-Grounded Multi-Perspective Agent for LLM Logical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24765v1)
- **Authors**: Yunyao Zhang, Xinglang Zhang, Junxi Sheng, Wenbing Li, Junqing Yu, Wei Yang, Zikai Song
- **Abstract**: Logical reasoning is a fundamental capability of large language models (LLMs). However, existing studies largely overlook the interplay between logical complexity and semantic complexity, resulting in methods that struggle to address challenging scenarios involving abstract propositions, ambiguous contexts, and conflicting stances, which are central to human reasoning. For this gap, we propose LogicAgent, a semiotic-square-guided framework designed to jointly address logical complexity and semantic complexity. LogicAgent explicitly performs multi-perspective deduction in first-order logic (FOL), while mitigating vacuous reasoning through existential import checks that incorporate a three-valued decision scheme (True, False, Uncertain) to handle boundary cases more faithfully. Furthermore, to overcome the semantic simplicity and low logical complexity of existing datasets, we introduce RepublicQA, a benchmark that reaches college-level difficulty (FKGL = 11.94) and exhibits substantially greater lexical and structural diversity than prior benchmarks. RepublicQA is grounded in philosophical concepts, featuring abstract propositions and systematically organized contrary and contradictory relations, making it the most semantically rich resource for evaluating logical reasoning. Experiments demonstrate that LogicAgent achieves state-of-the-art performance on RepublicQA, with a 6.25% average gain over strong baselines, and generalizes effectively to mainstream logical reasoning benchmarks including ProntoQA, ProofWriter, FOLIO, and ProverQA, achieving an additional 7.05% average gain. These results highlight the strong effectiveness of our semiotic-grounded multi-perspective reasoning in boosting LLMs' logical performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LogicAgent, a semiotic-square-guided framework designed to improve logical reasoning in large language models (LLMs). The framework explicitly performs multi-perspective deduction in first-order logic (FOL), addressing both logical and semantic complexity.  It employs a three-valued decision scheme (True, False, Uncertain) and incorporates existential import checks to mitigate vacuous reasoning. To address limitations in existing datasets, the authors introduce RepublicQA, a new benchmark grounded in philosophical concepts from Plato's Republic, featuring abstract propositions and systematically organized contrary and contradictory relations. Experiments demonstrate that LogicAgent achieves state-of-the-art performance on RepublicQA and generalizes effectively to other logical reasoning benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper exhibits a moderate level of novelty.  While existing works have explored combining LLMs with symbolic reasoning and using chain-of-thought prompting, the semiotic-square-guided framework for multi-perspective deduction in FOL is a distinctive contribution. The introduction of RepublicQA is a significant addition, addressing the lack of semantically complex benchmarks. However, the core idea of combining symbolic reasoning with LLMs isn't entirely new, and the FOL formalization, while sound, isn't a breakthrough in itself.  The main novelty lies in the specific combination and application of these elements.

**Significance:** The paper's significance is substantial, particularly in highlighting the importance of semantic complexity in logical reasoning.  Existing benchmarks often focus on logical structure but neglect the nuances of natural language and real-world scenarios. RepublicQA addresses this gap by providing a more challenging and realistic testbed. The improvements achieved by LogicAgent on both RepublicQA and other benchmarks suggest that the semiotic-square-guided framework is a promising approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **RepublicQA Benchmark:** The development of RepublicQA is a key strength. It's a well-motivated and carefully constructed benchmark that fills a significant gap in existing resources.
*   **Semiotics-Based Reasoning:** The use of semiotic squares to guide multi-perspective reasoning is a clever and insightful idea that helps to address ambiguity and conflicting stances.
*   **Generalization:** The framework's ability to generalize to other datasets like ProntoQA, ProofWriter, FOLIO, and ProverQA demonstrates its robustness and broad applicability.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a clear and detailed description of the LogicAgent framework, making it easy to understand and potentially replicate.
*   **Ablation Study:**  The ablation studies are thorough and informative, providing valuable insights into the contributions of each component.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity Threshold:** The results showing that, at times, shorter planning horizons work better suggests that more work is needed to understand the limits of the approach with reasoning chain lengths. The paper notes this, but more analysis could shed light on the ideal reasoning length.
*   **FOL Translation Errors:** Despite the relatively low frequency of FOL translation errors, the paper acknowledges they can propagate. More robust and accurate FOL translation methods might further enhance the framework.
*   **Hallucination in Verification:** The occasional hallucination during the verification phase is a concern, indicating that the framework is not foolproof and that further research is needed to improve its consistency. While rare, it undermines some of the benefits provided by the structure.
*   **Computational Overhead:** While the ablation studies are informative, the computational cost of LogicAgent compared to simpler baselines might be a limiting factor in certain applications.  The trade-off between accuracy and efficiency needs careful consideration.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's overall contribution is solid, though not groundbreaking. RepublicQA and the multi-perspective framework clearly address a relevant gap in the field of LLM reasoning.  The paper is well-written, the experiments are thorough, and the results are compelling. However, the aforementioned weakness around increased computational costs, limitations around planning, and hallucinations in verification limit its impact a bit. Therefore,
