### Large language models for behavioral modeling: A literature survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24782v1)
- **Authors**: Muhammad Laiq
- **Abstract**: In recent years, large language models (LLMs) have been extensively utilized for behavioral modeling, for example, to automatically generate sequence diagrams. However, no overview of this work has been published yet. Such an overview will help identify future research directions and inform practitioners and educators about the effectiveness of LLMs in assisting behavioral modeling. This study aims to provide an overview of the existing research on the use of LLMs for behavioral modeling, particularly focusing on use case and sequence diagrams. Through a term-based search, we filtered and identified 14 relevant primary studies. Our analysis of the selected primary studies reveals that LLMs have demonstrated promising results in automatically generating use case and sequence diagrams. In addition, we found that most of the current literature lacks expert-based evaluations and has mainly used GPT-based models. Therefore, future work should evaluate a broader range of LLMs for behavioral modeling and involve domain experts to evaluate the output of LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a literature survey on the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for behavioral modeling, specifically focusing on generating use case and sequence diagrams. The authors searched Google Scholar, identified 14 relevant primary studies, and analyzed them based on UML modeling tasks, evaluated LLMs, data sources, and evaluation approaches. The study found that LLMs show promise in automatically generating these diagrams, but there is a need for more comprehensive evaluations involving a wider range of LLMs (including open-source models) and more robust evaluation methods (including expert evaluations and automated consistency checks). The authors also discuss implications for educators and students, suggesting LLMs can lower learning barriers and improve outcomes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses a timely and relevant topic: the application of LLMs in software engineering, particularly behavioral modeling.
    *   The methodology is clearly described, following established guidelines for literature surveys. The search strategy, screening process, and data extraction template are all well-defined.
    *   The analysis provides a structured overview of the existing research, highlighting key trends and gaps in the literature.
    *   The discussion section identifies important areas for future research, such as the need for more diverse model evaluations and robust evaluation approaches.
    *   The implications for education are insightful and practical.
    *   The paper acknowledges and discusses potential validity threats.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The search was limited to Google Scholar and focused primarily on use case and sequence diagrams, potentially missing relevant research in other databases or related modeling techniques (e.g., state diagrams, activity diagrams). Also, the term "behavioral model" is a bit narrow.
    *   **Superficial Analysis:** While the paper provides a good overview, the analysis of the individual studies is somewhat superficial. It would have been beneficial to delve deeper into the specific techniques used, the challenges encountered, and the limitations of each study.
    *   **Lack of Critical Comparison:** The paper doesn't critically compare and contrast the different LLMs or evaluation approaches. It mostly presents descriptive statistics without drawing strong conclusions about their relative strengths and weaknesses. The comparative power of LLMs is not clear here.
    *   **Search Performed in 2025:** The paper is submitted in 2025, but it includes forward-looking statements about research that would be performed in the future. This is jarring and reduces the credibility of the literature review.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper contributes to the field by providing a first overview of LLMs used in generating UML behavioural diagrams.
    *   The identification of gaps in the existing research and the suggestions for future work are valuable for researchers in this area.
    *   The implications for educators and students are helpful for those who are interested in integrating LLMs into their teaching practices.
    *   However, the contribution is incremental rather than groundbreaking, since it's primarily a synthesis of existing work. A more in-depth analysis or a new framework for evaluating LLMs in behavioral modelling would have significantly increased the novelty.

*   **Impact on the Field:** The paper will likely be useful for researchers entering the field of LLMs for behavioral modeling. It provides a good starting point for understanding the current state of the art and identifying promising research directions.

* **Score Rationale:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, a score of 6 reflects that the paper is a valuable contribution to the literature in behavioural modelling, but it lacks the depth and breadth to be considered truly exceptional. The paper offers a useful overview and highlights important gaps, but a more critical and comparative analysis could have elevated its impact. The limited scope, superficial analysis, and lack of critical comparison keep the score from being higher.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Vision Function Layer in Multimodal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24791v1)
- **Authors**: Cheng Shi, Yizhou Yu, Sibei Yang
- **Abstract**: This study identifies that visual-related functional decoding is distributed across different decoder layers in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Typically, each function, such as counting, grounding, or OCR recognition, narrows down to two or three layers, which we define as Vision Function Layers (VFL). Additionally, the depth and its order of different VFLs exhibits a consistent pattern across different MLLMs, which is well-aligned with human behaviors (e.g., recognition occurs first, followed by counting, and then grounding). These findings are derived from Visual Token Swapping, our novel analytical framework that modifies targeted KV cache entries to precisely elucidate layer-specific functions during decoding. Furthermore, these insights offer substantial utility in tailoring MLLMs for real-world downstream applications. For instance, when LoRA training is selectively applied to VFLs whose functions align with the training data, VFL-LoRA not only outperform full-LoRA but also prevent out-of-domain function forgetting. Moreover, by analyzing the performance differential on training data when particular VFLs are ablated, VFL-select automatically classifies data by function, enabling highly efficient data selection to directly bolster corresponding capabilities. Consequently, VFL-select surpasses human experts in data selection, and achieves 98% of full-data performance with only 20% of the original dataset. This study delivers deeper comprehension of MLLM visual processing, fostering the creation of more efficient, interpretable, and robust models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the internal workings of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), specifically focusing on how visual information is processed across different decoder layers. The authors introduce a novel analytical framework called "Visual Token Swapping" to identify "Vision Function Layers" (VFLs), which are specific layer blocks where distinct visual functions (e.g., counting, grounding, OCR) are executed. The paper reveals that VFLs exhibit a consistent arrangement across different MLLMs, aligning with human cognitive processes. Furthermore, the authors demonstrate the utility of these insights by developing "VFL-LoRA" (a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method targeting VFLs) and a "VFL-select" data selection strategy, showcasing improved performance and efficiency compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the method (Visual Token Swapping) and subsequent analysis of MLLM decoder layers to pinpoint VFLs.  Previous work has explored token importance and cross-modal interactions, but this paper goes further in isolating the *functional role* of specific layers in visual processing.  The discovery of a consistent hierarchical arrangement of VFLs across different MLLM architectures is also a significant and somewhat surprising finding. The application of these insights to VFL-LoRA and VFL-select adds practical value, albeit these applications are incremental improvements on existing techniques.
* **Significance:** Understanding the internal mechanisms of MLLMs is crucial for creating more efficient, interpretable, and robust models.  The paper addresses a critical gap in understanding *how* visual tokens are processed within MLLMs, going beyond just knowing *which* tokens are important. The proposed analytical framework and the resulting insights could potentially influence the design of future MLLM architectures. The practical implications are noteworthy, as VFL-LoRA and VFL-select demonstrate ways to improve performance and efficiency, which are increasingly important as models grow larger and more complex. The implications on model interpretability, enabling a user to look inside the "black box" of MLLMs' vision processing are particularly notable.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Methodology:** Visual Token Swapping is a well-designed approach to isolate the function of individual layers.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper provides extensive experimental results across multiple MLLM architectures and tasks.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The proposed VFL-LoRA and VFL-select are compelling examples of how the findings can be used to improve MLLMs.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the methods and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Applications:** While VFL-LoRA and VFL-select are effective, they are essentially targeted applications of existing fine-tuning and data selection techniques. The true significance stems more strongly from understanding the models better than from simply creating new techniques, and understanding will persist even if the specific techniques fade.
    *   **Limited scope of Vision Functions:** The paper focuses primarily on a limited set of visual functions (counting, grounding, OCR, and recognition). A broader exploration of visual functions could further strengthen the findings.
    *   **Benchmarking Scope:** While various benchmarks are explored, some are fairly targeted (e.g., the tailored image pairs). Showing broader performance and generality on more diverse tasks (e.g., from the long tail of possible image/text queries) could further strengthen the findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the design of future MLLMs by encouraging a more modular and targeted approach to visual processing. The proposed analytical framework could be used to further investigate the internal workings of MLLMs and to develop new methods for improving their performance and efficiency.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and insightful analysis of the internal workings of MLLMs, leading to a greater understanding of the layers involved in visual processing. This work could have a significant impact on MLLM design and optimization. VFL-LoRA and VFL-Select add some value. While the practical applications are somewhat incremental improvements on existing methods, the novel understanding may lead to more significant breakthroughs. The paper merits a score of 7.5, as it provides a strong contribution to the field with the potential for broader influence.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Causal-Adapter: Taming Text-to-Image Diffusion for Faithful Counterfactual Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24798v1)
- **Authors**: Lei Tong, Zhihua Liu, Chaochao Lu, Dino Oglic, Tom Diethe, Philip Teare, Sotirios A. Tsaftaris, Chen Jin
- **Abstract**: We present Causal-Adapter, a modular framework that adapts frozen text-to-image diffusion backbones for counterfactual image generation. Our method enables causal interventions on target attributes, consistently propagating their effects to causal dependents without altering the core identity of the image. In contrast to prior approaches that rely on prompt engineering without explicit causal structure, Causal-Adapter leverages structural causal modeling augmented with two attribute regularization strategies: prompt-aligned injection, which aligns causal attributes with textual embeddings for precise semantic control, and a conditioned token contrastive loss to disentangle attribute factors and reduce spurious correlations. Causal-Adapter achieves state-of-the-art performance on both synthetic and real-world datasets, with up to 91\% MAE reduction on Pendulum for accurate attribute control and 87\% FID reduction on ADNI for high-fidelity MRI image generation. These results show that our approach enables robust, generalizable counterfactual editing with faithful attribute modification and strong identity preservation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Causal-Adapter, a novel modular framework for counterfactual image generation using text-to-image diffusion models. Unlike previous methods that rely heavily on prompt engineering or retraining entire models, Causal-Adapter injects causal semantic attributes into a frozen diffusion backbone via a learnable adapter.  The core innovation lies in two attribute regularization strategies: Prompt-Aligned Injection (PAI) aligns causal attributes with textual embeddings for precise control, while Conditioned Token Contrastive loss (CTC) disentangles attribute factors and reduces spurious correlations.  The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on synthetic and real-world datasets, showing improved attribute control, fidelity, and identity preservation compared to existing approaches.  The framework is modular, adaptable, and requires minimal fine-tuning, making it a practical solution for various counterfactual editing tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of counterfactual image generation by addressing key limitations of existing methods. Here's a detailed breakdown:

*   **Novelty:** The combination of a modular adapter network with explicit causal modeling and attribute regularization strategies (PAI and CTC) is a significant innovation.  While previous works have explored causal diffusion models, the approach of injecting semantic information into a frozen diffusion backbone via a pluggable adapter and the specific design of PAI and CTC losses are novel. Furthermore, Causal-Adapter enables precise numeric control of diffusion models in the way prompt engineering struggles with. The modular design also allows easy adaptation to different backbone diffusion models and causal graphs.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in counterfactual image generation: ensuring faithful attribute modification and strong identity preservation while avoiding spurious correlations. By explicitly modeling causal relationships and using regularization techniques, Causal-Adapter produces more reliable and interpretable edits than previous methods. This has significant implications for applications such as medical image simulation and fairness-aware data augmentation. The modular nature and minimal fine-tuning requirements makes Causal-Adapter a potentially widely adopted solution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results on diverse datasets (Pendulum, CelebA, ADNI), demonstrating state-of-the-art performance across various metrics.
    *   **Comprehensive ablation study:**  The ablation study clearly demonstrates the effectiveness of the PAI and CTC regularization strategies.
    *   **Qualitative results:** The qualitative results show compelling visual comparisons, showcasing the superior attribute control and identity preservation of Causal-Adapter.
    *   **Modularity:** The modular design makes it easy to adapt to different diffusion models and causal graphs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope of causal graphs:** The paper focuses on relatively simple causal graphs. While the framework is theoretically applicable to more complex graphs, the practical challenges of learning and representing complex causal relationships in high-dimensional image spaces are not fully addressed.
    *   **Dependence on pretrained models:** The method relies on a pretrained diffusion backbone, limiting its flexibility in adapting to novel domains or modalities where such pretrained models are not available. However, that can also be viewed as a design decision and strength.
    *   **Potential for misuse:** As with any generative model, there is a potential for misuse, such as generating deepfakes or manipulating medical images for malicious purposes.
    *   **Limited out-of-distribution generalization analysis:** The study includes some OOD evaluation related to female faces, however, more comprehensive analyses of general OOD performance are not made.

*   **Potential influence on the field:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of counterfactual image generation. The modular design, strong empirical results, and clear explanations make it a valuable contribution that can be built upon by other researchers. The concepts of PAI and CTC could be adapted for use in other generative models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a strong contribution with significant novelty and clear empirical validation. The method's modularity, the effective attribute regularization, and demonstrated performance advantages on several datasets justify a high score. The limitations are clearly stated and provide directions for future research. Overall, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, suggesting that this paper will have a tangible impact on the field.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DSAT-HD: Dual-Stream Adaptive Transformer with Hybrid Decomposition for Multivariate Time Series Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24800v1)
- **Authors**: Zixu Wang, Hongbin Dong, Xiaoping Zhang
- **Abstract**: Time series forecasting is crucial for various applications, such as weather, traffic, electricity, and energy predictions. Currently, common time series forecasting methods are based on Transformers. However, existing approaches primarily model limited time series or fixed scales, making it more challenging to capture diverse features cross different ranges. Additionally, traditional methods like STL for complex seasonality-trend decomposition require pre-specified seasonal periods and typically handle only single, fixed seasonality. We propose the Hybrid Decomposition Dual-Stream Adaptive Transformer (DSAT-HD), which integrates three key innovations to address the limitations of existing methods: 1) A hybrid decomposition mechanism combining EMA and Fourier decomposition with RevIN normalization, dynamically balancing seasonal and trend components through noise Top-k gating; 2) A multi-scale adaptive pathway leveraging a sparse allocator to route features to four parallel Transformer layers, followed by feature merging via a sparse combiner, enhanced by hybrid attention combining local CNNs and global interactions; 3) A dual-stream residual learning framework where CNN and MLP branches separately process seasonal and trend components, coordinated by a balanced loss function minimizing expert collaboration variance. Extensive experiments on nine datasets demonstrate that DSAT-HD outperforms existing methods overall and achieves state-of-the-art performance on some datasets. Notably, it also exhibits stronger generalization capabilities across various transfer scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DSAT-HD: Dual-Stream Adaptive Transformer with Hybrid Decomposition for Multivariate Time Series Forecasting."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DSAT-HD, a novel Transformer-based architecture for multivariate time series forecasting. It addresses limitations of existing methods by incorporating three key innovations: (1) a hybrid decomposition mechanism combining EMA and Fourier decomposition with RevIN normalization and noisy Top-k gating for dynamic seasonal and trend component balancing; (2) a multi-scale adaptive pathway utilizing a sparse allocator for feature routing to parallel Transformer layers, enhanced by hybrid attention combining local CNNs and global interactions; and (3) a dual-stream residual learning framework with CNN and MLP branches processing seasonal and trend components separately, coordinated by a balanced loss function. Experiments on nine datasets demonstrate that DSAT-HD outperforms existing methods and achieves state-of-the-art performance on some datasets, also exhibiting stronger generalization capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper combines several techniques in a novel way. The hybrid decomposition with EMA, Fourier transforms and the noisy Top-K gating mechanism appears to be a significant contribution. The use of the sparse allocator and multi-scale experts in the adaptive pathway is also creative and aimed at addressing multi-scale time series data. The dual-stream design and balanced loss are appropriate for handling the separated trend and seasonal components.
    *   **Completeness and Rigor:** The experimental setup is comprehensive, using nine datasets, including ETT, and covering various forecast horizons. Comparisons are made against strong baselines, including recently published Transformer variants and other SOTA methods. The ablation studies are valuable in dissecting the contributions of individual components.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the architecture and its components, making it easier to follow the technical details.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Advance:** While the combination of techniques is novel, the individual components themselves are based on existing concepts. It's a sophisticated and thoughtful amalgamation, but not fundamentally disruptive in its approach. It would be more novel if it introduces a new layer or attention mechanism.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the architecture is motivated by practical considerations (e.g., handling multi-scale data, separating trend and seasonality), there isn't a strong theoretical justification for the specific design choices. The paper relies more on empirical evidence to demonstrate the efficacy of the approach.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture is relatively complex, involving multiple components and hyperparameters. This complexity may make it more difficult to implement and tune in practice. The benefits from the improved performance may be outweighed by the need to invest a great deal more computational resources.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Although the authors stated their code is available, there is no link to it yet.
*   **Significance:**
    *   The results presented are competitive and show improvement over the baselines on several datasets.
    *   The ablations offer useful insights into the contributions of each component, which can inform future research.
    *   The paper addresses a practical problem in time series forecasting (handling multi-scale data, non-stationarity) and provides a solution that is demonstrated to be effective.

**Justification:**

DSAT-HD represents a solid engineering contribution to the field of time series forecasting. It effectively combines and adapts existing techniques to address real-world challenges, demonstrating improved performance on a range of datasets. However, its incremental nature and lack of strong theoretical justification prevent it from being considered a groundbreaking contribution. It is a well-executed approach, but other papers introduce much more novel concepts that might not perform as well on several datasets. The complexity and lack of open-source code slightly diminishes its overall impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### TimeOmni-1: Incentivizing Complex Reasoning with Time Series in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24803v1)
- **Authors**: Tong Guan, Zijie Meng, Dianqi Li, Shiyu Wang, Chao-Han Huck Yang, Qingsong Wen, Zuozhu Liu, Sabato Marco Siniscalchi, Ming Jin, Shirui Pan
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in multimodal time series learning underscore a paradigm shift from analytics centered on basic patterns toward advanced time series understanding and reasoning. However, existing multimodal time series datasets mostly remain at the level of surface alignment and question answering, without reaching the depth of genuine reasoning. The absence of well-defined tasks that genuinely require time series reasoning, along with the scarcity of high-quality data, has limited progress in building practical time series reasoning models (TSRMs). To this end, we introduce Time Series Reasoning Suite (TSR-Suite), which formalizes four atomic tasks that span three fundamental capabilities for reasoning with time series: (1) perception, acquired through scenario understanding and causality discovery; (2) extrapolation, realized via event-aware forecasting; and (3) decision-making, developed through deliberation over perception and extrapolation. TSR-Suite is the first comprehensive time series reasoning suite that supports not only thorough evaluation but also the data pipeline and training of TSRMs. It contains more than 23K samples, of which 2.3K are carefully curated through a human-guided hierarchical annotation process. Building on this foundation, we introduce TimeOmni-1, the first unified reasoning model designed to address diverse real-world problems demanding time series reasoning. The model is trained in multiple stages, integrating a mixture of task scenarios, novel reward functions, and tailored optimizations. Experiments show that TimeOmni-1 delivers strong out-of-distribution generalization across all tasks and achieves a high rate of valid responses. It significantly improves causality discovery accuracy (64.0% vs. 35.9% with GPT-4.1) and raises the valid response rate by over 6% compared to GPT-4.1 on the event-aware forecasting task.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TIMEOMNI-1: INCENTIVIZING COMPLEX REASONING WITH TIME SERIES IN LARGE LANGUAGE MODELS":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TIMEOMNI-1, a new unified reasoning model for time series data built upon Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors address the scarcity of high-quality data and benchmarks that genuinely require time series reasoning by creating a new suite called TSR-SUITE. This suite formalizes four atomic tasks spanning perception, extrapolation, and decision-making. TIMEOMNI-1 is trained in two stages: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to inject temporal priors and reinforcement learning (RL) with task-grounded rewards to enhance reasoning beyond simple mimicry. Experiments demonstrate strong out-of-distribution generalization and improved performance compared to other LLMs, particularly in causal discovery and event-aware forecasting. The authors also highlight the benefits of joint training across different time series reasoning tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Dataset and Benchmark:** TSR-SUITE is a significant contribution. The lack of comprehensive, high-quality datasets for time series reasoning has been a major bottleneck. The human-guided hierarchical annotation process adds value, addressing limitations in existing datasets like Time-MQA.
*   **Unified Reasoning Model:** TIMEOMNI-1 is the first unified model that tackles a broad range of time series reasoning tasks. The two-stage training approach (SFT followed by RL) is well-motivated, aiming to overcome the limitations of LLMs, which often lack the necessary temporal priors.
*   **Out-of-Distribution Generalization:** The results demonstrating strong OOD performance are impressive. This suggests that TIMEOMNI-1 is learning genuine reasoning capabilities rather than simply overfitting to the training data.
*   **Significant Performance Gains:** The substantial improvements over GPT-4.1 in causal discovery are noteworthy, indicating the effectiveness of the proposed approach for complex reasoning tasks.
*   **Joint Training Benefits:** The analysis of joint training benefits provides valuable insights into how different time series reasoning capabilities can be synergistically learned.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a detailed explanation of the methodology, including the data collection process, task formulation, training procedure, and evaluation metrics. This transparency enhances the reproducibility of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The paper does not explicitly address the computational cost associated with training and deploying TIMEOMNI-1. Given that it uses LLMs and reinforcement learning, the resources required could be substantial. This needs to be more clearly highlighted.
*   **Evaluation Metric Choice:** While the paper reports standard metrics like accuracy (ACC) and MAE, it does not extensively explore additional metrics like calibration or uncertainty estimation. It's essential to understand how TIMEOMNI-1 handles uncertainty in its predictions, especially in critical applications.
*   **Reliance on LLMs:** While leveraging LLMs is a reasonable starting point, the approach is fundamentally limited by their inherent weaknesses. It's unclear how far this approach can be pushed before specialized architectures for time series are needed.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper is novel because it introduces a new dataset suite (TSR-SUITE) specifically designed for complex time series reasoning. It's also significant because it pioneers a unified model that can handle diverse reasoning tasks within the time series domain. TIMEOMNI-1 demonstrates that LLMs can be adapted for time series reasoning through appropriate training and task formulation.

**Potential Influence:**

This work has the potential to influence several areas:

*   **Time series research:** It could spur the development of new time series reasoning models and benchmarks.
*   **LLM applications:** It demonstrates how LLMs can be applied to domains beyond text and code, opening up new possibilities for multimodal reasoning.
*   **Real-world applications:** Improved time series reasoning could have a significant impact on areas such as energy management, finance, and healthcare.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5** to this paper.

*   The creation of TSR-SUITE and TIMEOMNI-1 represent a strong and valuable contribution to time series research. The improvements over GPT-4.1 are significant. The two-stage training process, and investigation of the benefits of joint training is valuable.
*   While the paper is well-executed, several weaknesses temper the assessment. The lack of discussion regarding computational costs is a concern. The dependence on a powerful LLM like GPT-4.1 may limit the portability of the approach to resource-constrained settings. The work needs to make a bigger, more substantial leap forward.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### KnowGuard: Knowledge-Driven Abstention for Multi-Round Clinical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24816v1)
- **Authors**: Xilin Dang, Kexin Chen, Xiaorui Su, Ayush Noori, Iñaki Arango, Lucas Vittor, Xinyi Long, Yuyang Du, Marinka Zitnik, Pheng Ann Heng
- **Abstract**: In clinical practice, physicians refrain from making decisions when patient information is insufficient. This behavior, known as abstention, is a critical safety mechanism preventing potentially harmful misdiagnoses. Recent investigations have reported the application of large language models (LLMs) in medical scenarios. However, existing LLMs struggle with the abstentions, frequently providing overconfident responses despite incomplete information. This limitation stems from conventional abstention methods relying solely on model self-assessments, which lack systematic strategies to identify knowledge boundaries with external medical evidences. To address this, we propose \textbf{KnowGuard}, a novel \textit{investigate-before-abstain} paradigm that integrates systematic knowledge graph exploration for clinical decision-making. Our approach consists of two key stages operating on a shared contextualized evidence pool: 1) an evidence discovery stage that systematically explores the medical knowledge space through graph expansion and direct retrieval, and 2) an evidence evaluation stage that ranks evidence using multiple factors to adapt exploration based on patient context and conversation history. This two-stage approach enables systematic knowledge graph exploration, allowing models to trace structured reasoning paths and recognize insufficient medical evidence. We evaluate our abstention approach using open-ended multi-round clinical benchmarks that mimic realistic diagnostic scenarios, assessing abstention quality through accuracy-efficiency trade-offs beyond existing closed-form evaluations. Experimental evidences clearly demonstrate that KnowGuard outperforms state-of-the-art abstention approaches, improving diagnostic accuracy by 3.93\% while reducing unnecessary interaction by 7.27 turns on average.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KnowGuard, a novel "investigate-before-abstain" approach for improving the abstention capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in multi-round clinical reasoning. Unlike traditional methods that rely on LLM self-assessments, KnowGuard integrates systematic knowledge graph exploration to identify knowledge gaps and guide targeted investigation before making abstention decisions. It consists of two stages: 1) Evidence Discovery, which expands a contextualized evidence pool through graph expansion and direct retrieval, and 2) Evidence Evaluation, which ranks evidence based on graph coherence, embedding similarity, and other factors, adapting exploration to the patient's context.  The method is evaluated on a newly constructed open-ended, multi-round clinical benchmark against several baselines and demonstrates improved diagnostic accuracy and reduced unnecessary interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The "investigate-before-abstain" paradigm is a significant departure from conventional abstention methods. Systematically exploring knowledge boundaries *before* making a decision is a much more robust strategy, especially in high-stakes domains like clinical decision-making. Integrating knowledge graph exploration for this purpose also seems like a sound choice given its ability to present organized medical information. The paper's emphasis on multi-round reasoning in an open-ended format better reflects real-world clinical practice compared to multiple-choice datasets. The open-ended experimental setup is also important as it more realistically captures the challenge of abstention.
*   **Significance:** The limitations of LLMs in clinical settings, particularly their overconfidence and tendency to make premature decisions, are well-recognized. The paper addresses a crucial safety aspect by improving the abstention behavior of LLMs. The performance gains demonstrated are practically relevant because they improve accuracy while reducing interaction time. The method is also explainable as it traces back to structured reasoning in a knowledge graph. Given that medical diagnosis and AI have both become prevalent fields in research, this integration has the potential for significant impact.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework is well-motivated and addresses a critical limitation of LLMs in clinical applications.
    *   The design of KnowGuard is innovative and well-engineered, combining graph exploration, retrieval, and evidence evaluation effectively.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, including a new benchmark and extensive comparisons with various baselines. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology and results.
    * The paper recognizes the limitations of LLMs in clinical settings, particularly their overconfidence and premature decisions. KnowGuard provides a method to work around this.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The knowledge graph creation relies on WHO guidelines. This may limit generalizability or introduce biases depending on the completeness and scope of these guidelines.
    *   While the paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments, further analysis of failure cases and error modes would strengthen the evaluation. For instance, are there specific types of clinical cases where KnowGuard struggles?
    *   The computational overhead of the knowledge graph exploration might be a concern in real-time clinical settings. The paper could benefit from a discussion of efficiency and scalability.
    * The study only uses GPT-4 as the core LLM. Using more LLMs would test the robustness of the study.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research in clinical AI, particularly on topics related to safe and reliable AI systems. The idea of "investigate-before-abstain" can be extended to other high-stakes domains. The multi-round, open-ended benchmark will be valuable for evaluating future abstention methods.
*   **Rigorous rationale:** The paper presents a strong advancement by addressing a key limitation in LLMs when applied to multi-round diagnostic scenarios. Its systematic approach for knowledge investigation coupled with the construction of a novel benchmark makes it a valuable contribution. There are, however, concerns with the limitation to one LLM model and the computational overhead of knowledge graph exploration.

**Score: 8**

The paper presents a significant contribution with its novel approach and clear demonstration of performance improvements. The work is well-motivated and thoroughly evaluated. Though there are minor weaknesses related to generalizability and computational complexity, the paper represents a substantial advancement in the field of clinical AI and abstention methods.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Of-SemWat: High-payload text embedding for semantic watermarking of AI-generated images with arbitrary size
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24823v1)
- **Authors**: Benedetta Tondi, Andrea Costanzo, Mauro Barni
- **Abstract**: We propose a high-payload image watermarking method for textual embedding, where a semantic description of the image - which may also correspond to the input text prompt-, is embedded inside the image. In order to be able to robustly embed high payloads in large-scale images - such as those produced by modern AI generators - the proposed approach builds upon a traditional watermarking scheme that exploits orthogonal and turbo codes for improved robustness, and integrates frequency-domain embedding and perceptual masking techniques to enhance watermark imperceptibility. Experiments show that the proposed method is extremely robust against a wide variety of image processing, and the embedded text can be retrieved also after traditional and AI inpainting, permitting to unveil the semantic modification the image has undergone via image-text mismatch analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "OF-SemWat: High-Payload Text Embedding for Semantic Watermarking of AI-Generated Images with Arbitrary Size" proposes a post-generation watermarking method to embed a textual semantic description (or prompt) into AI-generated images of any size. The method builds upon a traditional, robust watermarking technique incorporating orthogonal and turbo codes, frequency-domain embedding, and perceptual masking.  The key novelty is the high payload capacity, enabling the embedding of relatively long textual descriptions. The watermark is robust against common image processing operations and even AI-based inpainting, allowing for image-text mismatch analysis to detect semantic alterations. An LLM is optionally used to correct errors in the extracted text.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **High Payload Capacity:** The ability to embed large amounts of text in high-resolution images is a significant advantage over many existing post-generation watermarking techniques. This is crucial for conveying richer semantic information about the image.
    *   **Robustness:** The paper demonstrates strong robustness against various image processing operations, including JPEG compression, noise addition, resizing, rotation, and cropping. The robustness to AI-based inpainting is also noteworthy, as it allows detecting manipulations.
    *   **Semantic Watermarking Framework:**  Using the watermark to embed semantic information and enable image-text consistency checks is a valuable application. This can be crucial for detecting and understanding semantic modifications of AI-generated content.
    *   **Flexibility:** The method is flexible, working with images of arbitrary sizes and allowing for adjustments to the trade-off between robustness and payload capacity.
    *   **Publicly Available Code:** The authors have made their implementation publicly available, which is crucial for reproducibility and adoption by other researchers.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Traditional Techniques:** The core watermarking technique is based on well-established methods (orthogonal codes, turbo codes, frequency domain embedding). The novelty lies primarily in applying these techniques in a specific context (AI-generated images, high payload, semantic watermarking). The 'OF' (Old Fashioned) in the method's name highlights the reliance on well known techniques, which means the originality aspect must be carefully considered.
    *   **Computational Complexity:** While not explicitly discussed, frequency-domain embedding can be computationally expensive, especially for very large images. The paper does not offer information about the computation time.
    *   **LLM Dependency for High Error Rates:** The reliance on an LLM for error correction suggests that the raw watermark extraction may not be entirely robust in certain scenarios. This adds complexity and potentially introduces biases associated with the LLM. The paper should explore if there are techniques that could be implemented in the original scheme to improve extraction of the signal without the need to utilize an LLM.
    *   **Limited Comparison to State-of-the-Art:** The paper argues that direct comparison to other post-generation methods is difficult due to size and payload limitations of those methods. However, the lack of a more quantitative comparison makes it harder to assess the performance gains relative to the field. An indirect comparison, or theoretical analysis on efficiency vs. other techniques, would have been valuable.
    *   **Limited Diversity of AI Models:** Experiments are carried out with AI-generated images from Stable Diffusion (SD)-XL, FLUX.1-dev and Mystic, therefore the research would have benefited from further experimentation with a broader set of AI image generators.

*   **Novelty:** The novelty is moderate. While the core watermarking technique isn't entirely new, the application to large-scale AI-generated images, the focus on high payload capacity for semantic embedding, and the integration with image-text mismatch analysis contribute to a valuable advancement in the field.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem: how to detect semantic manipulations of AI-generated images. The ability to embed and retrieve semantic descriptions robustly has significant implications for content provenance, authentication, and detection of misinformation. The approach offers a practical tool for combating the misuse of AI-generated media.

*   **Impact:** The potential impact is considerable, especially considering the growing concern over deepfakes and manipulated content. The method provides a means to track and verify the authenticity of AI-generated images, potentially helping to mitigate the spread of misinformation. Furthermore, other researchers can build upon this work to further improve the robustness, efficiency, and stealth of semantic watermarking techniques.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a valuable and practical solution for semantic watermarking of AI-generated images, addressing a timely and relevant problem. The high payload capacity and robustness are significant strengths. However, the incremental nature of the core watermarking technique, the LLM dependency, and the limited comparison to existing methods hold back the score. While the approach is not revolutionary, it is a solid and significant contribution with potential for real-world impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### AIPOM: Agent-aware Interactive Planning for Multi-Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24826v1)
- **Authors**: Hannah Kim, Kushan Mitra, Chen Shen, Dan Zhang, Estevam Hruschka
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are being increasingly used for planning in orchestrated multi-agent systems. However, existing LLM-based approaches often fall short of human expectations and, critically, lack effective mechanisms for users to inspect, understand, and control their behaviors. These limitations call for enhanced transparency, controllability, and human oversight. To address this, we introduce AIPOM, a system supporting human-in-the-loop planning through conversational and graph-based interfaces. AIPOM enables users to transparently inspect, refine, and collaboratively guide LLM-generated plans, significantly enhancing user control and trust in multi-agent workflows. Our code and demo video are available at https://github.com/megagonlabs/aipom.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AIPOM, an "Agent-aware Interactive Planning for Multi-Agent Systems" system. AIPOM aims to improve transparency, controllability, and human oversight in orchestrated multi-agent systems (OMAS) by providing users with both a conversational (chat panel) and a graphical (plan panel) interface. This allows users to inspect, refine, and collaboratively guide LLM-generated plans.  Users can interact with the plan as a DAG (Directed Acyclic Graph), modifying task descriptions, agent assignments, data flow, and execution order. The system uses a mixed-initiative planning approach, enabling flexible collaboration between humans and LLMs. The paper includes usage scenarios, a quantitative experiment evaluating plan refinement performance, and a pilot study comparing different plan representation formats and feedback modalities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of a conversational interface with a graphical interface for OMAS planning is a key novel aspect. While visual workflow tools and LLM-based planners exist separately, AIPOM's integration specifically targets enhanced human-in-the-loop control over agent-based plans. The ability to directly manipulate plan structures (nodes, edges, agents, inputs/outputs) within a graph environment alongside LLM assistance offers a unique level of interaction.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in the deployment of LLM-powered OMAS: the lack of transparency and controllability. As OMAS become more complex and are applied in higher-stakes scenarios, the ability for humans to understand and influence the planning process is essential for trust and safety. AIPOM provides a pathway to address these issues.  The pilot study indicates improved user understanding and preference for graph-based editing.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of current LLM-based planning approaches in OMAS.
    *   **Well-Defined System:** The AIPOM system is described with sufficient detail, including its modules (planning, conversation, execution coordinator, controller) and implementation choices.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The inclusion of quantitative experiments and a pilot user study provides evidence of AIPOM's effectiveness. The experiment on plan refinement operations is well designed, using meaningful metrics like execution accuracy, isomorphic subgraph match, and graph edit distance. The pilot study explores how different plan representations (text vs. graph) and feedback modalities affect user performance and preferences.
    *   **Practical Application:** The usage scenarios illustrate how AIPOM can be applied to real-world tasks like job searching and problem-solving.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of User Study:** The user study involves a small number of participants (9), all from a research lab environment. This limits the generalizability of the findings to a broader user population.
    *   **Task Domain:** The quantitative experiments focus solely on math word problems. While this allows for easier evaluation, it doesn't demonstrate AIPOM's effectiveness across diverse task domains where OMAS might be applied (e.g., healthcare, finance, logistics).
    *   **Agent Implementation:** The paper mentions that the agents used in the simulations are specialized agents implemented with python functions, APIs and LLMs, but it would have been beneficial to elaborate on the details of what APIs and LLMs were used.
    *   **Limited Comparison to Existing Tools:** While the related work section mentions existing visual workflow and LLM-based systems, the evaluation does not include a direct comparison to these tools. A benchmark against alternative approaches would strengthen the paper's claims of superiority.
    *   **Scaling Challenges are unaddressed:** The paper describes how structural constrains will be added, but doesn't specify if scaling will be addressed.

*   **Potential Influence:**  AIPOM has the potential to influence the design of future human-AI collaboration tools for OMAS.  It provides a concrete example of how to combine conversational and visual interfaces to enhance transparency and control. It also provides insights into the types of interactions users find most effective for guiding LLM-based planners.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7/10. The paper presents a well-defined system that addresses an important problem in LLM-based planning. The combination of conversational and graphical interfaces for enhanced control is novel and promising. The empirical evaluation, while limited in scope, provides initial evidence of AIPOM's effectiveness. However, the small user study, limited task domain, and lack of comparison to existing tools reduce the overall impact of the work. A larger, more diverse user study across a wider range of application domains would be necessary to significantly increase the paper's significance. It would have also been beneficial to provide details of the agents employed in the simulations, and discuss how these agents were implemented.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### SemShareKV: Efficient KVCache Sharing for Semantically Similar Prompts via Token-Level LSH Matching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24832v1)
- **Authors**: Xinye Zhao, Spyridon Mastorakis
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) continue to scale, the memory footprint of key-value (KV) caches during inference has become a significant bottleneck. Existing approaches primarily focus on compressing KV caches within a single prompt or reusing shared prefixes or frequently ocurred text segments across prompts. However, such strategies are limited in scenarios where prompts are semantically similar but lexically different, which frequently occurs in tasks such as multi-document summarization and conversational agents. We propose \textit{SemShareKV}, a KV cache sharing and compression framework that accelerates LLM inference by reusing KVCache in semantically similar prompts. Instead of relying on exact token matches, SemShareKV applies fuzzy token matching using locality-sensitive hashing (LSH) on token embeddings and incorporates Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE) to better preserve positional information. By selectively reusing relevant key-value pairs from a reference prompt's cache, SemShareKV reduces redundant computation while maintaining output quality. Experiments on diverse summarization datasets show up to 6.25$\times$ speedup and 42\% lower GPU memory usage with 5k tokens input, with negligible quality degradation. These results highlight the potential of semantic-aware cache sharing for efficient LLM inference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SemShareKV: Efficient KVCache Sharing for Semantically Similar Prompts via Token-Level LSH Matching" introduces a novel KVCache sharing framework (SemShareKV) designed to accelerate LLM inference by reusing precomputed KVCaches across semantically similar prompts. The key innovation lies in employing a fuzzy token matching approach using Locality-Sensitive Hashing (LSH) on token embeddings augmented with Rotary Position Embeddings (RoPE) to preserve positional information. This allows the system to identify and reuse relevant key-value pairs from a "reference" prompt's cache for a new "target" prompt, even when there aren't exact token matches. Experiments on summarization datasets demonstrate speedups and reduced GPU memory usage with minimal quality degradation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty is in its approach to KVCache sharing based on *semantic* similarity rather than exact token matching, prefix matching, or frequency. Prior work has largely focused on single-prompt optimization or exact match-based cache reuse. The use of LSH with RoPE is a reasonable, if not entirely groundbreaking, combination of existing techniques to achieve this. The integration of position encoding into the E cache is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The growing memory footprint of KVCaches is a well-recognized bottleneck in LLM inference, particularly for long contexts. Efficient KVCache sharing has the potential to significantly improve scalability and reduce computational costs. SemShareKV makes a valuable contribution by extending KVCache reuse to scenarios where semantic similarity is present, a common case in many applications (e.g., multi-document summarization, chatbots, and conversational AI).  The paper's experiments suggest real-world benefits. However, further investigation is required.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper tackles an important and pressing problem in LLM inference.
    *   The proposed approach is well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The experimental results are promising, showing significant speedups and memory savings.
    *   The analysis of token importance and recomputation strategies is insightful. The "attention sink" mitigation using RoPE is a strong point.
    *   The inclusion of both ablation studies (token matching impact) and similarity-effect analysis strengthens the paper's conclusions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation is primarily focused on summarization tasks. The generalizability to other LLM applications (e.g., code generation, translation) needs to be explored further. The current benchmark may not be representative of real-world workloads.

    *   The approach is somewhat complex and may introduce overhead, especially for shorter prompts, as noted in the paper.

    *   The paper acknowledges that a few hyperparameters need careful tuning. This suggests sensitivity to parameter settings and potential challenges in practical deployment.

    *   The evaluation datasets are created by modifying pre-existing datasets. As highlighted in the paper, some of them may not retain the original distribution.

*   **Potential Influence:** The SemShareKV framework could influence future research on KVCache management, prompting the development of more semantic-aware caching strategies. It could also encourage exploration of different similarity measures and position encoding techniques for improved KVCache reuse. If proven robust across a wider range of tasks, it has the potential to be adopted in practical LLM serving systems.

*   **Score Justification:** The paper presents a novel approach to KVCache sharing that addresses a relevant problem and demonstrates promising experimental results. However, the limited evaluation scope and the complexity of the approach, as well as the parameter tunning, prevents me from assigning it a higher score. The limitations in evaluation are particularly noteworthy. Therefore, SemShareKV is a solid and valuable contribution, but it is not quite transformative.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Pushing LLMs to Their Logical Reasoning Bound: The Role of Data Reasoning Intensity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24836v1)
- **Authors**: Zhen Bi, Zhenlin Hu, Jinnan Yang, Mingyang Chen, Cheng Deng, Yida Xue, Zeyu Yang, Qing Shen, Zhenfang Liu, Kang Zhao, Ningyu Zhang, Jungang Lou
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) highlight the importance of training data structure and quality in shaping reasoning behavior. However, most existing approaches focus on transforming data formats while neglecting the internal reasoning complexity of training samples, leaving the reasoning potential of data under-explored and underutilized. In this work, we posit that LLM logical reasoning performance is jointly constrained by the potential of the training data and the cognitive capacity of the model. To make this relationship measurable, we introduce Data Reasoning Intensity (DRI), a novel metric that quantifies the latent logical reasoning complexity of samples by decomposing and aggregating their logical structures. This allows us to analyze how well current LLMs utilize logical reasoning signals and identify performance gaps relative to data potential. Based on this insight, we introduce a re-cognizing optimization strategy that systematically enhances the logical reasoning intensity of training data.Rather than increasing data volume, our method re-optimizes existing samples to better align with the LLM's logical reasoning boundary. Extensive experiments show that our approach significantly improves performance and generalization over data-centric strategies. We further validate our method under a reinforcement learning framework. Our results indicate that prioritizing reasoning complexity in data rather than sheer scale or superficial form is essential to realizing LLMs' full cognitive potential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the challenge of improving the logical reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) by focusing on the quality and structure of training data. The authors argue that existing data-centric approaches primarily focus on data format transformations and data augmentation, neglecting the internal reasoning complexity inherent in the training samples. To address this, they introduce "Data Reasoning Intensity" (DRI), a novel metric for quantifying the latent logical reasoning complexity within a sample by decomposing its logical structure.  Based on DRI, they propose a "re-cognizing optimization strategy" that enhances the reasoning signals within the training data. The method involves re-optimizing existing samples to better align with the LLM's logical reasoning boundary. Through experiments, the authors demonstrate that their approach improves performance and generalization compared to data-centric strategies and demonstrate its efficacy in a reinforcement learning setting, thus arguing that data complexity rather than sheer scale is critical to realizing the full cognitive potential of LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel metric (DRI) and associated training strategy, representing a step beyond simple data augmentation or format changes. Prior work has recognized the importance of data quality, but the explicit quantification of *reasoning intensity* within a sample seems to be a new contribution. This aspect helps in bridging the gap between theoretical potential and actual LLM performance. The novelty also lies in *how* DRI is used - to re-optimize *existing* samples rather than just selecting or augmenting data.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is tied to the growing consensus that data quality is a crucial bottleneck in advancing LLMs.  The presented results, which show significant improvements in logical reasoning performance, underscore the importance of reasoning complexity as a data feature. The demonstrated effectiveness of the re-cognizing optimization, especially compared to data-centric baselines, points towards a potentially useful strategy for more efficient and effective LLM training. This provides actionable insight for researchers and practitioners.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing data-centric methods.
    *   **Well-defined metric:** The DRI score is a formally defined metric, derived from clear concepts.
    *   **Strong empirical evaluation:** The experimental section demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach across multiple datasets and models. The ablation study highlighting the importance of each stage of the optimization is valuable. Validating the method in a reinforcement learning framework further bolsters the findings.
    *   **Careful analysis:**  The error rate across DRI bins are analyzed in detail.
    *   **Clear exposition:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM reliance for DRI calculation:** The DRI calculation relies on LLMs for logical element extraction and reasoning, raising potential circularity issues. Although the authors address this by emphasizing that the impact of errors are minimal, this might not hold true across different data samples.
    *   **Limited model scope:** While the experiments cover multiple datasets, they are largely confined to two backbone models, LLaMA3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-7B. Broader validation across models with substantially different architectures would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Scalability to other domains:** The current evaluation is focused on logical reasoning. How well the DRI and re-cognizing optimization strategies would generalize to other domains, such as commonsense reasoning or knowledge-intensive tasks, isn't fully explored.
    *   **Computational cost:** Though the method aims at more efficient data utilization, a detailed analysis of the computational overhead involved in calculating the DRI scores and performing the re-cognizing optimization would be valuable.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper may inspire further research on developing better metrics for quantifying data quality in LLM training.
    *   The proposed re-cognizing optimization strategy could become a standard technique for improving the performance of LLMs in logical reasoning tasks.
    *   The work might shift the focus of data-centric LLM research from simple data augmentation to more sophisticated methods that consider the underlying reasoning complexity.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers both a novel metric and a practical training strategy that demonstrably improves LLM reasoning. While there are some limitations concerning the reliance on LLMs for part of the process and the breadth of model validation, the presented work is nonetheless a significant contribution. The core idea of quantifying and strategically optimizing data for reasoning intensity represents an important advancement.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cell2Text: Multimodal LLM for Generating Single-Cell Descriptions from RNA-Seq Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24840v1)
- **Authors**: Oussama Kharouiche, Aris Markogiannakis, Xiao Fei, Michail Chatzianastasis, Michalis Vazirgiannis
- **Abstract**: Single-cell RNA sequencing has transformed biology by enabling the measurement of gene expression at cellular resolution, providing information for cell types, states, and disease contexts. Recently, single-cell foundation models have emerged as powerful tools for learning transferable representations directly from expression profiles, improving performance on classification and clustering tasks. However, these models are limited to discrete prediction heads, which collapse cellular complexity into predefined labels that fail to capture the richer, contextual explanations biologists need. We introduce Cell2Text, a multimodal generative framework that translates scRNA-seq profiles into structured natural language descriptions. By integrating gene-level embeddings from single-cell foundation models with pretrained large language models, Cell2Text generates coherent summaries that capture cellular identity, tissue origin, disease associations, and pathway activity, generalizing to unseen cells. Empirically, Cell2Text outperforms baselines on classification accuracy, demonstrates strong ontological consistency using PageRank-based similarity metrics, and achieves high semantic fidelity in text generation. These results demonstrate that coupling expression data with natural language offers both stronger predictive performance and inherently interpretable outputs, pointing to a scalable path for label-efficient characterization of unseen cells.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Cell2Text paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Cell2Text, a multimodal generative framework that transforms single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) data into structured natural language descriptions.  It leverages gene-level embeddings from single-cell foundation models (like Geneformer) and integrates them with instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) via a lightweight adapter module. Cell2Text generates coherent summaries capturing cellular identity, tissue origin, disease associations, and pathway activity, even generalizing to unseen cells. Experimental results demonstrate that Cell2Text outperforms baselines in classification accuracy, exhibits strong ontological consistency, and generates high-fidelity semantic text. The authors also created a large-scale multimodal dataset of 1M cells from CELLxGENE to facilitate this cross-modal training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to generate natural language descriptions from scRNA-seq data isn't entirely novel.  Prior works like Cell2Sentence (C2S) explored this.  However, Cell2Text significantly improves upon C2S by using gene-level embeddings from pre-trained single-cell foundation models *instead* of simply concatenating gene names based on expression levels. This is a key difference. Additionally,  Cell2Text's modular architecture, including the adapter module, and its use of instruction-tuned LLMs represent a significant advancement. The creation of a large-scale, readily available multimodal dataset is also a valuable contribution.
* **Significance:** The paper tackles a significant problem in single-cell biology: the bottleneck of manual cell annotation, which is slow, subjective, and doesn't scale. Cell2Text offers a path toward automated, interpretable cell characterization. Generating natural language descriptions provides biologists with a richer understanding of cell function and identity compared to simple cell type classifications.  The strong performance on classification tasks, combined with the high semantic fidelity of the generated text, indicates the potential for Cell2Text to become a useful tool for researchers. The ontological consistency is a notable strength, implying the model learns biologically meaningful relationships.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Improved representation:**  Leveraging gene-level embeddings from Geneformer provides a more nuanced and biologically relevant representation of scRNA-seq data compared to previous approaches.
    * **Modular architecture:** The separation of the cell encoder, adapter module, and language decoder allows for flexibility and future improvements to each component.
    * **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of Cell2Text, including classification accuracy, ontological consistency, and text generation quality (using both lexical and semantic metrics).
    * **Public dataset:**  The creation and public release of the large-scale multimodal dataset will significantly benefit the research community.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on Geneformer:** The reliance on Geneformer as a fixed cell encoder limits the model's adaptability to datasets where Geneformer might not perform optimally.  Exploring alternative cell encoders or fine-tuning the encoder could be avenues for future work.
    * **Complexity of Implementation:** The integration of multiple models and the need for specialized hardware for LLM training may make the method less accessible to some researchers.
    * **Error analysis:** The paper lacks detailed error analysis. What types of descriptions does Cell2Text struggle to generate? Where does it fail in the classification tasks? Knowing these limitations would be useful.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:**  While the authors explore the effect of LLM architecture, a more thorough ablation study on the adapter module and other components would strengthen the analysis.

* **Potential Influence:**  Cell2Text has the potential to significantly impact how single-cell data is interpreted and annotated.  The ability to generate meaningful descriptions can accelerate discoveries and reduce the reliance on manual curation.  The large-scale dataset will likely spur further research in this area.

**Overall:**

Cell2Text represents a solid advancement in using LLMs for single-cell data analysis. The combination of pre-trained embeddings with instruction-tuned LLMs is a powerful approach. While it builds upon existing work, it provides a significant improvement in representation, evaluation, and overall performance. The limitations, such as the dependency on a fixed cell encoder and the lack of detailed error analysis, offer clear directions for future research.

Score: 7.  The paper demonstrates significant novelty and has potential impact. However, there is room for improvement in terms of generalizability and accessibility, leading to a score of 7 rather than a higher rating.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hierarchical Error Correction for Large Language Models: A Systematic Framework for Domain-Specific AI Quality Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24841v1)
- **Authors**: Zhilong Zhao, Yindi Liu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models face significant performance challenges in specialized domains, with state-of-the-art models achieving only 45.9% accuracy on medical coding tasks. This study proposes a Hierarchical Error Correction (HEC) framework that addresses domain-specific AI limitations through systematic error analysis and targeted intervention strategies. We analyze error patterns across four specialized domains and find that AI errors follow consistent hierarchical structures: Knowledge-layer errors (58.4%), Reasoning-layer errors (39.6%), and Complexity-layer errors (2.0%). Based on these patterns, we develop a three-stage correction framework that addresses errors according to their hierarchical importance and demonstrates that framework effectiveness correlates inversely with baseline task performance. Experimental validation across medical transcription (4,921 cases), legal document classification (1,000 cases), political bias detection (645 cases), and legal reasoning (1,000 cases) shows consistent improvements. Cross-model validation across five LLM architectures demonstrates average improvements of 11.2 percentage points (p < 0.001). However, analysis reveals framework limitations in high-baseline tasks (>75% accuracy), where hierarchical intervention may interfere with effective reasoning processes. The results suggest that systematic error analysis can guide effective AI enhancement strategies in specialized domains, particularly for moderate-baseline tasks, while highlighting the importance of understanding framework boundaries for optimal deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hierarchical Error Correction (HEC), a framework designed to enhance the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in specialized domains.  It argues that AI errors in these domains exhibit a consistent, hierarchical structure: Knowledge-layer, Reasoning-layer, and Complexity-layer. The HEC framework employs a three-stage correction process targeting errors based on their hierarchical importance.  The paper validates the framework through experiments across medical transcription, legal document classification, political bias detection, and legal reasoning tasks, demonstrating consistent performance improvements, particularly in tasks with moderate baseline accuracy. It also investigates boundary conditions of HEC, noting that its effectiveness diminishes in tasks with high baseline accuracy, where it can even hinder performance. Cross-model validation is performed using five LLM architectures (DeepSeek Chat, GPT-40-mini, Gemini-2.0-flash-exp, Qwen-2.5-72B, GPT-4.1-nano) demonstrates universal applicability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic approach to error analysis and correction in LLMs, specifically tailored for specialized domains. The hierarchical error model itself, drawing from cognitive error theory, isn't entirely new. However, applying this framework to analyze and improve LLM performance in domain-specific tasks is a valuable contribution. The investigation into the "inverse relationship" between baseline accuracy and HEC effectiveness is a novel and practically relevant finding.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical challenge in the deployment of LLMs: their often subpar performance in specialized domains where high accuracy is crucial. By providing a structured methodology for error analysis and targeted intervention, it offers a path toward more reliable and trustworthy AI systems in fields like healthcare and law.  The findings about boundary conditions are also significant, as they help guide the appropriate application of HEC and prevent its misuse. The cross-model validation establishes a degree of robustness to the architectural nuances of specific LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Methodology:** The paper presents a well-defined framework with clear steps for error analysis, intervention strategy development, and validation.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The framework is rigorously tested across multiple domains and LLM architectures, providing strong empirical evidence for its effectiveness and limitations.  The use of appropriate statistical analysis enhances the credibility of the results.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The paper offers practical guidance for organizations seeking to deploy LLMs in specialized domains, including recommendations for baseline assessment and domain adaptation. The open-source release strengthens its impact and uptake.
    *   **Attention to Boundary Conditions:**  The identification of conditions where HEC might *not* be effective demonstrates a thorough and responsible approach to the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Domains:** While the paper covers four domains, the generalizability of the hierarchical error model and the HEC framework could be further strengthened by testing it across a wider range of specializations (e.g., engineering, finance).  Further studies could explore domains with significantly different underlying knowledge structures.
    *   **Depth of Analysis:** The paper presents a high-level overview of interventions strategies. More in-depth discussion and comparison of intervention strategies would be beneficial, addressing their relative computational complexity, data requirements, and implementation costs.
    *   **Reliance on Exact Match Accuracy:** Using *exact match accuracy* can be a limitation, especially in tasks where near-correct predictions are still valuable.  Exploring other evaluation metrics (e.g., semantic similarity) could provide a more nuanced picture of the framework's impact.
    *   **Potential for "Over-Engineering":** Although not a weakness currently because of manual intervention strategies, a fully automated HEC framework risks over-engineering. The inverse relationship noted in higher baseline tasks suggests that, at some point, more complexity is less effective.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by shifting the focus from ad hoc LLM optimization to structured, theory-driven approaches. It could inspire further research into error analysis and correction methodologies, leading to more reliable and trustworthy AI systems in specialized domains.
* **Score Justification:** In consideration of both the strengths and weaknesses of this study, it has:
        * A strong methodology for error analysis and targeted interventions.
        * Empirical validation across multiple domains and LLM architectures.
        * Identified and discussed the limitation of the findings which help to understand optimal deployment of the HEC.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Between Help and Harm: An Evaluation of Mental Health Crisis Handling by LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24857v1)
- **Authors**: Adrian Arnaiz-Rodriguez, Miguel Baidal, Erik Derner, Jenn Layton Annable, Mark Ball, Mark Ince, Elvira Perez Vallejos, Nuria Oliver
- **Abstract**: The widespread use of chatbots powered by large language models (LLMs) such as ChatGPT and Llama has fundamentally reshaped how people seek information and advice across domains. Increasingly, these chatbots are being used in high-stakes contexts, including emotional support and mental health concerns. While LLMs can offer scalable support, their ability to safely detect and respond to acute mental health crises remains poorly understood.
