Progress is hampered by the absence of unified crisis taxonomies, robust annotated benchmarks, and empirical evaluations grounded in clinical best practices. In this work, we address these gaps by introducing a unified taxonomy of six clinically-informed mental health crisis categories, curating a diverse evaluation dataset, and establishing an expert-designed protocol for assessing response appropriateness. We systematically benchmark three state-of-the-art LLMs for their ability to classify crisis types and generate safe, appropriate responses. The results reveal that while LLMs are highly consistent and generally reliable in addressing explicit crisis disclosures, significant risks remain. A non-negligible proportion of responses are rated as inappropriate or harmful, with responses generated by an open-weight model exhibiting higher failure rates than those generated by the commercial ones. We also identify systemic weaknesses in handling indirect or ambiguous risk signals, a reliance on formulaic and inauthentic default replies, and frequent misalignment with user context. These findings underscore the urgent need for enhanced safeguards, improved crisis detection, and context-aware interventions in LLM deployments. Our taxonomy, datasets, and evaluation framework lay the groundwork for ongoing research and responsible innovation in AI-driven mental health support, helping to minimize harm and better protect vulnerable users.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the critical issue of how Large Language Models (LLMs) handle mental health crises, focusing on identifying potential harms and areas for improvement. The authors introduce a unified taxonomy for categorizing mental health crises (suicidal ideation, self-harm, anxiety crisis, violent thoughts, substance abuse, risk-taking), curate a dataset of over 2,000 user inputs from publicly available sources, and develop an expert-designed protocol to assess LLM response appropriateness. They benchmark three LLMs (gpt-4o-mini, gpt-5-nano, llama-4-scout) for crisis classification and response generation. The evaluation uses a 5-point Likert scale ranging from harmful to fully appropriate. Results indicate that while LLMs generally handle explicit crisis disclosures well, significant risks remain, especially in self-harm and suicidal ideation. Open-weight models showed higher failure rates than commercial ones. The study also identifies weaknesses in handling indirect risk signals, reliance on formulaic replies, and misalignment with user context. The paper emphasizes the need for enhanced safeguards and improved crisis detection for safer LLM deployments. Datasets and code are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its comprehensive approach to evaluating LLM responses to mental health crises. It addresses several gaps in the existing literature by providing a unified taxonomy, a robust benchmark dataset, and a clinically grounded evaluation protocol.
    *   **Significance:** The significance of the work is high. The increasing use of LLMs in mental health contexts demands careful scrutiny, and this paper provides valuable empirical evidence about their capabilities and limitations. The findings have direct implications for responsible AI development and deployment in this sensitive area.
    *   **Methodology:** The methodology is rigorous, incorporating expert input and a multi-faceted evaluation approach. The LLM-as-a-judge approach allows for large-scale evaluation while maintaining a degree of consistency. The detailed analysis of different crisis categories and the inclusion of both commercial and open-source models add to the robustness of the study.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The public availability of the code and data enhances the reproducibility and transparency of the research, facilitating further investigation and improvement in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Generalizability:** The study focuses on English language data and three specific LLMs. While this is a reasonable starting point, the results may not generalize to other languages, modalities (e.g., audio), or newer/different models.
    *   **Static Evaluation:** The evaluation protocol, while comprehensive, is essentially static. It assesses responses in isolation, without considering the longitudinal aspect of therapeutic interactions or the potential for iterative refinement of responses over time.
    *   **Reliance on LLM as a Judge:** While using an LLM as a judge enables scale, it also introduces potential biases inherent in the LLM's training data and architecture. The evaluation's dependence on *gpt-4o-mini* introduces a layer of abstraction that might obscure subtleties that human experts could perceive.
    *   **Limited Scope of Harm Assessment:** The paper emphasizes *direct* harms (e.g., giving harmful information), but could expand the discussion on *indirect* harms such as invalidating user experiences by relying heavily on formulaic or boilerplate statements.
    *   **The LLMs used in the experiment are outdated** The authors are projecting the date when it was launched but it appears from the text that it did not actually launch on that date. This should be rectified.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper's influence is likely to be substantial. It offers a framework for evaluating LLMs in mental health, which can be adopted and extended by other researchers. The identified weaknesses and recommendations provide concrete directions for future development efforts. The taxonomy and datasets will serve as valuable resources for the community.
    *   The paper's findings can inform policy discussions and guidelines for the ethical and responsible use of LLMs in mental health, helping to protect vulnerable users.

*The analysis has to be revised and should reflect that the information is dated*

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**
The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by providing a comprehensive and rigorous evaluation of LLMs in mental health crisis handling. The development of a unified taxonomy, the curation of a benchmark dataset, and the design of an expert-driven evaluation protocol are valuable achievements. However, the limitations related to generalizability, static evaluation, potential biases in the LLM-as-a-judge approach, and the limited exploration of indirect harms temper the overall assessment. Given these considerations, a score of 7.5 reflects the paper's substantial contribution while acknowledging the need for further research to address the identified limitations and expand the scope of the analysis.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Metaphor identification using large language models: A comparison of RAG, prompt engineering, and fine-tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24866v1)
- **Authors**: Matteo Fuoli, Weihang Huang, Jeannette Littlemore, Sarah Turner, Ellen Wilding
- **Abstract**: Metaphor is a pervasive feature of discourse and a powerful lens for examining cognition, emotion, and ideology. Large-scale analysis, however, has been constrained by the need for manual annotation due to the context-sensitive nature of metaphor. This study investigates the potential of large language models (LLMs) to automate metaphor identification in full texts. We compare three methods: (i) retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), where the model is provided with a codebook and instructed to annotate texts based on its rules and examples; (ii) prompt engineering, where we design task-specific verbal instructions; and (iii) fine-tuning, where the model is trained on hand-coded texts to optimize performance. Within prompt engineering, we test zero-shot, few-shot, and chain-of-thought strategies. Our results show that state-of-the-art closed-source LLMs can achieve high accuracy, with fine-tuning yielding a median F1 score of 0.79. A comparison of human and LLM outputs reveals that most discrepancies are systematic, reflecting well-known grey areas and conceptual challenges in metaphor theory. We propose that LLMs can be used to at least partly automate metaphor identification and can serve as a testbed for developing and refining metaphor identification protocols and the theory that underpins them.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate metaphor identification in full texts.  It compares three methods: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), Prompt Engineering (including zero-shot, few-shot, and chain-of-thought prompting), and Fine-tuning. The study uses a corpus of hand-coded film reviews annotated with a 'phraseological' approach.  The results demonstrate that state-of-the-art closed-source LLMs, especially when fine-tuned, can achieve high accuracy in metaphor identification. The paper also analyzes discrepancies between human and LLM annotations, highlighting grey areas and conceptual challenges in metaphor theory.  The authors propose LLMs as a tool for semi-automated metaphor identification, theory refinement, and a benchmark for annotation protocols.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper has several novel contributions:

*   **Full-text Metaphor Identification:**  Most existing NLP work on metaphor detection focuses on classifying individual, often pre-selected, words as literal or metaphorical within decontextualized sentences. This paper tackles the more ecologically valid and linguistically relevant task of full-text metaphor identification and annotation, addressing a key limitation of prior research.
*   **Methodological Comparison:**  It provides a systematic comparison of three core methods (RAG, Prompt Engineering, Fine-tuning) for deploying LLMs in metaphor identification, offering insights into the strengths and weaknesses of each. The careful exploration of different prompting strategies within prompt engineering (zero-shot, few-shot, chain-of-thought) also adds to the methodological rigor.
*   **Phraseological Approach:** The study uses a “phraseological” approach for manual annotations and LLM output assessment, which better aligns with how metaphor operates in discourse, considering both single-word and multi-word metaphorical expressions. This also allows the paper to capture more complex or non-lexicalized metaphorical language.
*   **Analysis of Discrepancies:**  The in-depth analysis of human-LLM discrepancies is particularly valuable. It moves beyond simply reporting accuracy scores and delves into the specific types of errors made by LLMs, connecting them to existing theoretical debates and challenges in metaphor theory. It also presents a path for refining existing manual coding frameworks.
*   **Emphasis on Accessibility & Replicability:** The release of the code, the manually annotated corpus, the codebook, and the prompts used are critical and increases the reusability of this study and contributes to the reproducibility of the findings.
*   **Rebuttal of Prevailing Views:** Explicitly responding to negative views on LLMs for annotation tasks.

**Significance:**

*   **Improved Scalability of Metaphor Research:**  If LLMs can automate or semi-automate metaphor identification, it can significantly reduce the cost and time required for large-scale metaphor analysis, enabling researchers to explore more expansive datasets and address research questions that were previously infeasible.
*   **Refinement of Metaphor Theory:** The analysis of LLM errors and discrepancies with human annotations provides valuable insights for refining metaphor identification protocols and addressing persistent theoretical challenges.
*   **Wider Applicability:**  The methods and findings are relevant to other areas of NLP and computational linguistics that involve complex semantic analysis and annotation tasks.
*   **Challenges Prevailing Assumptions in NLP:** The approach provides a counterweight to the more narrow, word-centric view common in much of the computational linguistics literature on metaphor detection.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Corpus Size:** While the corpus is adequate for an initial exploration, a larger and more diverse corpus could further strengthen the findings and improve the generalizability of the results.
*   **Closed-Source Model Reliance:** The study's heavy reliance on closed-source LLMs (especially fine-tuned GPT-4 variants) raises concerns about reproducibility and accessibility. Open-source models lag behind, and their comparative weaknesses are demonstrated, but the field is rapidly evolving.
*   **Limited Exploration of RAG:** While RAG is included, more detailed examination of the impact and utility of different types and granularities of information retrieved could yield valuable insights. The codebook is provided, however, how the LLM used the content from the codebook could be better elucidated and the RAG component's efficacy.
*   **Token-level Evaluation:**  While the authors defend their token-level evaluation,  span-based evaluation remains important to consider since the manual analysis hinges on spans of texts, rather than a list of tokens.

**Justification for Score:**

This is a well-executed study that makes a significant contribution to the field. It tackles a complex and challenging task, provides a systematic comparison of different LLM-based methods, offers valuable theoretical insights, and makes a compelling case for the use of LLMs in metaphor analysis. The detailed analysis of discrepancies and the emphasis on accessibility are particularly commendable. The limitations are acknowledged by the authors, and they represent opportunities for future research. Taking into account the novelty, significance, and some limitations, I assign the following:

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### StreamForest: Efficient Online Video Understanding with Persistent Event Memory
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24871v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangyu Zeng, Kefan Qiu, Qingyu Zhang, Xinhao Li, Jing Wang, Jiaxin Li, Ziang Yan, Kun Tian, Meng Tian, Xinhai Zhao, Yi Wang, Limin Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have recently achieved remarkable progress in video understanding. However, their effectiveness in real-time streaming scenarios remains limited due to storage constraints of historical visual features and insufficient real-time spatiotemporal reasoning. To address these challenges, we propose StreamForest, a novel architecture specifically designed for streaming video understanding. Central to StreamForest is the Persistent Event Memory Forest, a memory mechanism that adaptively organizes video frames into multiple event-level tree structures. This process is guided by penalty functions based on temporal distance, content similarity, and merge frequency, enabling efficient long-term memory retention under limited computational resources. To enhance real-time perception, we introduce a Fine-grained Spatiotemporal Window, which captures detailed short-term visual cues to improve current scene perception. Additionally, we present OnlineIT, an instruction-tuning dataset tailored for streaming video tasks. OnlineIT significantly boosts MLLM performance in both real-time perception and future prediction. To evaluate generalization in practical applications, we introduce ODV-Bench, a new benchmark focused on real-time streaming video understanding in autonomous driving scenarios. Experimental results demonstrate that StreamForest achieves the state-of-the-art performance, with accuracies of 77.3% on StreamingBench, 60.5% on OVBench, and 55.6% on OVO-Bench. In particular, even under extreme visual token compression (limited to 1024 tokens), the model retains 96.8% of its average accuracy in eight benchmarks relative to the default setting. These results underscore the robustness, efficiency, and generalizability of StreamForest for streaming video understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the StreamForest paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces StreamForest, a novel architecture designed for efficient online video understanding, specifically addressing the challenges of real-time processing under resource constraints. The key components are:

1.  **Persistent Event Memory Forest (PEMF):** An adaptive memory mechanism organizing video frames into event-level tree structures based on temporal distance, content similarity, and merge frequency penalties. This facilitates long-term memory retention.

2.  **Fine-grained Spatiotemporal Window (FSTW):** Captures detailed short-term visual cues for improved real-time scene perception.

3.  **OnlineIT:** A new instruction-tuning dataset tailored for streaming video tasks, aimed at improving real-time perception and future prediction capabilities of MLLMs.

4.  **ODV-Bench:** A new benchmark for evaluating streaming video understanding in autonomous driving scenarios.

The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on StreamingBench, OVBench, and OVO-Bench, while also showcasing robustness under extreme visual token compression.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the combination of the PEMF and FSTW architectures, alongside the introduction of a tailored dataset (OnlineIT) and benchmark (ODV-Bench). Prior works have explored memory compression and streaming video, but StreamForest presents a unique approach to adaptive event-based memory organization guided by multiple penalty functions. The OnlineIT dataset also addresses the specific challenges of training MLLMs for streaming videos, especially the bias present in offline datasets. The design of ODV-bench is a significant contribution as it explicitly focuses on real-time perception and future prediction in autonomous driving.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to enable more efficient and effective real-time video understanding in resource-constrained environments. The strong performance on the benchmarks, particularly the ODV-Bench, suggests the practical applicability of StreamForest in domains like autonomous driving, robotics, and live video streaming. The robustness under extreme compression is a critical feature for real-world deployment. The benchmarks and dataset they provide has significant value for other researchers in the field.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined challenges.
    *   Novel and well-motivated architecture.
    *   Strong experimental results on multiple benchmarks, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance.
    *   Introduction of a new benchmark (ODV-Bench) focused on a relevant and challenging application domain.
    *   Demonstrated robustness to visual token compression, increasing the real-world practicality of the method.
    *   The paper is clearly written and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The penalty functions used in PEMF, while effective, seem somewhat heuristic. A more theoretically grounded approach to defining these penalties could further strengthen the paper.
    *   While the OnlineIT dataset is a valuable contribution, further details on its construction process and diversity might be warranted.
    *   The computational complexity analysis of PEMF, while presented, could be expanded to provide deeper insights into its scalability.
    *   The model relies on inter-frame similarity which can be limiting.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in streaming video understanding, particularly in areas like efficient memory management, real-time perception, and application-specific benchmarking. The PEMF architecture could serve as a foundation for future memory mechanisms in MLLMs. The ODV-Bench benchmark may encourage the development of more robust and reliable video understanding systems for autonomous driving.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of streaming video understanding by introducing a novel architecture (StreamForest), a tailored dataset (OnlineIT), and a practical benchmark (ODV-Bench). The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance and robustness.  The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem with a well-designed solution and provides valuable resources for the research community. While there are minor weaknesses, the strengths far outweigh them.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Environment-Aware Satellite Image Generation with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24875v1)
- **Authors**: Nikos Kostagiolas, Pantelis Georgiades, Yannis Panagakis, Mihalis A. Nicolaou
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based foundation models have recently garnered much attention in the field of generative modeling due to their ability to generate images of high quality and fidelity. Although not straightforward, their recent application to the field of remote sensing signaled the first successful trials towards harnessing the large volume of publicly available datasets containing multimodal information. Despite their success, existing methods face considerable limitations: they rely on limited environmental context, struggle with missing or corrupted data, and often fail to reliably reflect user intentions in generated outputs. In this work, we propose a novel diffusion model conditioned on environmental context, that is able to generate satellite images by conditioning from any combination of three different control signals: a) text, b) metadata, and c) visual data. In contrast to previous works, the proposed method is i) to our knowledge, the first of its kind to condition satellite image generation on dynamic environmental conditions as part of its control signals, and ii) incorporating a metadata fusion strategy that models attribute embedding interactions to account for partially corrupt and/or missing observations. Our method outperforms previous methods both qualitatively (robustness to missing metadata, higher responsiveness to control inputs) and quantitatively (higher fidelity, accuracy, and quality of generations measured using 6 different metrics) in the trials of single-image and temporal generation. The reported results support our hypothesis that conditioning on environmental context can improve the performance of foundation models for satellite imagery, and render our model a promising candidate for usage in downstream tasks. The collected 3-modal dataset is to our knowledge, the first publicly-available dataset to combine data from these three different mediums.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel diffusion model for generating satellite images conditioned on environmental context.  The model is trained using a newly compiled dataset combining satellite imagery, text descriptions, and metadata (static and dynamic environmental variables like wind, precipitation, and solar radiation).  A key innovation is a metadata fusion strategy that handles missing or corrupted data by modeling interactions between metadata embeddings.  The paper demonstrates superior qualitative (robustness to missing metadata, responsiveness to control inputs) and quantitative (fidelity, accuracy, quality) performance compared to existing methods in both single-image and temporal generation tasks. The dataset itself is presented as a significant contribution, being the first publicly available dataset combining these three modalities for satellite imagery.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper exhibits a good degree of novelty. While diffusion models for image generation are not new, their application to satellite imagery with a focus on fine-grained environmental conditioning *is* a valuable contribution. The inclusion of dynamic environmental variables as control signals and the metadata fusion strategy to handle missing data represent incremental yet meaningful advancements. The construction and release of the 3-modal dataset is a strong positive.
* **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a crucial limitation of existing satellite imagery generation models: the lack of comprehensive environmental context. By incorporating dynamic weather and climate data, the model can generate more realistic and contextually accurate satellite images. This has the potential to improve downstream tasks like environmental monitoring, disaster assessment, and climate change studies.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Environmental Conditioning:** The model's ability to incorporate a wide range of environmental factors is a significant advantage.
    * **Robust Metadata Handling:**  The metadata fusion strategy is well-motivated and appears to be effective in mitigating the impact of incomplete data. This addresses a practical challenge in real-world satellite imagery applications.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents both qualitative and quantitative results demonstrating the superiority of the proposed method.  The inclusion of comparisons against existing methods strengthens the evaluation.
    * **Public Dataset Release:**  Releasing the dataset enables further research and development in this area, fostering reproducibility and facilitating comparisons.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Advancement:** The paper builds upon existing diffusion model architectures. While the modifications are well-engineered for the specific task, the core diffusion model itself is not fundamentally changed.
    * **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models are computationally expensive to train and generate samples. The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational requirements or potential optimizations for deployment.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper compares against a baseline, more detailed ablation studies would strengthen the understanding of the contribution of each component (e.g., the metadata fusion strategy versus the specific environmental variables included). More detailed information regarding choices of parameters or the structure of the MLPs would also be beneficial.
    * **Potential for Overfitting:** The dataset, while novel, may not fully represent the diversity of all environmental conditions. This raises a concern about the generalizability of the model to unseen regions or extreme weather events.

**Justification:**

The paper's key strengths lie in its practical focus, addressing real-world limitations in satellite imagery generation with a clear emphasis on environmental context.  The dataset and robust metadata handling are significant contributions. However, the reliance on existing diffusion model architectures, coupled with limited ablation studies and the potential for dataset bias, constrain the overall score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Emergence of Social Science of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24877v1)
- **Authors**: Xiao Jia, Zhanzhan Zhao
- **Abstract**: The social science of large language models (LLMs) examines how these systems evoke mind attributions, interact with one another, and transform human activity and institutions. We conducted a systematic review of 270 studies, combining text embeddings, unsupervised clustering and topic modeling to build a computational taxonomy. Three domains emerge organically across the reviewed literature. LLM as Social Minds examines whether and when models display behaviors that elicit attributions of cognition, morality and bias, while addressing challenges such as test leakage and surface cues. LLM Societies examines multi-agent settings where interaction protocols, architectures and mechanism design shape coordination, norms, institutions and collective epistemic processes. LLM-Human Interactions examines how LLMs reshape tasks, learning, trust, work and governance, and how risks arise at the human-AI interface. This taxonomy provides a reproducible map of a fragmented field, clarifies evidentiary standards across levels of analysis, and highlights opportunities for cumulative progress in the social science of artificial intelligence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a systematic review and computational taxonomy of research on the social science of large language models (LLMs). It analyzes 270 studies, employing text embeddings, clustering, and topic modeling to categorize the literature into three main domains: LLM as Social Minds (examining mind attribution and biases), LLM Societies (studying multi-agent interactions and institutions), and LLM-Human Interactions (analyzing how LLMs reshape human tasks and create risks). The authors validate their computational taxonomy by comparing it with an expert-informed taxonomy and demonstrate strong structural overlap. The paper aims to provide a comprehensive and reproducible map of this fragmented field, clarifying evidentiary standards and highlighting opportunities for future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic and Rigorous Methodology:** The paper uses a clearly defined and reproducible methodology that combines a PRISMA-compliant systematic review with computational techniques. This approach enhances the objectivity and transparency of the analysis.
*   **Novel Approach:** The use of unsupervised clustering and topic modeling to build a taxonomy of LLM social science research represents a novel approach compared to traditional narrative reviews. It uncovers emergent themes in a data-driven way.
*   **Validation:** The comparison with expert-coded categories provides evidence that the computational taxonomy aligns with human understanding, reinforcing its validity.
*   **Comprehensive Scope:** The review covers a substantial body of research, offering a broad overview of the field.
*   **Clear Taxonomy:** The identified three domains are intuitive and provide a useful framework for organizing the literature.
*   **Highlights Gaps and Opportunities:** By mapping the field, the paper identifies areas where further research is needed and helps standardize constructs for cumulative progress.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Social Science Perspectives:** Although the paper strives for breadth, the "social science" lens might unintentionally exclude certain valuable contributions from other disciplines.
*   **Potential for Bias:** Despite the use of computational methods, inherent biases can arise in the selection of keywords for the search strategy, as well as in the interpretation of topic models.
*   **Snapshot in Time:** Given the rapid evolution of LLMs, a snapshot in time, even with an extensive dataset, becomes quickly dated, limiting the long-term value of the results. The authors acknowledge this limitation, but it remains a significant factor.
*   **Oversimplification:** Reducing complex research topics into a limited number of clusters and themes necessarily involves some degree of oversimplification. The nuances of individual studies may be lost.
*   **English-language restriction:** The exclusion of non-English publications limits the scope and introduces a potential bias.
*   **The date is set in the future (2025).** This is quite unorthodox and calls the integrity of the document into question.
*   **Limited novelty:** While the methodology is sound, the results are predictable and straightforward.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its methodological approach of using computational techniques for systematic review and taxonomy building in the context of LLM social science research. Its significance is in providing a valuable resource for researchers in the field by creating a map of the key research areas, highlighting connections between them, and identifying gaps. However, the predictability of the results (the three domains are fairly intuitive) and limitations associated with the rapidly evolving nature of the field and oversimplification of results temper its long-term impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is well-executed, and presents a solid methodology for tackling the challenge of synthesizing a rapidly growing body of research. The validation against expert knowledge enhances its credibility. However, the predictable nature of the results, the limitations of being a snapshot in time, and the narrow perspective on social sciences limit the transformative nature of the study. Moreover, the date being set in the future calls its validity into question.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 5/10

### Expanding Computation Spaces of LLMs at Inference Time
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24884v1)
- **Authors**: Yoonna Jang, Kisu Yang, Isabelle Augenstein
- **Abstract**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) rationale enables language models to use additional task-related text for problem-solving, benefiting not only from detailed reasoning steps but also from the expanded computational space of longer inputs. Prior work has trained filler or special tokens to serve as additional computation spaces. In this study, we investigate whether language models can leverage artificially inserted sequences of filler tokens solely at inference. We first identify effective token types, numbers, and insertion locations, then examine at what stage of training models begin to exploit the expanded computation space, and finally analyze dynamics within these spaces via attention maps. Experiments on models ranging from 1.7B to 32B across open-domain QA and math tasks show that appropriate token types and counts vary, but placing filler tokens directly before the final 'Answer:' token is most effective. Smaller models benefit most, up to 12.372 percentage points in SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct, indicating that these spaces act as additional computational capacity rather than redundant input. Attention maps reveal that expanded spaces often continue the original attention mechanism and sometimes focus on questions or answer options, suggesting meaningful computation for problem-solving.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether Large Language Models (LLMs) can leverage artificially inserted sequences of filler tokens as expanded computation spaces *solely at inference time*, without any prior training on those specific filler token insertions. The authors explore various aspects including effective token types, number of tokens, insertion locations, the stage of training at which LLMs start exploiting these spaces, and the dynamics within these spaces through attention map analysis. Experiments on models ranging from 1.7B to 32B across open-domain QA and math tasks reveal that appropriate token types and counts vary, and placing tokens before the final 'Answer:' token is generally most effective.  Smaller models benefit the most, suggesting that these spaces act as additional computational capacity rather than merely redundant input.  Attention maps show that the expanded spaces can either extend the original attention mechanism or focus on the question or answer options, indicating meaningful computation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of expanding computational space in LLMs *at inference time* without training on specific filler tokens has some novelty.  Prior works generally fine-tuned models with added filler tokens. Evaluating the ability of pre-trained or instruction-tuned models to use existing, frequent tokens found in the training data as 'scratchpads' is a good starting point.

*   **Significance:** The significance is somewhat limited.  While the paper demonstrates that models can indeed leverage these inserted tokens, the magnitude of improvement, particularly for larger models, is often marginal. The core question of *why* these tokens help is not deeply explored. While the attention maps offer some insights, the interpretation is somewhat high-level and doesn't provide a mechanistic understanding of the process.
    The paper's findings that smaller models benefit more and that specific token types (e.g., `.` or `\n`) are generally more effective than, for example, `<pad>` suggests that there is indeed something models learn about the usage of these tokens during their training.
    A major limitation is the focus on simple QA and math tasks. The impact of expanded computation spaces in more complex reasoning or creative tasks remains unclear. Also, the results depend heavily on the specific prompt format used. A more systematic exploration of prompting strategies would be needed to make the approach more reliable and universally applicable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Ablation studies: The paper systematically explores different token types, numbers, and insertion locations.
    *   Attention analysis: Attention maps are used to offer insights into what might be happening within the expanded computational space.
    *   Model scale: Results are reported across a range of model sizes.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited improvement: The observed performance gains are often small, especially in larger models. This raises questions about the practical utility of the method.
    *   Lack of mechanistic understanding: The attention map analysis provides only a high-level view. There is no clear explanation of *how* the LLMs are actually using the added tokens for computation.
    *   Task limitations: The experiments are limited to relatively simple QA and math tasks.
    *   Prompt dependence: The findings are likely dependent on the specific prompts used and may not generalize well.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper might inspire further research into more efficient and structured ways to expand LLMs' computational capacity at inference time. The approach could potentially be useful for very small models where parameter capacity is a major bottleneck. However, its practical impact seems limited given the current results.

**Score: 5**

**Rationale:**

The paper's novelty lies in its focus on inference-time computation space expansion without additional training, which is a worthwhile exploration. However, the results are relatively modest and primarily beneficial for small models. The lack of a deeper, mechanistic understanding of how the added tokens are being used, and the reliance on specific tasks and prompts, limit the paper's overall impact and significance. The investigation is thorough, but the gains are not compelling enough to warrant a higher score. The paper opens an avenue for further research, but it doesn't yet represent a major breakthrough.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### MMRQA: Signal-Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Models for MRI Quality Assessment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24888v1)
- **Authors**: Fankai Jia, Daisong Gan, Zhe Zhang, Zhaochi Wen, Chenchen Dan, Dong Liang, Haifeng Wang
- **Abstract**: Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) quality assessment is crucial for clinical decision-making, yet remains challenging due to data scarcity and protocol variability. Traditional approaches face fundamental trade-offs: signal-based methods like MRIQC provide quantitative metrics but lack semantic understanding, while deep learning approaches achieve high accuracy but sacrifice interpretability. To address these limitations, we introduce the Multimodal MRI Quality Assessment (MMRQA) framework, pioneering the integration of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with acquisition-aware signal processing. MMRQA combines three key innovations: robust metric extraction via MRQy augmented with simulated artifacts, structured transformation of metrics into question-answer pairs using Qwen, and parameter-efficient fusion through Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) of LLaVA-OneVision. Evaluated on MR-ART, FastMRI, and MyConnectome benchmarks, MMRQA achieves state-of-the-art performance with strong zero-shot generalization, as validated by comprehensive ablation studies. By bridging quantitative analysis with semantic reasoning, our framework generates clinically interpretable outputs that enhance quality control in dynamic medical settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "MMRQA: Signal-Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Models for MRI Quality Assessment":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMRQA, a framework designed to improve MRI quality assessment by integrating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with acquisition-aware signal processing. MMRQA addresses the limitations of traditional methods that either lack semantic understanding (signal-based) or interpretability (deep learning). The framework combines robust metric extraction (using MRQy augmented with simulated artifacts), structured transformation of metrics into question-answer pairs (using Qwen), and parameter-efficient fusion using LoRA on LLaVA-OneVision. Experiments on MR-ART, FastMRI, and MyConnectome datasets show state-of-the-art performance and strong zero-shot generalization. The authors claim that MMRQA bridges quantitative analysis with semantic reasoning, generating clinically interpretable outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating signal-based metrics, structured QA pairs generated by LLMs, and visual information via a MLLM (specifically, LLaVA-OneVision fine-tuned with LoRA) for MRI quality assessment *is* a significant step forward. Prior work has touched on multimodal approaches and LLM prompting, but MMRQA presents a cohesive pipeline for more comprehensive quality analysis. The novelty lies in the specific combination of these three elements and the engineering required to make them work effectively together. The use of simulated artifacts to augment training data is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** MRI quality assessment is crucial in clinical practice, and improvements in this area directly impact patient care. The potential for automation and standardization offered by MMRQA has the potential to reduce variability and improve decision-making. By providing clinically interpretable outputs, the system could be valuable for assisting radiologists and technicians. The demonstrated zero-shot generalization suggests that the approach could be adaptable to different MRI protocols and datasets. However, the clinical impact requires further real-world validation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   The proposed MMRQA framework is well-structured and integrates several innovations.
    *   The experiments are reasonably thorough, using multiple datasets.
    *   The ablation study provides insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   The authors address the data scarcity problem by incorporating simulated artifacts, improving the robustness of the training.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The GPT-5 evaluation for description quality, while interesting, has the problem that it is based on another LLM and therefore might contain biases or inconsistencies.  Human evaluation would strengthen these results considerably.
    *   The "clinical interpretability" claim, while promising, lacks in-depth qualitative analysis. The paper should provide more detailed examples of the kind of insights that MMRQA provides *to* a clinician.
    *   While the zero-shot generalization on MyConnectome is encouraging, more testing on completely unseen datasets is needed to confirm the robustness of the approach.
    *   The current method depends on commercial LLM APIs (Qwen), which might become problematic in the future in terms of usage fees, availability, and open access for research.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research into using MLLMs for medical image quality assessment. The modular design of MMRQA makes it a useful framework to be built upon, and the specific integration of signal processing and language-based reasoning offers a valuable direction for future exploration. It addresses a practical problem and provides a viable solution that can be useful in clinical settings.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to an important problem. The integration of signal processing, QA transformation, and LoRA-tuned LLaVA-OneVision is a valuable contribution. The weaknesses are relatively minor and can be addressed in future work. The paper’s significance is substantial, offering potential for improving MRI quality control and clinical decision-making. While the reliance on GPT-5 evaluation and the need for stronger clinical evaluation are points to consider, the overall quality and potential of the work justify a score of 7. The potential for high clinical impact and the likely influence on future research pushes the score above a simple passing grade.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### VAGUEGAN: Stealthy Poisoning and Backdoor Attacks on Image Generative Pipelines
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24891v1)
- **Authors**: Mostafa Mohaimen Akand Faisal, Rabeya Amin Jhuma
- **Abstract**: Generative models such as GANs and diffusion models are widely used to synthesize photorealistic images and to support downstream creative and editing tasks. While adversarial attacks on discriminative models are well studied, attacks targeting generative pipelines where small, stealthy perturbations in inputs lead to controlled changes in outputs are less explored. This study introduces VagueGAN, an attack pipeline combining a modular perturbation network PoisonerNet with a Generator Discriminator pair to craft stealthy triggers that cause targeted changes in generated images. Attack efficacy is evaluated using a custom proxy metric, while stealth is analyzed through perceptual and frequency domain measures. The transferability of the method to a modern diffusion based pipeline is further examined through ControlNet guided editing. Interestingly, the experiments show that poisoned outputs can display higher visual quality compared to clean counterparts, challenging the assumption that poisoning necessarily reduces fidelity. Unlike conventional pixel level perturbations, latent space poisoning in GANs and diffusion pipelines can retain or even enhance output aesthetics, exposing a blind spot in pixel level defenses. Moreover, carefully optimized perturbations can produce consistent, stealthy effects on generator outputs while remaining visually inconspicuous, raising concerns for the integrity of image generation pipelines.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a justified novelty and significance score.

**Summary:**

The paper "VagueGAN: Stealthy Poisoning and Backdoor Attacks on Image Generative Pipelines" introduces a novel framework for injecting stealthy backdoors into image generative models, specifically GANs. It presents VagueGAN, which integrates a "PoisonerNet" module to introduce imperceptible perturbations in training data. These perturbations, while visually inconspicuous and sometimes even improving the visual quality of generated images, cause the GAN to learn specific "backdoor" behaviors triggered by specific inputs. The authors evaluate the effectiveness of their approach using spectral signature analysis and a custom backdoor success proxy. They also demonstrate that these poisoned signals can transfer to diffusion-based image generation pipelines like Stable Diffusion with ControlNet, indicating a broader vulnerability across different generative architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The core idea of *improving* image quality while simultaneously injecting backdoors is a novel and counter-intuitive approach to attacking generative models. Most prior work focuses on attacks that degrade visual quality or are easily detectable. This makes the attack harder to detect with standard methods.
    *   **Significance:** The potential impact of this research is significant. Generative models are increasingly used in safety-critical and creative applications. Backdoors that are difficult to detect and don't degrade performance can be exploited for malicious purposes, undermining trust in these systems. The demonstration of transferability to diffusion models makes the threat even more widespread and relevant.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The paper provides a solid technical description of the VagueGAN architecture, the PoisonerNet module, and the training procedure. The use of spectral signature analysis and a backdoor success proxy demonstrates an attempt to quantify the effectiveness of the attack and its stealthiness.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The experiments provide evidence supporting the claims of stealthiness, backdoor success, and transferability. The visual examples, while only qualitative, support the argument about improved visual quality of the poisoned outputs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Datasets:** The experiments primarily focus on a single scratch image for training. While this is acceptable for showcasing a minimum-data poisoning scenario, it raises questions about the scalability and generalizability of the attack to larger, more diverse datasets. It's unclear if the backdoor would be as easily implanted with a huge dataset where any single image has less influence.
    *   **Qualitative Evaluation:** The "beauty as stealth" argument relies heavily on the qualitative assessment of image quality. While visually compelling, it lacks a more rigorous, quantitative evaluation of perceptual quality, such as using established image quality metrics (e.g., FID, LPIPS) or human evaluations through user studies.
    *   **Detection Limitations:** The spectral signature analysis's limited success isn't surprising, given the attack's stealthiness. But the paper could benefit from exploring more sophisticated anomaly detection techniques or adaptive defenses that might be more effective at detecting subtle changes in the model's latent space.
    *   **Trigger Simplicity:** The trigger used in the backdoor success proxy (a square patch) is quite simple and easily detectable if one were actively looking for such triggers. The paper mentions that "Realistic attacks may exploit semantic or texture-based triggers that are harder to isolate", but doesn't actually experiment with such triggers.
    *   **Limited analysis of ControlNet’s impact:** The transferability experiment to Stable Diffusion using ControlNet is convincing, however, the specifics of how ControlNet conditions on the Poisoned Generator output in these experiments deserves more examination.

*   **Novelty and Significance Justification:**

The paper demonstrates an attack that improves visual quality while injecting backdoors. The result that stealthy backdoors transfer from GANs to Stable Diffusion has a certain significance and is a new threat vector, however the paper’s novelty and significance are somewhat mitigated by some simple trigger designs and its reliance on single image datasets for training, limiting it from being groundbreaking.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rigorous Rationale:** The score reflects the paper's novelty in the specific attack methodology (backdoors that *improve* quality), its practical implications (transferability to diffusion models), and its sound technical basis. However, the limitations in dataset size, evaluation metrics (especially around perceptual quality), and trigger complexity lower the score. The paper identifies an interesting and concerning vulnerability, but further research is needed to fully assess its scope and potential impact. Therefore, it is above average but not outstanding.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### RealUnify: Do Unified Models Truly Benefit from Unification? A Comprehensive Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24897v1)
- **Authors**: Yang Shi, Yuhao Dong, Yue Ding, Yuran Wang, Xuanyu Zhu, Sheng Zhou, Wenting Liu, Haochen Tian, Rundong Wang, Huanqian Wang, Zuyan Liu, Bohan Zeng, Ruizhe Chen, Qixun Wang, Zhuoran Zhang, Xinlong Chen, Chengzhuo Tong, Bozhou Li, Chaoyou Fu, Qiang Liu, Haotian Wang, Wenjing Yang, Yuanxing Zhang, Pengfei Wan, Yi-Fan Zhang, Ziwei Liu
- **Abstract**: The integration of visual understanding and generation into unified multimodal models represents a significant stride toward general-purpose AI. However, a fundamental question remains unanswered by existing benchmarks: does this architectural unification actually enable synergetic interaction between the constituent capabilities? Existing evaluation paradigms, which primarily assess understanding and generation in isolation, are insufficient for determining whether a unified model can leverage its understanding to enhance its generation, or use generative simulation to facilitate deeper comprehension. To address this critical gap, we introduce RealUnify, a benchmark specifically designed to evaluate bidirectional capability synergy. RealUnify comprises 1,000 meticulously human-annotated instances spanning 10 categories and 32 subtasks. It is structured around two core axes: 1) Understanding Enhances Generation, which requires reasoning (e.g., commonsense, logic) to guide image generation, and 2) Generation Enhances Understanding, which necessitates mental simulation or reconstruction (e.g., of transformed or disordered visual inputs) to solve reasoning tasks. A key contribution is our dual-evaluation protocol, which combines direct end-to-end assessment with a diagnostic stepwise evaluation that decomposes tasks into distinct understanding and generation phases. This protocol allows us to precisely discern whether performance bottlenecks stem from deficiencies in core abilities or from a failure to integrate them. Through large-scale evaluations of 12 leading unified models and 6 specialized baselines, we find that current unified models still struggle to achieve effective synergy, indicating that architectural unification alone is insufficient. These results highlight the need for new training strategies and inductive biases to fully unlock the potential of unified modeling.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "RealUnify: Do Unified Models Truly Benefit from Unification? A Comprehensive Benchmark":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a critical question in the field of multimodal AI: Do unified models, which combine visual understanding and generation, actually achieve synergistic benefits from unification, or are they simply colocated models performing tasks independently? To answer this, the authors introduce RealUnify, a new benchmark designed to evaluate bidirectional capability synergy between understanding and generation. The benchmark consists of 1,000 human-annotated instances spanning 10 categories and 32 subtasks, organized around two core axes: Understanding Enhances Generation (UEG) and Generation Enhances Understanding (GEU). A dual-evaluation protocol is used, combining direct end-to-end assessment with a diagnostic stepwise evaluation to pinpoint performance bottlenecks.  Through extensive evaluations of various unified models and specialized baselines, the paper finds that current unified models struggle to achieve effective synergy, suggesting that architectural unification alone is insufficient. The authors argue for new training strategies and inductive biases to unlock the potential of unified modeling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *purposeful design* of the benchmark itself, *RealUnify*. Existing benchmarks primarily evaluate understanding or generation in isolation, or only superficially integrate both. RealUnify, in contrast, is specifically designed to elicit and measure synergistic interaction, a crucial and previously underexplored aspect of unified models. The dual-evaluation protocol is also a valuable contribution, allowing for more nuanced diagnosis of model performance. The benchmark itself and the question it is trying to address are indeed novel contributions.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a fundamental question about the direction of multimodal AI research. If unification doesn't lead to synergy, then the architectural choices underpinning the current trend might be misguided. The findings have significant implications for how unified models are trained and designed moving forward. The paper highlights the need for more sophisticated training techniques that promote actual integration rather than simply combining existing capabilities. The meticulous design and detailed analysis could pave the way for improved models of visual understanding and generation. The large-scale evaluations of multiple models and baselines make it relevant for current and future reseach.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the research question and its importance.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The benchmark design is thorough, with careful consideration of task categories and evaluation protocols. The human annotation and validation add to the reliability of the results. The comparison with specialized models adds to the validity of the claims.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper evaluates a large number of models using multiple metrics, providing a robust analysis of the current state of unified AI.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, with clear explanations of the methodology and results. The illustrations and tables effectively convey the key findings.
    *   **Focus on diagnostic analysis:** The stepwise evaluation is an excellent addition to the evaluation, which reveals weaknesses that wouldn't be seen with end-to-end evaluation alone.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs for verification:** The reliance on another LLM (Gemini Pro) to judge the success of the generation could add another layer of complexity and potential bias. Though the authors show through comparisons with humans that Gemini is a good choice, it could also be prone to its own errors.
    *   **Limited Model Scope:** While the paper evaluates a good number of models, there's always the question of whether the results generalize to other architectures or training paradigms not explicitly tested.
    *   **Benchmark Scale:** While 1,000 instances is a reasonable starting point, scaling this benchmark further would solidify its position as a leading evaluation resource.
    *   **Open-source data:** While the paper states that it will publicly release the data, the impact of the paper will be increased when the data is actually made public.

*   **Potential Influence:** The RealUnify benchmark has the potential to become a standard evaluation tool for the multimodal AI community. The findings could influence the development of new training techniques and architectural designs, leading to more genuinely unified models. The paper will likely be cited and discussed extensively in the field, shaping the direction of future research.

*   **Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by identifying a crucial gap in the current evaluation of unified multimodal models and then creating a benchmark to measure the extent of genuine synergy. The design and evaluation strategies are sound, and the results provide meaningful insights for future research. However, the reliance on LLMs in image verification, relatively small size of the benchmark, and currently private data are potential drawbacks.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Attention Surgery: An Efficient Recipe to Linearize Your Video Diffusion Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24899v1)
- **Authors**: Mohsen Ghafoorian, Denis Korzhenkov, Amirhossein Habibian
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based video diffusion models (VDMs) deliver state-of-the-art video generation quality but are constrained by the quadratic cost of self-attention, making long sequences and high resolutions computationally expensive. While linear attention offers sub-quadratic complexity, prior attempts fail to match the expressiveness of softmax attention without costly retraining. We introduce \textit{Attention Surgery}, an efficient framework for \textit{linearizing} or \textit{hybridizing} attention in pretrained VDMs without training from scratch. Inspired by recent advances in language models, our method combines a novel hybrid attention mechanism-mixing softmax and linear tokens-with a lightweight distillation and fine-tuning pipeline requiring only a few GPU-days. Additionally, we incorporate a cost-aware block-rate strategy to balance expressiveness and efficiency across layers. Applied to Wan2.1 1.3B, a state-of-the-art DiT-based VDM, Attention Surgery achieves the first competitive sub-quadratic attention video diffusion models, reducing attention cost by up to 40\% in terms of FLOPs, while maintaining generation quality as measured on the standard VBench and VBench-2.0 benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Attention Surgery," a method for making video diffusion transformers (VDMs) more efficient by linearizing or hybridizing their attention mechanisms.  It addresses the computational bottleneck of self-attention (quadratic complexity) which limits the scalability of VDMs to long sequences and high resolutions. The method combines a novel hybrid attention mechanism (mixing softmax and linear attention) with lightweight distillation and fine-tuning.  A cost-aware block-rate strategy balances expressiveness and efficiency across different layers. Applied to a state-of-the-art DiT-based VDM (Wan2.1), Attention Surgery achieves competitive performance while reducing attention cost in terms of FLOPs without sacrificing generation quality on standard benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a critical problem:** The quadratic complexity of self-attention in VDMs is a major obstacle to broader adoption and applicability. The paper directly tackles this bottleneck.
*   **Efficient Distillation:** The method is designed to work with pre-trained models and requires only a few GPU-days to train, significantly reducing the barrier to entry compared to training from scratch. This is a practical and important consideration.
*   **Hybrid Attention Mechanism:** The proposed hybrid approach is a well-reasoned compromise. By selectively applying softmax attention to a small subset of tokens, the model retains global structure and important dependencies while scaling efficiently elsewhere. The token subsampling strategy is also designed specifically for video, attempting to maintain temporal coherence.
*   **Block-Rate Optimization:** The cost-aware block rate selection strategy allows the model to adapt to the varying complexity of different layers, further enhancing efficiency.
*   **Solid Empirical Validation:** The experiments are conducted on a state-of-the-art VDM (Wan2.1) and evaluated on standard benchmarks. User studies are also included, providing a more holistic assessment of the approach. The ablation studies appear thorough, demonstrating the impact of individual components. The comparison with M4V, a concurrent work, is also relevant.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** While the combination is novel, the individual components of the method (linear attention, distillation, hybrid attention) are not entirely new. The paper draws inspiration from existing work, particularly in language models.
*   **Quadratic Complexity Still Present:** The method is not *truly* linear in the sense of avoiding quadratic computation altogether. While it reduces the amount of computation associated with the *most* expensive part of the algorithm, quadratic complexity still *exists*. This can be a problem as the number of softmax tokens become significant. The computational advantage is, in effect, dependent upon the parameter, R, and that the rate is significantly large so as to remove the computational overhead.
*   **Dependency on a Pre-trained Model:** While the low training cost is a strength, it also means that the performance is limited by the quality of the pre-trained model.

**Significance:**

The paper represents a significant step towards making VDMs more efficient and accessible. The practical focus on distillation and the relatively low training cost make it a valuable contribution. While the approach isn't entirely revolutionary, it's a well-engineered solution that addresses a critical problem. Attention Surgery makes current large scale networks more usable. Additionally, it highlights that low cost transfer is possible, something that is commonly assumed is difficult.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7. Here's the rationale:

*   **+2 points:** For addressing a highly relevant and important problem in video generation. Reducing the quadratic complexity of attention is crucial for the field's advancement.
*   **+2 points:** For the practical design of the method. The lightweight distillation and fine-tuning approach make it readily applicable to existing models, lowering the barrier to adoption.
*   **+1 points:** For the combination of components, specifically the novel hybrid attention design and the cost-aware block-rate strategy.
*   **+1 point:** For the thorough empirical evaluation on a state-of-the-art model and standard benchmarks, including user studies.
*   **-1 point:** While the individual components are not groundbreaking, the *combination* is what drives the significance. But, the novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary.
*   **-1 point:** The method does not truly eliminate the quadratic complexity altogether, but rather hides much of the expense. If the sub-sampled rate becomes too significant, quadratic complexity re-emerges as the network scales.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### OpenGPT-4o-Image: A Comprehensive Dataset for Advanced Image Generation and Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24900v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihong Chen, Xuehai Bai, Yang Shi, Chaoyou Fu, Huanyu Zhang, Haotian Wang, Xiaoyan Sun, Zhang Zhang, Liang Wang, Yuanxing Zhang, Pengfei Wan, Yi-Fan Zhang
- **Abstract**: The performance of unified multimodal models for image generation and editing is fundamentally constrained by the quality and comprehensiveness of their training data. While existing datasets have covered basic tasks like style transfer and simple object manipulation, they often lack the systematic structure and challenging scenarios required for real-world applications. To address this bottleneck, we introduce OpenGPT-4o-Image, a large-scale dataset constructed using a novel methodology that combines hierarchical task taxonomy with automated data generation. Our taxonomy not only includes fundamental capabilities such as text rendering and style control but also introduces highly practical yet challenging categories like scientific imagery for chemistry illustrations and complex instruction editing requiring simultaneous execution of multiple operations. Through an automated pipeline leveraging structured resource pools and GPT-4o, we generate 80k high-quality instruction-image pairs with controlled diversity, covering 11 major domains and 51 subtasks. Extensive experiments show that fine-tuning leading models on our dataset achieves significant performance gains across multiple benchmarks, with improvements of up to 18\% on editing tasks (UniWorld-V1 on ImgEdit-Bench) and 13% on generation tasks (Harmon on GenEval). Our work demonstrates that systematic data construction is key to advancing multimodal AI capabilities.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces OpenGPT-40-Image, a large-scale (80k instruction-image pairs) dataset for training and evaluating multimodal image generation and editing models. The dataset is organized hierarchically, covering 11 major domains and 51 subtasks, with a focus on challenging scenarios like scientific imagery, complex instruction editing, and spatial reasoning. The authors demonstrate that fine-tuning existing models (UniWorld-V1, Harmon, OmniGen2, MagicBrush) on OpenGPT-40-Image leads to significant performance gains across multiple benchmarks, improving performance on image editing by up to 18% (on ImgEdit-Bench) and image generation by up to 13% (on GenEval). The authors emphasize systematic data construction as key to advancing multimodal AI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing the need for more comprehensive and structured training data in the multimodal image generation and editing space. While existing datasets often focus on basic tasks, OpenGPT-40-Image introduces more complex and practical scenarios.

**Novelty:** The hierarchical taxonomy and the focus on areas like scientific imagery and complex instruction editing represent a clear step forward. The automated pipeline leveraging GPT-40 for data generation is a practical approach to scale data creation. The authors also present an extensive experimental evaluation showing the effectiveness of the dataset.

**Significance:** The performance gains achieved by fine-tuning models on OpenGPT-40-Image demonstrate the dataset's potential impact. The structured approach to data construction can serve as a template for future dataset creation efforts. The detailed task definitions and hierarchical categorization allows for more targeted evaluations of model capabilities.

**Strengths:**
*   Clear and well-defined hierarchical taxonomy.
*   Focus on complex and practical real-world scenarios.
*   Automated pipeline for scalable data generation.
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with multiple benchmarks and models.
*   Significant performance gains after fine-tuning.

**Weaknesses:**
*   Potential biases introduced by relying on GPT-40 for data generation. The generated images may inherently reflect biases present in the underlying GPT-40 model.
*   Evaluation is primarily conducted on existing benchmarks, which may not fully capture real-world application scenarios. Although comprehensive, these benchmarks still represent curated environments and may not reflect the complexity and diversity of real-world data.
*   Although the dataset has hierarchical labels, it isn't entirely clear from the paper how these hierarchies were exploited during model training. Would a hierarchical learning approach (using these fine-grained categories) have yielded more benefits?

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. While the reliance on GPT-40 and existing benchmarks are valid concerns, the novelty in terms of the taxonomy, automated generation, and demonstrable performance improvements justify a positive assessment. The dataset provides a valuable resource for the research community and has the potential to influence future dataset creation methodologies.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Neural network embeddings recover value dimensions from psychometric survey items on par with human data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24906v1)
- **Authors**: Max Pellert, Clemens M. Lechner, Indira Sen, Markus Strohmaier
- **Abstract**: This study introduces "Survey and Questionnaire Item Embeddings Differentials" (SQuID), a novel methodological approach that enables neural network embeddings to effectively recover latent dimensions from psychometric survey items. We demonstrate that embeddings derived from large language models, when processed with SQuID, can recover the structure of human values obtained from human rater judgments on the Revised Portrait Value Questionnaire (PVQ-RR). Our experimental validation compares multiple embedding models across a number of evaluation metrics. Unlike previous approaches, SQuID successfully addresses the challenge of obtaining negative correlations between dimensions without requiring domain-specific fine-tuning. Quantitative analysis reveals that our embedding-based approach explains 55% of variance in dimension-dimension similarities compared to human data. Multidimensional scaling configurations from both types of data show fair factor congruence coefficients and largely follow the underlying theory. These results demonstrate that semantic embeddings can effectively replicate psychometric structures previously established through extensive human surveys. The approach offers substantial advantages in cost, scalability and flexibility while maintaining comparable quality to traditional methods. Our findings have significant implications for psychometrics and social science research, providing a complementary methodology that could expand the scope of human behavior and experience represented in measurement tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel methodological approach called "Survey and Questionnaire Item Embeddings Differentials" (SQuID) to improve the ability of neural network embeddings derived from large language models (LLMs) to recover latent dimensions from psychometric survey items.  The authors demonstrate that applying SQuID to embeddings can effectively replicate the structure of human values obtained from human rater judgments on the Revised Portrait Value Questionnaire (PVQ-RR). They compare different embedding models using several evaluation metrics, showing that SQuID addresses the issue of missing negative correlations between dimensions without requiring domain-specific fine-tuning.  The results suggest that the embedding-based approach explains a significant portion of variance in dimension-dimension similarities compared to human data.  The authors argue that this approach offers advantages in cost, scalability, and flexibility while maintaining comparable quality to traditional methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the SQuID method itself. The idea of subtracting a "questionnaire embedding" from individual item embeddings to remove common linguistic features that artificially inflate similarity is clever and demonstrably effective. This addresses a significant limitation of previous attempts to use embeddings for psychometric analysis. It is also a more general method than re-annotating training corpora or dropping the sign of the correlation.

*   **Significance:** The significance comes from the potential to reduce the reliance on costly and time-consuming human rater judgments in psychometric research. Successfully replicating human-derived structures with LLMs opens doors to rapid questionnaire development, cross-cultural analysis, and exploration of constructs across different languages, all at a much lower cost. Furthermore, it offers a way to identify potential cultural biases encoded in LLMs.  The ability to effectively capture negative correlations is also significant, as it enables the replication of complex theoretical relationships between constructs (e.g., the circumplex model of values).

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The SQuID method is simple, general, and effective.
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured comparison to existing methods.
    *   The use of a well-established and validated questionnaire (PVQ-RR) strengthens the validity of the findings.
    *   The paper provides a good overview of the related work and clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   The authors acknowledge limitations of their work, and provide a transparent account of how they came to their findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the PVQ-RR is a good choice, further validation on other types of questionnaires (e.g., personality inventories with more complex item structures) would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The authors mention but don't delve deeply into measurement validity and the potential for bias in LLMs, which warrants further investigation.
    *   The use of a small sample (57 questionnaire items), although standard, may affect the reliability of some statistical tests.
    *   Although some models are open-source, the results would be stronger if the model training regimes and datasets were more available for inspection and scrutiny.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence psychometric research by providing a practical and effective method for using LLMs to augment or even partially replace human rater judgments.  It could lead to more rapid development of questionnaires, easier cross-cultural comparisons, and a deeper understanding of the semantic structure of psychological constructs.

*   **Score:** 7.5

*   **Justification:**  The paper presents a genuinely novel and impactful method (SQuID) that addresses a significant challenge in applying LLMs to psychometric research. The empirical results are convincing and support the authors' claims. While further validation is needed, the potential benefits of this approach are substantial. The clarity of exposition and thorough discussion of related work further enhance the value of the paper. The score is less than a 8.0 or 9.0 due to the limited range of questionnaires and model architectures explored, as well as the relatively shallow discussion of measurement validity issues. The relatively high score acknowledges the strong value of the SQUID method which addresses an important long-standing problem.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### BOE-XSUM: Extreme Summarization in Clear Language of Spanish Legal Decrees and Notifications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24908v1)
- **Authors**: Andrés Fernández García, Javier de la Rosa, Julio Gonzalo, Roser Morante, Enrique Amigó, Alejandro Benito-Santos, Jorge Carrillo-de-Albornoz, Víctor Fresno, Adrian Ghajari, Guillermo Marco, Laura Plaza, Eva Sánchez Salido
- **Abstract**: The ability to summarize long documents succinctly is increasingly important in daily life due to information overload, yet there is a notable lack of such summaries for Spanish documents in general, and in the legal domain in particular. In this work, we present BOE-XSUM, a curated dataset comprising 3,648 concise, plain-language summaries of documents sourced from Spain's ``Bolet\'{\i}n Oficial del Estado'' (BOE), the State Official Gazette. Each entry in the dataset includes a short summary, the original text, and its document type label. We evaluate the performance of medium-sized large language models (LLMs) fine-tuned on BOE-XSUM, comparing them to general-purpose generative models in a zero-shot setting. Results show that fine-tuned models significantly outperform their non-specialized counterparts. Notably, the best-performing model -- BERTIN GPT-J 6B (32-bit precision) -- achieves a 24\% performance gain over the top zero-shot model, DeepSeek-R1 (accuracies of 41.6\% vs.\ 33.5\%).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**
